# Middleware readiness audit: full record

Appendix to [../middleware-readiness.md](../middleware-readiness.md). Raw output of the audit run (2026-11-09): seven dimension audits with their complete inventories, every claim with the two skeptics' verdicts and reasoning, the three advocate cases, and the judge's scoring. Status labels: *confirmed* = neither skeptic refuted; *contested* = one did (often a severity or scope correction, read the corrected claim); *refuted* = both did; *unverified* = the checks did not run. File:line references are as the auditors recorded them.

## Programmatic coverage (capability matrix: /api/v1 vs server-action-only vs cron vs internal)

### Summary

The Hub has two clearly different halves. The Vetrina (owner app) half is already built as middleware: 21 action kinds in src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts (13 catalog + 8 homepage) are all reachable through POST /api/v1/proposals -> confirm with JSON Schema self-description, an operations log with `via`, and undo; 21 of its 34 "use server" exports are thin wrappers over the same service functions the API calls, so the API and UI share one path (src/server/api/*.ts imports src/server/vetrina/operations/service.ts directly). The admin half (the 10 tabs under src/app/(admin): sync, preview, publish, pricing, taxonomies, collections, feeds, import, orders, duplicates) is the opposite: 73 of its server-action exports across 19 files have zero /api/v1 counterpart; only 3 underlying functions (full pull, GoldenSneakers sync, KicksDB refresh) are reachable headlessly via /api/cron/* with CRON_SECRET, and runPublish via the session-bound, same-origin /api/publish. Overall: 107 server-action exports, ~21 with an API equivalent (all Vetrina), ~4 with a cron/plain-route analogue, ~82 UI-only. The big middleware capabilities an LLM would need and cannot reach today are store pull (start/advance/cancel), sync preview/plan and apply, publish, SKU import, feeds upload, pricing rules/margins config, taxonomy rules, smart collections CRUD, duplicates trash, orders workflow, overrides/locks, store variation edits, repair/rebuild, and most catalog reads (overview, term detail, placement). Most admin actions are thin parse-and-call wrappers (cheap to expose), but five files carry real domain logic inside the "use server" file: preview.ts (~600 lines of sync planning), config.ts (~350 lines of pricing-rule editing), feeds.ts (runKicksdbRefresh, which the cron route imports from the actions file), debug.ts, export.ts, plus store-edit.ts and duplicates.ts which call the Woo client inline. On the WordPress side the Hub talks only to wc/v3 (Woo client) and the plugin's wc-gh/v1 homepage routes; there is no wp/v2 usage at all, so menus, non-homepage pages, posts and the media library are not Hub capabilities in any class. API tests exist only in e2e/api.spec.ts (5 tests, exercising 2 of 21 kinds plus order, undo, proposals and 3 reads); src/server/api/*.ts has no unit tests.

## 2. Vetrina action registry (src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41-220) — 21 kinds, all class A via proposals

Catalog (13): hide, show, sale, unsale, tag, untag, categorize, uncategorize, pin, margin, order, sourceOrder, delete.
Homepage (8): sectionSwitch, sectionMove, sectionsArrange, sectionAdd, sectionRemove, sectionSource, sectionEdit, blockEdit.
describeActions() (248-266) emits JSON Schema via z.toJSONSchema from the same Zod unions the server validates with; parseAction (269-273) routes page vs catalog kinds. `order` and `sourceOrder` additionally have direct POST endpoints with dryRun (no proposal step).

## 4. Scheduler (class C, in-process; src/server/scheduler.ts:20-70, 97)
DAILY_STEPS = pull, gs, kicksdb, storeSync, collections, repair, backfill, recategorize; feed cycle every N minutes; orders pull every 15 min; media drain; collections check every 5 min. Of these, only pull, kicksdb refresh, GS sync and media have /api/cron routes; storeSync (apply), collections, repair, backfill, recategorize, orders pull have no HTTP trigger.

## 5. Totals
- Server-action exports: 107. With /api/v1 equivalent: ~21 (all Vetrina). With cron/plain-route analogue only: 4 (startStorePull~runFullPull, runGsSyncFromApi, runKicksdbRefresh, runPublish via /api/publish). UI-only: ~82 (73 admin + 9 Vetrina reads/helpers).
- Action kinds in registry: 21/21 API-exposed.
- UI call sites importing actions (src/app + src/components): 80 import statements across 24 modules (vetrina-operations 8, vetrina 8, vetrina-catalog 6, collections 6, overrides 5, publish 4, ...).

## 6. WordPress vs WooCommerce reach
- Woo client (src/server/woo/client.ts): wc/v3 only — products page/full/get/create/update/delete, variations get/batch, orders page (read), categories/brands/tags/attributes/attribute-terms list+create, default catalog orderby.
- WP plugin (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:131-208): wc-gh/v1 routes capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block (POST), homepage/layout (POST), homepage/history. Homepage page only.
- grep for wp/v2, nav_menu, menu-items, menus in src/server: no matches except unrelated. Media is attached through the wc/v3 product `images` field (woo/media.ts:82), not wp/v2/media. => navigation menus, arbitrary pages/posts, media library: not Hub capabilities in any class.

## 7. Tests of the programmatic layer
- e2e/api.spec.ts (149 lines, 5 tests, lines 30,41,74,99,123): GET /actions; POST /catalog/order + GET /operations/{id} + undo; POST /proposals (sectionSwitch) + confirm twice + undo; stale proposal 409 (sectionMove); reads GET /homepage, /catalog/products?q=, /operations, /operations?limit=0 -> 400. Action kinds exercised: 2 of 21 via proposals (sectionSwitch, sectionMove) + `order` via direct route.
- Not covered: catalog/terms, order-sources (all 5 endpoints), DELETE, Accept-Language, any of the 13 catalog kinds through proposals, undo of page actions after a conflicting change.
- src/server/api/*.ts: 373 lines, no unit tests (only src/proxy.test.ts matched a grep for api/v1 among *.test.ts). Registry/proposals/operations do have db tests in src/server/vetrina/operations/*.test.ts.

### Claims and verdicts

#### PC-1 [confirmed · strength] All 21 Vetrina action kinds are reachable through /api/v1 with JSON Schema self-description, proposal/confirm, log and undo, and the API calls the same service functions the UI's server actions call.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41-220 (21 kinds), :248-266 describeActions with z.toJSONSchema; src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22 propose(action,"api"); src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:166-168 startPageOperation/startOperation; src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14; src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:52-86 wrap the same operations/service.ts exports; src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:75-224 all writes call startPageOperation.
- **Implication**: The Vetrina half is already structured as middleware; an MCP layer can be one thin adapter per kind over propose/confirm/undo (as docs/operations.md:84-106 sketches). Strong argument for keep/grow rather than fresh for this half.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location and looked for counterexamples. (1) 21 kinds: src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41-220 defines ACTIONS keyed by ActionKind with 13 catalog kinds (hide, show, sale, unsale, tag, untag, categorize, uncategorize, pin, margin, order, sourceOrder, delete) and 8 homepage kinds (sectionSwitch, sectionMove, sectionsArrange, sectionAdd, sectionRemove, sectionSource, sectionEdit, blockEdit) = 21; the `{ [K in ActionKind]: ActionSpec<K> }` type forces exhaustiveness. (2) JSON Schema self-description: registry.ts:248-266 describeActions() builds `z.toJSONSchema(schema)` per kind from the same OperationInputSchema/PageActionInputSchema unions the server validates with (schemasByKind, :224-231), served by GET /api/v1/actions (src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12-14). (3) Proposal/confirm through /api/v1: proposals/route.ts:22 calls propose(action,"api"); proposals.ts:105-110 propose() uses parseAction (any of the 21 kinds) + previewOf; proposals/[id]/confirm/route.ts:15 calls confirmProposal, which at proposals.ts:164-168 dispatches isPageActionKind → startPageOperation else startOperation — the same service.ts exports (service.ts:79, :112). (4) Log and undo: operations/route.ts, operations/[id]/route.ts exist; undo/route.ts:14 calls service.undoOperation; service.ts:142-176 handles product kinds and page kinds via undoPageAction, whose switch at page.ts:525-577 covers all 8 homepage kinds; plan.ts:406 undoable() covers product kinds (tests at plan.test.ts:325-335, page.db.test.ts:58-64, proposals.db.test.ts:76 confirm via:"api"/"assistant"). (5) Same service functions from UI: src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:52-86 wraps operations.previewOperation/startOperation/loadOperation/listOperations/undoOperation from operations/service.ts; src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:88-262 — publishVetrinaRail, publishVetrinaBlock, changeVetrinaSource, addVetrinaSection, removeVetrinaSection, arrangeVetrinaSections, moveVetrinaSection, switchVetrinaSection — each calls startPageOperation with the matching kind. grep for startOperation/startPageOperation across src/app and src/server/actions finds only these two files, no bypass. OPERATION_VIAS (catalog-ops.ts:247) includes "api" and "assistant", and docs/operations.md:84-106 sketches the MCP adapter exactly as the implication says. Caveats that do not refute the claim as worded: (a) the UI's server actions call startOperation/startPageOperation directly (no proposal/digest step), so the proposal layer is API-only, though the underlying service is shared; (b) homepage operations can return a null operation when the log could not file them (service.ts:110, page.ts:464), so "logged" is best-effort for the 8 page kinds; (c) some Vetrina writes exist outside the 21 kinds — per-SKU price publishing (src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts:48 publishProductPrices → runPriceSync, not an operation, not in /api/v1) and order-source CRUD (vetrina-order-sources.ts:77,97; /api/v1/order-sources exists for those) — relevant to "the Vetrina half is already structured as middleware" but outside the literal claim about the 21 kinds.
  - Corrected claim: All 21 Vetrina action kinds (13 catalog, 8 homepage) are reachable through /api/v1 with JSON Schema self-description (GET /api/v1/actions), proposal/confirm, log and undo, and the API's confirm path calls the same operations/service.ts startOperation/startPageOperation/undoOperation functions the UI's server actions call. Two nuances: the UI calls startOperation directly (the proposal/digest step is API-only), and a homepage action's log entry can be null if filing fails; per-SKU price publishing (vetrina-prices.ts) is a Vetrina write that is not an action kind and has no /api/v1 route.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked the evidence and the code around it.

Factual part confirmed: src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41-220 holds the ACTIONS map (catalog + homepage kinds), :246-266 describeActions() emits z.toJSONSchema per kind and :269-274 parseAction dispatches catalog vs page kinds. src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22 calls propose(action, "api"); src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:166-168 confirmProposal routes to startPageOperation/startOperation — the same service.ts exports that src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:74-116 and src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:92-260 wrap for the UI (every homepage write there is a startPageOperation({kind: ...}) call). Undo: src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14 -> operations/service.ts undoOperation, same as the server action at vetrina-operations.ts:108-116. Auth/language is centralized in src/server/api/v1.ts:57-72. docs/operations.md:84-106 does sketch the MCP layer as one tool per kind over propose/confirmProposal/undoOperation, and the file comments (registry.ts:9-18, runner.ts:11-22) show this was designed for it. The service layer has substantial db tests (operations.db.test.ts, proposals.db.test.ts, page.db.test.ts, registry.test.ts).

Attempted refutations of the implication, and why they do not overturn it:
1. Scope gap: not every Vetrina write is one of the 21 kinds. src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts:48-80 publishProductPrices (used by src/components/vetrina/ProductSheet.tsx) calls runPriceSync directly — no operation row, no proposal, no undo, no /api/v1 route. hideTermNewcomers (vetrina-operations.ts:135) is server-action-only, though its effect is also reached by the queued sweep after moves (runner.ts:143,180). So "the Vetrina half" is ~95% covered, not 100%; the auditor's claim is scoped to the 21 kinds, which is exact.
2. Runtime coupling: the runner lives on globalThis in the Next process (runner.ts:19-22, 42); a restart drops the queue (marked interrupted, never resumed). An MCP layer is "thin" only if co-located (as docs/operations.md:86 says) or if it goes over /api/v1 HTTP — both work, so the adapter conclusion stands, but a fresh out-of-process repo would have to re-host or proxy the runner.
3. Auth model: one static bearer token, `via` is a fixed string (v1.ts:57-61, proposals route :22); proposer and confirmer are the same caller, nothing distinguishes actors. A real concern for LLM exposure, but orthogonal to the structural claim, and the user asked not to focus on the LLM role.
4. No HTTP-level tests for /api/v1 routes (only src/proxy.test.ts mentions the path), but the service layer under them is tested.

None of these is a mitigation the auditor missed that flips the implication; they are caveats. A competent engineer would reach the same keep/grow conclusion for this half. Severity "strength" is right.
  - Corrected claim: All 21 Vetrina action kinds are reachable through /api/v1 with JSON Schema self-description (registry.ts describeActions), proposal/confirm (proposals route -> confirmProposal -> startOperation/startPageOperation), log and undo, and the API calls the same operations/service.ts functions the UI's server actions wrap. Caveats that do not change the conclusion: two Vetrina writes sit outside this layer (publishProductPrices in src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts runs runPriceSync directly with no log/undo/API; hideTermNewcomers is server-action-only), the background runner is an in-process globalThis queue so an MCP layer must be co-located or go over /api/v1, and the API has a single static bearer token with no actor identity. The Vetrina half is structured as middleware and argues for keep/grow.

#### PC-2 [contested · blocker] None of the 73 admin-tab server-action exports (19 files: sync, preview, publish, config, taxonomy, collections, feeds, import, orders, overrides, store-edits, store-edit, duplicates, catalog, repair, export, store, health, debug) has a /api/v1 counterpart; only 3 underlying functions are reachable headlessly via /api/cron and runPublish via a session-bound same-origin route.

- **Evidence**: src/app/api/v1/** imports only src/server/api/*, vetrina/operations/*, vetrina/order-sources/* (all 14 route files read); src/app/api/cron/pull-store/route.ts:3 runFullPull, refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 runKicksdbRefresh, sync-goldensneakers/route.ts:3, media/route.ts:3; src/app/api/publish/route.ts:16-38 same-origin check, no bearer token; export counts per file from grep '^export async function' (107 total, 34 Vetrina).
- **Implication**: The capabilities an LLM-driven middleware would need most — store pull start/advance/cancel, sync preview/plan + apply, publish, SKU import, feed upload, pricing rules, taxonomy rules, smart collections CRUD, duplicates, orders workflow, overrides/locks, variation edits, repair/rebuild — are UI-only today. Exposing them is the bulk of the remaining work whichever option is chosen.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked: (1) Export counts — `grep -c '^export async function'` over the 19 named files in /home/user/woo-hub/src/server/actions/ sums to exactly 73 (sync 9, preview 5, publish 5, config 4, taxonomy 4, collections 13, feeds 5, import 2, orders 5, overrides 5, store-edits 3, store-edit 1, duplicates 1, catalog 3, repair 2, export 1, store 1, health 1, debug 3); the 5 vetrina-*.ts files add 34 (5+9+6+2+12) = 107 total. Matches. (2) /api/v1 imports — all 14 route files under src/app/api/v1/** import only `@/server/api/v1`, `@/server/api/reads`, `@/server/api/catalog-order`, `@/server/api/order-sources`, `@/server/vetrina/source`, `@/server/vetrina/operations/{service,proposals,registry}`, `@/server/vetrina/order-sources/service`, zod. src/server/api/*.ts in turn import only vetrina/*, collections/{store,repo}, lib/*. No import of `@/server/actions/*` anywhere under src/app/api/v1 or src/server/api. So "none of the 73 has a /api/v1 counterpart" is literally true. (3) Headless reachability — `grep -rn 'from "@/server/actions/'` outside components/pages shows only: src/app/api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 (runKicksdbRefresh, feeds.ts:168), src/app/api/publish/route.ts:2 (runPublish, publish.ts:131), src/server/scheduler.ts:14, src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 (internal). Publish route (src/app/api/publish/route.ts:16-38) does only an Origin==Host check, no bearer token — confirmed session-bound/same-origin. (4) Cron — there are 4 cron routes, not 3 functions: pull-store/route.ts:3 runFullPull (server/woo/pull), refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 runKicksdbRefresh (which IS one of the 73 admin exports), sync-goldensneakers/route.ts:3 syncGoldenSneakersFromApi (the same function the admin export runGsSyncFromApi at feeds.ts:93-96 dynamically imports), media/route.ts:3 drainMedia. All four gate on `Authorization: Bearer CRON_SECRET`. So the sub-claim "only 3 underlying functions via /api/cron" is off by one (4), and strictly two of the 73 admin exports (runKicksdbRefresh directly; runGsSyncFromApi by shared underlying function) are headlessly reachable via /api/cron with a bearer token — a trivial correction that does not change the conclusion. Everything in the implication list (store pull start/advance/cancel in sync.ts:41-80, startStoreSync/advanceStoreSync in preview.ts:818-895, publish, import, taxonomy, collections, duplicates, orders, overrides, store-edit(s), repair) has no bearer-authenticated or /api/v1 route. docs/api.md confirms /api/v1 covers only homepage/catalog reads, catalog order, order-sources, proposals, operations, actions registry. Claim stands with the minor numeric correction.
  - Corrected claim: None of the 73 admin-tab server-action exports (19 files) has a /api/v1 counterpart; /api/v1 (14 route files) reaches only src/server/api/* and src/server/vetrina/* (homepage/catalog reads, catalog order, order-sources, proposals, operations, actions registry). Outside /api/v1, 4 underlying functions are reachable headlessly with the CRON_SECRET bearer token via /api/cron (runFullPull, runKicksdbRefresh, syncGoldenSneakersFromApi, drainMedia) — of which runKicksdbRefresh is itself one of the 73 admin exports and syncGoldenSneakersFromApi is the function behind the admin export runGsSyncFromApi — and runPublish is reachable only via /api/publish, a same-origin-checked, session-bound route with no token auth. All other admin capabilities (store pull start/advance/cancel, sync preview/plan + apply, SKU import, feed upload, pricing/taxonomy rules, smart collections CRUD, duplicates, orders workflow, overrides/locks, variation edits, repair/rebuild) are UI-only today.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Accepting the facts (no /api/v1 route for the 73 admin-tab server actions), the implication that exposing them is "the bulk of the remaining work whichever option is chosen" and the "blocker" severity do not follow from the code.

1. The server actions are explicitly thin shims over a Next-free core. src/server/actions/collections.ts:14-17 ("thin, validated wrappers over server/collections"), src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:26-33 (same), src/server/actions/sync.ts:41-85 (startStorePull/advanceStorePull/cancelStorePull are zod-parse + call into @/server/woo/pull), src/server/actions/import.ts:14-18 ("Both frontends call the same pipeline the previews use — growCatalogFromSkus"), src/server/actions/store.ts:14-25, src/server/actions/orders.ts:4-15. The only file under src/server that imports from "next/*" besides the actions is src/server/api/v1.ts (grep). So the domain logic, validation schemas and result shapes an API would need already exist and are not bound to the UI; "UI-only" describes the transport, not the capability.

2. The repo already contains the exact LLM-exposure framework the auditor says must be built: src/server/api/v1.ts:57-70 `serve()` with Bearer HUB_API_TOKEN auth and uniform {ok,code,error} JSON; src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:10-18 an action registry with JSON Schema derived from the same zod schemas the server checks, descriptions written "in words a model reads"; src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts (GET /actions "described for a script or an assistant"); proposals preview→confirm with 409 stale re-check (docs/api.md:189-235); operations log + undo (src/app/api/v1/operations/*); a background runner that outlives the request (src/server/vetrina/operations/runner.ts:11-18). docs/api.md:181-187 literally says "An assistant's tools are these." The Vetrina UI and the API already share one service layer (src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts vs src/app/api/v1/*), proving the pattern. Extending it to the admin tabs is registration/route plumbing on a template, not greenfield design.

3. Long-running admin flows already have headless drivers. src/server/sync/apply-job.ts:5-18 runs the store apply in-process with polling; pull runs are persisted and resumable by runId (sync.ts:41-70); src/server/scheduler.ts:20-67 and :378-457 runs store pull, GS sync, automatic categories, repair, metadata backfill and recategorize headlessly with no browser loop. The "client then loops advanceStorePull" shape is a UI choice, and the server-side loop already exists in the scheduler.

4. "Whichever option is chosen" equalizes two very different amounts of work. Keeping this repo: add route files / action kinds over existing services (4356 lines of thin actions, src/server core untouched). Fresh repo: rebuild the core plus the framework plus the routes. The fact pattern therefore argues for keeping the codebase, which the stated implication obscures.

Correct severity: a real, scoped gap (major), not a blocker — the capability matrix shows where routes are missing, with an in-repo template for filling them.
  - Corrected claim: None of the 73 admin-tab server-action exports has a /api/v1 counterpart; only a few functions run headlessly via /api/cron, the in-app scheduler and a session-bound /api/publish route. However, these actions are thin zod-validated wrappers over a Next-independent core (src/server/*), and the repo already ships the LLM-facing framework the admin capabilities need — bearer-token `serve()` (src/server/api/v1.ts), an action registry with JSON Schema and model-readable descriptions (src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts, GET /api/v1/actions), proposals with preview/confirm/stale checks, an operations log with undo, and in-process background runners (apply-job.ts, operations/runner.ts, scheduler.ts). Exposing the admin capabilities is therefore route/registry plumbing on an existing template rather than the bulk of the work, and it is substantially cheaper in this codebase than in a fresh one. Severity: major (scoped gap), not blocker.

#### PC-3 [refuted · strength] Most admin server actions are thin parse-and-call wrappers over plain src/server functions, so they are cheap to expose; the Zod schema and the service call are already separated.

- **Evidence**: src/server/actions/collections.ts:51-145 (13 exports, each safeParse + service.X); overrides.ts:26-119 (updateOverrides + supersede); orders.ts:39-112 (pullRecentOrders, setWorkflow*); taxonomy.ts:48-80 (updateActiveConfig); publish.ts:113-125 (publishProducts); sync.ts:40-84,173-180,226-233 (startPull/advancePull/cancelPull, rebuildProducts, applySync); repair.ts:21-60; store-edits.ts:19-55; import.ts:35-90.
- **Implication**: For roughly 14 of 19 admin files, a route can reuse the same Zod schema and call the same service function — the pattern src/server/api/order-sources.ts already uses. This favors growing the programmatic layer in place over re-implementing.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): What holds: the cited files are mostly thin. src/server/actions/collections.ts:59-161 has exactly 13 exports, each `attempt(() => service.X(...))` with optional safeParse (8 of 13 take input); overrides.ts:33-143 (5 exports, safeParse + updateOverrides(with* model fn)); orders.ts:46-124 (pullRecentOrders, setWorkflowStatus/Tracking/Note after an orderExists check); taxonomy.ts:69-104 (safeParse + updateActiveConfig at :89/:99 -- not :48-80 as cited); publish.ts:131-148 (runPublish -> publishProducts at :139, not :113-125); sync.ts startStorePull/advanceStorePull/cancelStorePull :40-84, rebuildStoreProducts :194-206, applySyncPrices :262-273; repair.ts:62-79; store-edits.ts:36-62; import.ts:52-104. The 19-file count matches the non-vetrina, non-test files in src/server/actions.

Where the claim is overstated, non-trivially:
1. "The Zod schema and the service call are already separated / a route can reuse the same Zod schema": every schema in these files is a module-private `const` (grep `^export const` over src/server/actions/*.ts returns nothing). They are "use server" modules (Next 16.2.9, package.json), which may only export async functions, so the schemas cannot be exported from where they live; a route cannot reuse them without first moving them out. The existing API pattern does not reuse action schemas either: src/server/api/catalog-order.ts and order-sources.ts (:51 `export const ApplyBody`) define their own schemas and call src/server/vetrina/* services directly; src/app/api/publish/route.ts:2,41 instead imports and calls the server action `runPublish` as a function.
2. Several admin files are not thin wrappers -- the action module IS the service: preview.ts (898 lines) holds the core preview/plan pipeline inside the action file (planChunk :152-315, previewStoreChunk :502-625, fetchAndPreview :342-493, startStoreSync :818, advanceStoreSync :841 with cursor/commit logic), and server code depends on it in reverse: src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 imports startStoreSync/advanceStoreSync and src/server/scheduler.ts:14 imports runKicksdbRefresh from actions. config.ts owns the pricing mutation logic itself (updatePricing :50-99 mutates the rule inside the updateActiveConfig callback; savePricingRules :346-394 maps rules, version/demo checks; cleanScope, ruleExamples, pricingRuleCoverage :149-345). export.ts:50-102 builds the patch map in a loop. store-edit.ts:29-69 and duplicates.ts:22-47 build the Woo payload and call getWooClient() directly. debug.ts :35-311 (3 exports, no schema, logic inline). import.ts:52-104, feeds.ts:168-222 and sync.ts listRebuildableSkus :221-256 carry ingestion-run bookkeeping / set logic in the action.

Tally over the 19 admin files: clearly thin ~10 (collections, overrides, orders, taxonomy, publish, repair, store-edits, store, catalog, health), thin-with-inline-logic 3 (sync, import, feeds), not thin 6 (preview, config, export, store-edit, duplicates, debug). "Most" survives narrowly, "14 of 19" is generous, but the exceptions are the heaviest core workflows (preview/sync planning, pricing config), and the "reuse the same Zod schema" step requires a schema-extraction pass first. The implication (grow in place) still stands, but the cost is a refactor, not just adding routes.
  - Corrected claim: About two-thirds of the admin server actions (collections, overrides, orders, taxonomy, publish, repair, store-edits, store, catalog, health; sync/import/feeds with some inline bookkeeping) are thin safeParse-then-service wrappers, so they are cheap to expose -- but their Zod schemas are private consts inside "use server" modules (which can only export async functions), so a route cannot reuse them until they are moved into a shared module, as src/server/api/* already does with its own schemas. Six admin files are not thin: preview.ts (898 lines) holds the core preview/sync-planning pipeline itself and is imported by server/sync/price-sync.ts and server/scheduler.ts; config.ts owns the pricing-rule mutation logic; export.ts, store-edit.ts, duplicates.ts and debug.ts do their work (including direct Woo client writes) inside the action. Growing the programmatic layer in place is still the better path, but it requires extracting schemas and moving the preview/config/store-edit logic into src/server services first, not merely adding routes.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): The factual half holds: the cited admin actions are safeParse + one service call (collections.ts:59-160 via `attempt`, publish.ts:112-147, sync.ts:41-90/216-233/285-310, import.ts:52-104, etc.). The implication's mechanism and its "cheap" framing do not hold, for four reasons found in the code.

1. "A route can reuse the same Zod schema" is literally impossible as the code stands. Every schema is a module-private const inside a `"use server"` file (collections.ts:37-57 DraftSchema/Id; sync.ts:62, 78, 163-199, 312 AdvanceSchema/CancelSchema/ApplySchema/JobSchema; publish.ts:112 PublishSchema; import.ts:28-34 ImportSchema). Next.js forbids exporting anything but async functions from a `"use server"` module, so the schemas cannot be exported from where they live; the grep of `export (const|let)` over src/server/actions/*.ts returns none. The schema is NOT "already separated" from the action — it is fused into the action module and must be moved to a shared module first. Small, but it is the opposite of what the auditor states.

2. src/server/api/order-sources.ts is not an instance of "route reuses the action's schema and calls the service". It defines its own ApplyBody (order-sources.ts:50-53), funnels through parseOperationInput (vetrina/operations/input.ts:177) and operations.startOperation(..., "api") — the Vetrina operations log/proposals/undo machinery — and the corresponding action file vetrina-order-sources.ts defines separate schemas (lines 46, 70, 86-89). The existing /api/v1 is a distinct adapter architecture (docs/api.md: proposals → confirm with staleness check, operations log, undo), not a thin re-export of actions.

3. Error semantics live in the action, not the service, so "call the same service" is not enough. `serve` in src/server/api/v1.ts:62-73 maps only VetrinaError and ZodError to codes/status; CollectionError (collections/service.ts:37-45, codes not_configured/invalid/not_found/store) and every error thrown by woo/apply, woo/publish, woo/rebuild, catalog import would fall to the generic 500 "failed". Each action's catch (collections.ts:24-33 `attempt`; sync.ts:28-31 `errMessage` flattening to a string) is the only place codes/messages are shaped. A route per action must re-do that mapping per domain, or the services must be changed to throw VetrinaError — neither is "reuse".

4. The expensive part for LLM exposure is orchestration, which sits in client components, not in the action or the service. SyncWorkspace.tsx:186-212 drives the pull by looping advanceStorePull up to MAX_PULL_STEPS; RebuildPanel.tsx:92-96 chunks SKUs and threads auditId across calls; ImportWorkspace.tsx:89-92 chunks and threads runId; publish is capped at 200 SKUs per call (publish.ts:114); the live apply runs as an in-process globalThis Map job (sync/apply-job.ts:37-39, lost on restart, one at a time, polled by getSyncApplyJob). Exposing the actions 1:1 as routes hands an LLM run-id/chunk/poll loops that the UI exists to hide. A partial mitigation the auditor missed: server-side runFullPull (woo/pull.ts:249-255) already exists for cron, so the pull has a single-call server-side shape; nothing equivalent exists for rebuild, import or publish.

Net: thinness of the wrappers is real and does favor growing in place over a rewrite, but the "cheap to expose / just add routes" implication is wrong. The real cost is (a) hoisting schemas out of "use server" modules, (b) unifying the error model so `serve` can map admin-service failures, (c) building server-side, single-call (or job-based) orchestration for the chunked workflows, and (d) deciding whether admin actions should pass through the proposals/log/undo layer the Vetrina API uses. Severity should drop from a clean "strength" to a qualified one. A competent engineer would conclude the same direction (in place) but not from the stated reason.
  - Corrected claim: Most admin server actions are thin parse-and-call wrappers over src/server services, so a programmatic layer can reuse the services — but not the Zod schemas as they stand (module-private consts inside "use server" files that Next.js cannot export), nor the error handling (codes are shaped in each action's catch; /api/v1's `serve` maps only VetrinaError/ZodError, so admin-service failures would surface as 500 "failed"), nor the workflow orchestration (pull/import/rebuild/publish are chunked, run-id/audit-id-threaded loops driven from client components, and the live apply is an in-process job). The existing /api/v1 (order-sources.ts) is a separate proposals/log/undo adapter, not an action-reuse pattern. This still favors growing the programmatic layer in place over a rewrite, but the work is hoisting schemas, unifying the error model, and adding server-side orchestration — not adding routes over the existing actions.

#### PC-4 [refuted · major] Five action files hold real domain logic inside the "use server" module rather than in src/server services: preview.ts (~600 lines of sync planning), config.ts (~350 lines of pricing-rule editing), feeds.ts runKicksdbRefresh (which the cron route imports from the actions file), debug.ts, export.ts; store-edit.ts and duplicates.ts call the Woo client inline.

- **Evidence**: src/server/actions/preview.ts:109-237 planChunk, :256-380 fetchAndPreview, :385-478 previewStoreChunk, :494-588 previewFromStore (file is 898 lines); config.ts:41-125 updatePricing, :126-221 helpers, :262-394 savePricingRules; feeds.ts:152-222 and src/app/api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 importing from @/server/actions/feeds; store-edit.ts:30-55 batchVariations + patchSnapshotProduct; duplicates.ts:23-33 deleteProduct; export.ts:43-100.
- **Implication**: Sync planning and pricing-rule editing — the two capabilities most central to 'middleware between customer needs and the Woo store' — must be moved out of Next server-action files before they can be exposed to any non-UI caller. This is a refactor, not a wrapper; it is the same cost under keep or extract, and is what a fresh repo would have to re-derive.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Directionally right, but overstated in three ways that matter for the implication, and the cited line numbers are wrong throughout.

CONFIRMED parts:
- src/server/actions/preview.ts is 898 lines and holds the sync-planning orchestration inline: planChunk (:152-314), fetchAndPreview (:342-493), previewStoreChunk (:502-625), previewFromStore (:661-756), plus startStoreSync/advanceStoreSync/cancelStoreSync (:818-898). ~550 lines of orchestration in a "use server" module.
- src/server/actions/config.ts (394 lines): updatePricing (:50-148), savePricingRules (:346-394), helpers (:149-345) edit pricing rules inline against updateActiveConfig.
- src/app/api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 imports runKicksdbRefresh from @/server/actions/feeds; src/server/scheduler.ts:14 does too.
- store-edit.ts:37-45 calls getWooClient().batchVariations inline and :50 patchSnapshotProduct; duplicates.ts:38 calls getWooClient().deleteProduct inline.

OVERSTATED / wrong:
1. Every line range cited is off (e.g. planChunk is :152 not :109; fetchAndPreview :342 not :256; updatePricing :50 not :41; savePricingRules :346 not :262; runKicksdbRefresh :168 not :152). Functions exist, but the evidence as given does not point at them.
2. runKicksdbRefresh (feeds.ts:168-222) is NOT "real domain logic": it is a ~50-line wrapper that validates input, resolves config, and calls refreshStaleCatalog in src/server/catalog/refresh.ts (:27+), the actual service, plus ingestion-run bookkeeping. Likewise export.ts:50-102 delegates the real work to buildReimport (src/server/store-json/patch.ts); only the ~20-line plan-selection-to-patch loop is inline. debug.ts is diagnostic tooling, not a middleware capability.
3. The pure domain math is already outside actions: preview.ts:5,244 calls buildPlan from @core/core-spine; config.ts:5-7 uses resolveEffectiveRule/computePrice from @core. What sits in the action files is orchestration (matching via store-json/match, ledger via sync/ledger, persistence via plans/repo), not the pricing/planning algorithms.
4. The implication "must be moved out of Next server-action files before they can be exposed to any non-UI caller" is refuted by the repo itself: non-UI server code already imports and calls these "use server" exports — src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 imports startStoreSync/advanceStoreSync from actions/preview (and price-sync is used by scheduler.ts, vetrina/operations/margin.ts, actions/vetrina-prices.ts, sync/store-edits.ts); scheduler.ts:14 and the cron route call runKicksdbRefresh. A "use server" module's async exports are ordinary server functions when imported server-side; the only constraint is that the module may export only async functions (feeds.ts:22 comment). Moving them to src/server services is a boundary-hygiene refactor, not a precondition for a programmatic/MCP caller. Severity "major" does not hold; this is a moderate structural finding.
  - Corrected claim: Two action files hold substantial sync/pricing orchestration inside "use server" modules rather than in src/server services: preview.ts (898 lines; planChunk :152-314, fetchAndPreview :342-493, previewStoreChunk :502-625, previewFromStore :661-756, store-sync run control :818-898) and config.ts (394 lines; updatePricing :50-148, savePricingRules :346-394 and helpers). The pure planning/pricing math (buildPlan, computePrice, resolveEffectiveRule) already lives in @core; what is in the action files is matching, ledger, persistence and config-lock orchestration. feeds.ts runKicksdbRefresh (:168-222) is a thin wrapper over src/server/catalog/refresh.ts refreshStaleCatalog, imported by the cron route (api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3) and scheduler.ts:14; export.ts delegates to buildReimport; store-edit.ts (:37-50) and duplicates.ts (:38) call the Woo client inline. Non-UI server code already consumes these action exports (sync/price-sync.ts:2, scheduler.ts:14, the cron route), so exposing them to an LLM/MCP caller does not strictly require moving them first; extracting preview.ts and config.ts orchestration into src/server services is a worthwhile hygiene refactor (same cost under keep or extract) but not a blocker. Severity: moderate.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Factual part mostly stands (line numbers drift: planChunk is at preview.ts:152, fetchAndPreview :342, previewStoreChunk :502, previewFromStore :661; updatePricing config.ts:50, savePricingRules :346; runKicksdbRefresh feeds.ts:168; store-edit.ts:45-66 calls client.batchVariations + patchSnapshotProduct; duplicates.ts:37 calls getWooClient().deleteProduct). But the implication — that this logic "must be moved out of Next server-action files before it can be exposed to any non-UI caller" and that this is a major, refactor-not-wrapper cost — is wrong, and the repo itself proves it:

1. Non-UI callers already consume these exact "use server" functions as plain imports: src/app/api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 (Route Handler -> runKicksdbRefresh), src/server/scheduler.ts:14 and :224 (in-process scheduler -> runKicksdbRefresh), src/app/api/publish/route.ts:2 and :40 (plain POST route -> runPublish(input) with a JSON body), and src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 (headless sync -> startStoreSync/advanceStoreSync from preview.ts). A "use server" module is an ordinary server module to any server-side importer; the pragma only generates RPC endpoints for client components. So the cron import the auditor cites as a smell is actually the existing wrapper pattern working.

2. The functions are already tool-call shaped: zod-validated plain inputs (preview.ts:343, config.ts:347, feeds.ts:171, store-edit.ts:33, duplicates.ts:26) and serializable {ok, error, ...} results. None of the cited files (preview, config, feeds, debug, export, store-edit, duplicates) touch cookies()/headers()/redirect()/revalidatePath — grep shows only vetrina.ts and vetrina-operations.ts import next/cache's refresh. So there is no request/UI coupling that would have to be unpicked; a Route Handler or MCP tool can call them directly, as src/app/api/publish/route.ts already does.

3. src/server/api/v1.ts:9-20 plus src/app/api/v1/* show the serve()/apiError() wrapper already exists for exactly this; adding /api/v1 endpoints for preview/pricing is a wrapper per function, not a relocation of ~1000 lines.

Correct framing: the placement is a code-organisation nit (and a weak-ish layering: src/server/sync/price-sync.ts depends on src/server/actions/preview.ts). It is not a precondition for LLM/MCP exposure and does not change the keep-vs-extract calculus. Severity should be minor, with the real gaps being coverage (no /api/v1 route for preview/sync/pricing yet) rather than code location.
  - Corrected claim: Several action files (preview.ts ~900 lines of sync planning, config.ts pricing-rule editing, feeds.ts runKicksdbRefresh, debug.ts, export.ts; store-edit.ts and duplicates.ts calling the Woo client inline) keep domain logic in "use server" modules rather than src/server services. This is a layering/organisation nit, not a blocker: these functions take zod-validated plain inputs, return serializable results, use no request-scoped Next APIs, and are already imported and called from non-UI callers (the cron route, the in-process scheduler, /api/publish, and src/server/sync/price-sync.ts). Exposing them to an LLM/MCP caller is a thin Route Handler or tool wrapper per function via the existing src/server/api/v1.ts serve() pattern; moving them into services is optional cleanup. Severity: minor. The real gap is coverage (no /api/v1 endpoints yet for preview/sync/pricing), not code location.

#### PC-5 [confirmed · major] The Hub has no WordPress-side capability beyond the plugin's homepage routes: it uses wc/v3 and wc-gh/v1 only, never wp/v2, so navigation menus, non-homepage pages/posts and the media library are not in any class (A/B/C/D).

- **Evidence**: src/server/woo/client.ts: only 'wc/v3' string (grep), methods list at lines 219-667 (products, variations, orders read, categories/brands/tags/attributes); src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32 `${origin}/wp-json/wc-gh/v1` and routes at :135-208 (capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history); grep for wp/v2|nav_menu|menu-items in src/server returns no WP endpoint usage; src/server/woo/media.ts:82 attaches images via product `images`.
- **Implication**: The owner's example (change the navigation menu alongside Woo categories) is not a matter of exposing an existing capability — it is new integration work (WP REST wp/v2 or more plugin routes). The codebase's structure does not currently cover mixed WP+Woo needs; this is dimension-relevant because it bounds what 'exposing the middleware' can deliver without new adapters.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location and searched for counterexamples. (1) src/server/woo/client.ts:192 builds the root as `${base}/wp-json/wc/v3` (or uses WOO_BASE_URL verbatim if it already contains /wp-json); every method at lines 219-667 (getProductsPage, getAllVariations, getOrdersPage, getFullProduct, getDefaultCatalogOrderby, getAttributeTaxonomies, listBrands/createBrand, listCategories/createCategory, listTags/createTag, listAttributeTerms/createAttributeTerm, createAttribute, batchVariations, getProductIndexPage, batchUpdateProducts, deleteProduct, findProductsBySku, createProduct, updateProduct, getProduct, updateProductOnce) is a wc/v3 product/variation/order/taxonomy call. (2) src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32 roots at `${origin}/wp-json/wc-gh/v1`, and the only routes (lines 135-208) are capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history; pageParam() (lines 40-43) only lets the configured homepage be a non-front page id, still through the plugin's homepage routes, not generic page/post editing. (3) A repo-wide grep (src, core, docs, scripts) for wp/v2, nav_menu, menu-item(s), wp_navigation, xmlrpc, wp-cli, /media returned no WP REST usage; the only 'wp-admin' hits are deep-link URLs for the operator (e.g. src/components/publish/PublishWorkspace.tsx:810) and i18n copy. (4) src/server/woo/media.ts attaches photos by writing the product's `images` array via updateProductOnce (media.ts:50, :82, :362, :391), never via wp/v2/media. (5) The repo's own design doc confirms the boundary: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 lists "Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it", and :36 lists menus among the things the Hub leaves alone. The claim is literally true; the only nuance (configurable page_id for the homepage) does not change it.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub has no WordPress-side capability beyond the golden-hive-blocks plugin's homepage routes: src/server/woo/client.ts talks only to wc/v3 (products, variations, orders read, categories/brands/tags/attributes) and src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts only to wc-gh/v1 (capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history, optionally targeting a configured page_id). No code uses wp/v2 or any other WP namespace, so navigation menus, non-homepage pages/posts and the media library (photos are attached only through the Woo product `images` field) fall in no capability class; docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 explicitly assigns navigation menus to the plugin/WordPress side. Mixed WP+Woo requests like "change the menu alongside these categories" would require new adapters (wp/v2 or new plugin routes).
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Factual part confirmed and strengthened. src/server/woo/client.ts:192 hard-codes the root to `${base}/wp-json/wc/v3`; src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32 hard-codes `${origin}/wp-json/wc-gh/v1`, and its route surface (wp-source.ts:135-208) is capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history only. A repo-wide grep for `wp/v2`, `nav_menu`, `menu-items` in src/ returns nothing; the only `/media` hits are the Hub's own photo queue (src/server/woo/media.ts, /api/cron/media), which attaches images through the Woo product `images` field, not wp/v2/media.

The implication (menus/pages/media are new integration work, not an exposure problem) is a genuine consequence, and two things the auditor did not cite make it stronger rather than weaker: (1) wp-source.ts:19-22 documents that the Hub authenticates with WooCommerce REST keys, which WordPress honours only for `wc-*` namespaces — so reaching wp/v2 is not just a new URL root but a new credential type (application password / cookie auth) or new plugin routes under wc-gh/v1; (2) docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 explicitly assigns "Navigation menus → Plugin: WordPress's menu editor already does it", and docs/hub-and-plugin.md:35-36 says people keep editing menus in WordPress. The absence is a deliberate ownership decision, which means closing the gap for an LLM-driven middleware requires reversing a documented architectural boundary, not merely wiring an endpoint.

Mitigations I checked for: the generic HTTP adapter (src/server/adapters/http.ts:53,65) and the VetrinaSource interface (source.ts) give an established seam for adding plugin-side routes, and the Hub already writes WordPress page content (homepage blocks) through that seam, so the extension path is known and bounded. That tempers the severity slightly but does not refute the implication: the owner's own motivating example (menu + categories in one request) cannot be served by exposing what exists today. Severity "major" is defensible given that example is the stated use case; a competent engineer would reach the same conclusion.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub has no WordPress-side capability beyond the golden-hive-blocks plugin's homepage routes: it talks only to wc/v3 (src/server/woo/client.ts:192) and wc-gh/v1 (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32, routes at :135-208), never wp/v2, so navigation menus, non-homepage pages/posts and the media library are in no class. This is a documented, deliberate ownership boundary (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 assigns navigation menus to WordPress's own editor), and the Hub's credentials (WooCommerce REST keys) only authenticate wc-* namespaces (wp-source.ts:19-22), so serving mixed WP+Woo requests such as "change the menu alongside these categories" requires new adapters and either new auth or new plugin routes — though the existing VetrinaSource/wc-gh/v1 seam shows the established, bounded path for adding them.

#### PC-6 [refuted · minor] Half of the Vetrina read model is UI-only: catalog overview, term detail, product placement, rail/cards/history, term actions/margin state and homepage section options have no /api/v1 read, while writes to those same objects are exposed.

- **Evidence**: src/server/actions/vetrina-catalog.ts:48-76 readOverview/readTerm/readPlacement (vetrina/catalog.ts:766,822,871) vs src/server/api/reads.ts:36,98 exposing only readHomepage and readProducts; vetrina-operations.ts:92-126 loadTermActions/loadTermMargin/loadHomepageSections; vetrina.ts:37-54 readRail/readCards/readHistory; vetrina-prices.ts:13-60 loadProductPrices/publishProductPrices (runPriceSync) absent from API.
- **Implication**: An LLM needs to read state before proposing; today it can list products, terms, order and homepage but cannot see a term's margin, a product's placement, or price-sync state. These are thin reads (cheap to add) but their absence means the API is write-complete yet read-incomplete for the Vetrina scope.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Checked every cited location and the full /api/v1 route tree (src/app/api/v1/**). The claim's evidence is incomplete: it says reads.ts exposes "only readHomepage and readProducts", but src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:23-34 (listTerms, served by src/app/api/v1/catalog/terms/route.ts) is built directly on readOverview() and returns every category/brand with id, name, slug, parent, path, count; catalog-order.ts:49-78 (readOrder) serves a term's products in shop order; and GET /operations, /operations/{id}, /proposals/{id}, /order-sources all exist. Item by item: (1) "catalog overview — no read": false, /catalog/terms is readOverview minus quick-view counts/indexProducts/demo (catalog.ts:766-784). (2) "term detail — no read": readTerm (catalog.ts:822-825) returns term + ancestors + children; /catalog/terms gives parent and path for every term, so ancestry/children are derivable, though there is no per-term endpoint. (3) "product placement — no read": readPlacement (catalog.ts:871-897) returns status, visibility, categories(+automatic,path), brands, sections(position,pinned), source; GET /catalog/products (reads.ts:98-128) already returns status, visibility, source, categories, brands, tags and sections{key,title,position,pinned} per product — only the per-category `automatic`/`path`/ids are missing (and `automatic` is exposed per section in readHomepage reads.ts:57). (4) "rail/cards — no read": readHomepage (reads.ts:36-78) exposes each section's key, attrsHash, shows, switchedOff, limit, fallback, pinned, hidden, editable (= pinnable, sections.ts:49) and first 12 products — most of readRail/loadHomepageSections; readCards (lookup by ids) has no exact equivalent since ProductsQuery has no ids filter. (5) Genuinely absent: readHistory (section revisions, service.ts:146), loadTermActions (standing hide/sale per term, operations/service.ts:184), loadTermMargin (operations/service.ts:227; the `margin` write IS exposed via registry.ts:121), and loadProductPrices/publishProductPrices (vetrina-prices.ts:25-60). (6) But the "write-complete yet read-incomplete" framing is wrong for price sync: runPriceSync/publishProductPrices appears nowhere under src/app/api or src/server/api (grep empty) and is not an action kind in registry.ts, so price sync is UI-only on BOTH sides, not a read gap against an exposed write. Net: of the ten cited loaders, four have no API counterpart (history, term actions, term margin, product prices) and the rest are served in compact form by /catalog/terms, /catalog/products and /homepage. "Half of the read model is UI-only" and "overview, term detail, product placement, rail/cards have no /api/v1 read" are overstated; the real gap is narrower (margin state, standing hide/sale per term, section revision history, per-section `takes` for a target, and price-sync state) and the correction is not trivial because it removes most of the listed items and one of the asymmetry examples.
  - Corrected claim: The /api/v1 reads (GET /homepage, /catalog/products, /catalog/terms, /catalog/order, /operations, /proposals/{id}, /order-sources) already cover, in compact form, the catalog overview, term tree, product placement (categories, brands, source, homepage sections with position/pinned) and the homepage sections (key, source, switch, pins, hidden, pinnable, first products). What remains UI-only (server actions) is a smaller set of reads: a term's margin state (loadTermMargin — while the `margin` write is exposed via /proposals), a term's standing hide/sale (loadTermActions), a section's revision history (readHistory), products by explicit ids (readCards), the per-target `takes` count of loadHomepageSections, and the price-sync sheet (loadProductPrices) — the last of which has no API write either (publishProductPrices/runPriceSync is not an action kind), so it is a read-and-write gap, not a read-only one. These are thin additions; the API is read-incomplete mainly for margin, standing term actions and revision history.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): The factual core (readOverview/readTerm/readPlacement, loadTermActions/loadTermMargin/loadHomepageSections, readRail/readCards/readHistory, loadProductPrices are server-action-only; src/server/api/reads.ts exposes only readHomepage/readProducts) is correct, but the implication "write-complete yet read-incomplete" and its three concrete examples do not hold up against the code.

1. "Cannot see a term's margin" — wrong. The API's design is propose→preview→confirm (src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts; docs/api.md:189-236). A margin proposal's preview carries `margin.current: TermMarginState` (src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:198 and :232; src/server/vetrina/operations/margin.ts:485-494 `current: await marginState(...)`), i.e. the term's own/inherited/standard margin and priced counts — exactly what loadTermMargin returns (service.ts:227-229 delegates to the same margin.ts reader). Posting a proposal writes nothing and expires unconfirmed, so it is a usable read of margin state over /api/v1.

2. "Cannot see a product's placement" — mostly wrong. GET /api/v1/catalog/products already returns per product `visibility`, `status`, `source`, `categories`, `brands` and `sections` {key,title,position,pinned} (reads.ts:98-125), which is ProductPlacement (lib/vetrina/catalog.ts:192-207) minus category ids/paths and the `automatic` flag; GET /homepage adds `automatic`, `pinned`, `hidden`, `editable` per section (reads.ts:44-66), covering HomepageSectionOption (catalog-ops.ts:342-356). readTerm/readOverview are derivable from GET /catalog/terms (id, parent, path, count; catalog-order.ts:24-35), and readHistory/term hide+sale state are in GET /operations, the same log loadTermActions is computed from (service.ts:184-215 reads repo.termOperationRows; operations/route.ts:8-17).

3. "Cannot see price-sync state" — true, but the premise "writes to those same objects are exposed" is false here: publishProductPrices/runPriceSync has no /api/v1 route either (grep of src/app/api for runPriceSync/price-sync: none), and it is a sync-domain server action, not a Vetrina action in the registry (registry.ts:49-218). So price-sync is neither read- nor write-exposed; the asymmetry the auditor infers does not exist for it.

Residual, genuine gaps (per-term standing hide/sale summary with `held` counts, rail history per section, per-SKU price locks/asks, category ids on a product) are smaller than claimed and the propose/preview path with `summary` sentences and 409 "stale" on confirm is the designed mitigation for "read before proposing". A competent engineer would conclude the API is not "read-incomplete for the Vetrina scope" in the way stated; the finding is at most a note about a few convenience reads, below "minor".
  - Corrected claim: A few Vetrina reads remain server-action-only (per-term standing hide/sale summary from loadTermActions, per-section rail history, per-SKU price locks/asks from loadProductPrices, category ids on a product), but the API is not read-incomplete relative to its writes: a term's margin state is returned in a margin proposal's preview (`margin.current`), a product's placement (visibility, status, source, categories, brands, homepage sections with position/pinned) is in GET /catalog/products plus GET /homepage, term hierarchy is in GET /catalog/terms, and the action log is GET /operations. Price-sync has neither a read nor a write on /api/v1 and is not a Vetrina action at all. The propose→preview→confirm flow is the designed "read state before acting" path for a tool-calling client. Severity: trivial / note.

#### PC-7 [contested · minor] Automated coverage of the programmatic layer is 5 Playwright tests exercising 2 of 21 action kinds via proposals plus the `order` route and 3 reads; order-sources endpoints, catalog/terms, DELETE, Accept-Language and the 13 catalog kinds via proposals are untested, and src/server/api/*.ts has no unit tests.

- **Evidence**: e2e/api.spec.ts:30,41,74,99,123 (test titles), kinds at :76 sectionSwitch, :101 sectionMove; paths called at :26,34,46,59,75,87,101,127,141,146,148; e2e/helpers.ts:10-24 api() helper; grep for api/v1|server/api across src/**/*.test.ts matched only src/proxy.test.ts; src/server/api/* = 373 lines, no *.test.ts.
- **Implication**: If this codebase becomes the spec for a fresh repo or an extracted package, the API contract is documented (docs/api.md:67-257) but only lightly pinned by tests; growing the API in place would need a contract test per kind to be safe, and extraction would lose even the current e2e coupling to the demo shop.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location. e2e/api.spec.ts has exactly 5 test() calls (:30, :41, :74, :99, :123). Proposals carry only kind "sectionSwitch" (:76) and "sectionMove" (:101); the only direct write route is POST /catalog/order (:46); other POSTs are /proposals, /proposals/{id}/confirm, /operations/{id}/undo (:59,:87,:90,:94,:119-120). e2e/helpers.ts:10-24 api() helper accepts only method "GET" | "POST" (:12), so no DELETE is possible through it, and it sets no Accept-Language header (:18). Grep of e2e/*.spec.ts for api( outside api.spec.ts: only e2e/sources.spec.ts:43,46,48 (GET /operations?limit=10, POST undo, GET /operations/{id}) — endpoints already covered, so no new coverage; no spec calls /order-sources or /catalog/terms (routes exist at src/app/api/v1/order-sources/{route,[id]/route,[id]/apply/route}.ts and src/app/api/v1/catalog/terms/route.ts; DELETE handler at src/app/api/v1/order-sources/[id]/route.ts:22; accept-language parsing at src/server/api/v1.ts:39). src/server/api holds 4 files, 373 lines (wc), no *.test.ts; the only src test matching api/v1|server/api is src/proxy.test.ts:76,138, which tests proxy routing of the path, not the API code; no test imports @/server/api (only the route files do). Registry (src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts) has 21 kinds: 13 scope "catalog", 8 scope "homepage", confirming the counts. The only imprecision is "3 reads": the spec also reads /actions (:34) and /operations/{id} (:28), i.e. 5 distinct GET endpoints; and sources.spec.ts touches the API too. Both are trivial corrections that do not change the direction or severity, so the claim stands.
  - Corrected claim: Automated coverage of the programmatic layer is 5 Playwright tests in e2e/api.spec.ts exercising 2 of 21 action kinds via proposals (sectionSwitch, sectionMove) plus the POST /catalog/order route and five GET endpoints (/actions, /homepage, /catalog/products, /operations, /operations/{id}); e2e/sources.spec.ts additionally reuses the /operations list/get/undo endpoints as a cleanup helper, and src/proxy.test.ts checks only that the proxy gates /api/v1 paths by host/secret. The order-sources endpoints (GET/POST/DELETE/apply), GET /catalog/terms, the DELETE method, the Accept-Language branch (src/server/api/v1.ts:39) and the 13 catalog-scope kinds via proposals are untested, and src/server/api/*.ts (373 lines) has no unit tests.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The factual part holds (e2e/api.spec.ts has 5 tests; e2e/sources.spec.ts:46 adds one undo call; no *.test.ts under src/server/api). The implication does not follow, because the auditor placed the API contract in the wrong layer.

1. The per-kind contract is not in src/server/api or the routes; it is in the registry, and it is already pinned per kind. POST /api/v1/proposals (src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:16-26) is kind-agnostic: it hands body.action straight to propose() (src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:105-109), which calls parseAction (registry.ts:269-273). GET /api/v1/actions (actions/route.ts:13) returns describeActions() unchanged. src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.test.ts:20-36 asserts for EVERY kind that ACTION_KINDS equals the union of OperationInputSchema options + PAGE_ACTION_KINDS (so a new kind cannot ship undescribed), that each example parses as that kind, that each JSON Schema has kind.const === kind and a description > 40 chars, and that bad inputs are refused with the exact owner-facing message. That is a contract test per kind for exactly the surface an LLM/MCP client consumes; it runs in CI (ci.yml:32 `npm test`). "Growing the API in place would need a contract test per kind" is therefore wrong: adding a kind touches registry.ts and the input union, both already guarded; the HTTP layer adds zero kind-specific code.

2. Behaviour per kind is tested below the API, against the same fixture demo shop: operations.db.test.ts (~35 cases: hide/show/sale/unsale/tag/categorize/pin/order etc., lines 394-1100), page.db.test.ts:54-180 (all 9 homepage kinds: switch, move, arrange, add, remove, source, edit, blockEdit, stale write), proposals.db.test.ts:66-141 (preview-without-write, confirm-once idempotence, 409 stale replacement, refused, expiry), plus plan tests (order-plan, margin-plan, proposal-plan). The kinds grep across these tests covers all 21 kinds. These use VETRINA_SOURCE=fixture (proposals.db.test.ts:15-18), i.e. the same demo shop the e2e uses.

3. "Extraction would lose even the current e2e coupling to the demo shop" is backwards: the coupling that matters lives in the vitest db tests, which sit with the core (src/server/vetrina/**) and would move with it; only the 5 Playwright tests (which also exercise the Konsta UI log, api.spec.ts:51-56) would stay behind, and they pin routing/auth, not kinds.

4. What is genuinely unpinned is narrower than claimed: the ~370 lines of API glue — serve() auth/503/401/language/status mapping (v1.ts:57-70), zod query schemas (reads.ts:76-90, catalog-order.ts:37-42), idsOfSkus (catalog-order.ts:91-98), uploadFrom content-type handling (order-sources.ts:21-47) — and the opt-in nature of db tests (RUN_DB_TESTS=1 gate, proposals.db.test.ts:12; CI runs `npm test` without it, ci.yml:32). A competent engineer would conclude: add unit tests for src/server/api/* and run db tests in CI, not "a contract test per kind". Severity minor is acceptable for that residual, but the stated implication overstates the risk and misdirects the remedy.
  - Corrected claim: HTTP-level coverage of /api/v1 is thin (5 Playwright tests over the order route, 2 proposal kinds and 3 reads; order-sources, catalog/terms, DELETE and Accept-Language untested) and the ~370 lines of API glue in src/server/api/*.ts (auth/status mapping, query schemas, SKU resolution, upload parsing) have no unit tests. However, the per-kind action contract an LLM would consume is not in that layer: POST /proposals and GET /actions are kind-agnostic pass-throughs to the registry, and registry.test.ts already pins every kind's presence, example, JSON Schema and refusals, while operations/page/proposals db tests cover all 21 kinds' behaviour on the fixture demo shop (opt-in via RUN_DB_TESTS, not run in CI). Growing the API does not need a contract test per kind; the gaps worth closing are unit tests for the API glue and wiring the db tests into CI. Extracting the core would carry its demo-shop tests with it, losing only the 5 UI-coupled e2e tests.


<a id="layering"></a>
## Layering and framework coupling of the core (src/server + core/ + src/lib vs Next.js and the UI)

### Summary

The domain code is far less coupled to Next.js than its location suggests. Across the 145 non-test files of src/server (32,803 lines), core/ (3 files, 2,222 lines) and src/lib, exactly 3 files import from "next": the two server-action wrappers src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3 and vetrina-operations.ts:3 (`refresh` from next/cache) and the HTTP envelope src/server/api/v1.ts:2 (NextRequest/NextResponse). No revalidatePath/revalidateTag anywhere; `cookies()` is used only in src/i18n/server.ts:2, which is imported by 3 action files and nothing in the domain. Environment access is centralized: src/lib/env.ts (190 lines, Zod, validated at module load) is imported by 15 server files, and the only out-of-band process.env reads in production code are NODE_ENV checks (scheduler.ts:843, db/client.ts:14) and the debug heap-watch (heap-watch.ts:42-47). The operations layer renders user-facing words through a single AsyncLocalStorage in src/server/vetrina/operations/words.ts (the only AsyncLocalStorage in the codebase); the dictionaries are plain TS objects, so a headless caller gets English by wrapping the call in `inLanguage("en", fn)`, exactly as /api/v1 already does from Accept-Language (v1.ts:38-39,63), and proposals persist their locale (proposals.ts:83,153,159). The real friction for a plain-Node entry point is mechanical, not architectural: 87 server files (plus src/lib/env.ts, src/lib/chunk.ts, src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts) start with `import "server-only"`, which throws outside a React server bundle, and the `@/` / `@core/` tsconfig path aliases need a loader; vitest already proves the domain runs under plain Node by aliasing server-only to a stub (vitest.config.ts) and running 17 *.db.test.ts suites with environment "node". There are genuine layering leaks to record: two domain modules import the "use server" actions layer upward (scheduler.ts:14 -> actions/feeds.ts; sync/price-sync.ts:2 -> actions/preview.ts, a 898-line file holding real sync logic), two actions import a server module misfiled under src/components (actions/catalog.ts:8, actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5), and the UI reaches directly into src/server internals from 96 files (not only via actions). core/core-spine.ts is live, not stale: its types and pricing/plan functions are imported by ~20 server files, but its WooStoreAdapter (core-spine.ts:792) is dead code and the real Woo client (src/server/woo/client.ts:182) does not implement StorePort. Finally, operation execution is an in-process queue on globalThis (runner.ts:11-25, 23 globalThis singletons in 15 files), so a separate MCP process importing the domain would run a second, uncoordinated writer unless it talks to the Hub over HTTP; an MCP endpoint inside the same Node process (as docs/operations.md:86-107 sketches) avoids this entirely.

### Inventory

## i18n coupling depth
- Entry: `words()` / `inLanguage()` / `currentLanguage()` in words.ts:26-37. Default locale "it" (src/i18n/config.ts:7) when no `inLanguage` is active.
- 84 call sites across 17 domain files (context.ts, margin.ts, margin-plan.ts, input.ts, page.ts, page-input.ts, plan.ts, preview.ts, proposals.ts, proposal-plan.ts, registry.ts, runner.ts, service.ts, standing.ts, writes.ts, order.ts, order-sources/service.ts) plus 4 adapters (3 actions, api/v1.ts).
- page.ts:90 pulls the **whole** Dictionary and reads `vetrina.blocks`, `vetrina.editor`, `vetrina.section`, `vetrinaCatalog.sections` (UI-section dictionaries), not only `ops`.
- Dictionaries: src/i18n has 29 non-test TS files, 6,475 lines; ops.en.ts 294 lines, ops.it.ts 369 lines; src/i18n/dictionary.ts and config.ts have no next/react imports. Only provider.tsx (react, next/navigation) and server.ts (next/headers) are framework-bound.
- Headless English: `inLanguage("en", () => ...)` — the pattern api/v1.ts:63 already uses; registry descriptions/JSON Schema are English by design (registry.ts:17-19).

## core/ usage (is it stale?)
- core/core-spine.ts 930 lines, core/config.ts 610, core/collections.ts 682; no imports outside `./` (verified). Alias `@core/*` in tsconfig.json paths; `@/*` -> ./src/*.
- Importers of @core/core-spine (non-test): catalog/repo.ts:4, catalog/refresh.ts:2, catalog/service.ts:1, catalog/enrich.ts:35, catalog/woo-register.ts:2, vetrina/operations/margin.ts:5, margin-plan.ts:12, plans/repo.ts:3, woo/publish.ts:3, woo/live-check.ts:1, woo/publish-plan.ts:1, woo/repair.ts:13, woo/identity.ts:2, actions/debug.ts:9, actions/config.ts:6-7, actions/preview.ts:5 (buildPlan), feeds/goldensneakers-model.ts:2, feeds/ownership.ts:1, feeds/owner.ts:2, sync/store-edit-plan.ts:1, sync/ledger.ts:3, kicks/service.ts:1, adapters/kicksdb/client.ts:6,81 (KicksDbSource implements SourcePort); plus UI: components/catalog/drawer-data.ts:4, components/preview/*.tsx (types).
- @core/config: 24 importers (server + components + lib); @core/collections: 14 importers.
- Dead: `WooStoreAdapter implements StorePort` at core-spine.ts:792 has zero importers; real client is `class WooClient` src/server/woo/client.ts:182 with `getWooClient()` at :683 reading env directly — it does not implement StorePort.

## Framework adapters (thin) vs domain
- src/app/api: 22 route.ts files, 578 lines total, 11-39 lines each (sample: src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts = 14 lines calling `serve(req, ...)` + `describeActions()`).
- src/server/api/v1.ts (71 lines): bearer check vs env.HUB_API_TOKEN (:57-61), `inLanguage(languageOf(req), handler)` (:63), VetrinaError/ZodError -> JSON envelope (:65-69). Only Next-typed module in src/server/api.
- src/server/api/reads.ts (`readHomepage()`, `readProducts(query)`), catalog-order.ts (`listTerms`, `readOrder`, `order`) take plain params; order-sources.ts takes web-standard `Request` (:21,:46).
- src/server/actions: 25 files, 4,189 non-test lines, all "use server". Largest: preview.ts 898, config.ts 394, sync.ts 332, debug.ts 311, vetrina.ts 266, feeds.ts 222.

## Layering leaks (direction: domain -> adapter, or server -> UI)
1. src/server/scheduler.ts:14 `import { runKicksdbRefresh } from "@/server/actions/feeds"`.
2. src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 `import { advanceStoreSync, startStoreSync, type PreviewResult } from "@/server/actions/preview"` (preview.ts holds fetchAndPreview :342, previewFromStore :661, startStoreSync :818, advanceStoreSync :841 — domain logic inside a "use server" module; neither file imports next, so this is a structural not a runtime leak).
3. src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts is a server-only module (imports @/server/catalog/repo, overrides/repo, feeds/owner, store-json/repo, sync/ledger, pricing/store-terms) living under components; imported by 2 actions.
4. UI -> server direct imports: 96 files under src/app + src/components import "@/server/*": actions 70, vetrina 41, api 20, woo 13, db 11, store-json 10, config 10, overrides 6, sync 3, feeds 3, catalog 3, others 1-2 each.
5. globalThis singletons: 23 occurrences / 15 files (vetrina/catalog.ts:542,756; operations/runner.ts:35; words.ts:22; standing.ts; powers.ts; woo/publishing.ts; woo/taxonomy-cache.ts; cache/redis.ts:7; scheduler.ts; sync/apply-job.ts; db/client.ts:11; pricing/store-terms.ts; collections/runner.ts; fixture sources). Documented reason (runner.ts:19-21): Next's instrumentation bundle and server-actions bundle each load their own module copy.

## Runtime prerequisites for a plain-Node import of the domain
- `server-only` (87 + 3 files): real package throws outside a react-server bundle; vitest.config.ts aliases it to src/test/server-only-stub.ts. A Node/tsx entry needs the same alias (or `--conditions=react-server`, or removing the 90 import lines).
- Path aliases `@/*`, `@core/*` (tsconfig.json): need tsx/tsconfig-paths or a build step.
- src/lib/env.ts requires DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL (env.ts:154-155) at module load; Redis degrades to MemoryCache on error (cache/redis.ts:27-36) but the URL must parse.
- Config data: src/config/index.ts `hubConfig` is pure data, imported by 11 server files (fine headless).
- Existing headless precedents: scripts/migrate.mjs (plain node, pg + drizzle), src/instrumentation.ts (dynamic import of scheduler/heap-watch when NEXT_RUNTIME === "nodejs"), 17 *.db.test.ts suites under vitest node environment.

## Extraction effort estimate (files to touch)
- **Headless entry inside the Next process (MCP route handler or a tsx script with the vitest-style alias): 0 domain files.** New: 1 entry file + loader/alias config.
- **Importable package / separate service:**
  - mechanical: remove `import "server-only"` in 90 files (sed-able);
  - 2 files: strip `refresh()` (next/cache) out of actions/vetrina.ts and actions/vetrina-operations.ts (or leave actions in the app shell);
  - 3 files: actions that call `getLocale()` (next/headers) stay in the app shell or take locale as a parameter;
  - 2 files + relocation: scheduler.ts:14 and sync/price-sync.ts:2 need runKicksdbRefresh and start/advanceStoreSync moved out of actions/feeds.ts (222 lines) and actions/preview.ts (898 lines) into src/server/{feeds,sync};
  - 1 file move + 2 importers: components/catalog/drawer-data.ts -> src/server;
  - i18n: move src/i18n/config.ts, dictionary.ts and the catalog dictionaries (at least ops.*, sections.*, and the `vetrina.*` groups page.ts reads) with the core, or narrow page.ts:90 to a domain-owned dictionary slice (1 file + dictionary restructuring);
  - env.ts: make REDIS_URL optional or inject; `@/` alias -> package paths (build-time, not per-file);
  - runner/queue: if the service runs in a separate process from the UI, the in-process queue on globalThis (runner.ts) and the presence/term caches must move to a shared coordinator (DB lock/Redis) or the UI must call the service over HTTP — this is the one non-mechanical design change.
  - Rough total: ~10 files with real edits, ~900 lines relocated, ~90 one-line deletions, plus the queue decision.

### Claims and verdicts

#### L1 [refuted · strength] Only 3 files in src/server + core + src/lib import anything from Next.js, and none of them is a domain module: two server-action wrappers (next/cache refresh) and the /api/v1 HTTP envelope (next/server types).

- **Evidence**: grep -rn 'from "next' src/server core src/lib -> src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:3, src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3 (import { refresh } from "next/cache"), src/server/api/v1.ts:2 (NextResponse, NextRequest). refresh() calls at vetrina-operations.ts:113 and vetrina.ts:48. No revalidatePath/revalidateTag anywhere; cookies() only in src/i18n/server.ts:2,8.
- **Implication**: The operations/woo/sync/collections modules are already framework-agnostic at the import level; exposing them to an MCP/tool layer does not require rewriting domain code. Strong argument for (a) keep or (b) extract over (c) fresh rewrite.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The narrow numeric part checks out: `grep -rn 'from "next' src/server core src/lib` yields exactly src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:3 and src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3 (`import { refresh } from "next/cache"`, used at vetrina.ts:48 and vetrina-operations.ts:113) and src/server/api/v1.ts:2 (`NextResponse, type NextRequest` from "next/server"). No revalidatePath/revalidateTag anywhere; cookies() only in src/i18n/server.ts:8. core/*.ts import only from each other (core/collections.ts:36,58-59). But the implication "already framework-agnostic at the import level" is false, because the grep pattern misses bare side-effect imports. ~90 files in src/server and src/lib/env.ts:1 start with `import "server-only";` — including every domain module the claim names: src/server/woo/client.ts:1, publish.ts:1, apply.ts:1, pull.ts:1; src/server/sync/apply-job.ts:1, price-sync.ts:1, ledger.ts:1; src/server/collections/service.ts:1, runner.ts:1, store.ts:1; src/server/vetrina/operations/*.ts:1 (service, runner, plan, order, writes...). `server-only` (node_modules/server-only/index.js) unconditionally `throw new Error("This module cannot be imported from a Client Component module...")` unless the bundler resolves the `react-server` export condition — i.e. it is a React/Next marker that crashes under plain Node. The repo itself acknowledges this: vitest.config.ts:9-11 aliases "server-only" to src/test/server-only-stub.ts with the comment "Next's server-only guard throws outside a server bundle". So an MCP/tool host running these modules outside Next would need the same alias (or a mass removal of the marker). Additionally, all src/server/actions/*.ts carry the Next-only `"use server"` directive, and two of them pull UI code into the server layer: src/server/actions/catalog.ts:8 and src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5 import `loadDrawerData` from "@/components/catalog/drawer-data". The direction of the claim (coupling is thin, domain logic does not need rewriting; keep/extract beats fresh rewrite) still holds — the coupling is a one-line marker plus a build alias, not framework logic — but "framework-agnostic at the import level" and "only 3 files import anything Next-specific" are overstated and the correction touches ~90 files, so it is not trivial.
  - Corrected claim: Only 3 files in src/server + core + src/lib import Next.js APIs by name, and none is a domain module: two server-action wrappers (src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3, vetrina-operations.ts:3, `refresh` from next/cache) and the /api/v1 HTTP envelope (src/server/api/v1.ts:2, NextResponse/NextRequest). However, ~90 files in src/server (including woo/, sync/, collections/, vetrina/operations/) and src/lib/env.ts carry `import "server-only"`, a React/Next marker that throws under plain Node unless aliased (vitest.config.ts:9-11 already stubs it for tests), all src/server/actions/*.ts use the Next-only "use server" directive, and src/server/actions/catalog.ts:8 and vetrina-prices.ts:5 import from "@/components". core/ is fully framework-free. The domain coupling is therefore thin and mechanical (a one-line marker resolvable with a bundler alias or a sed pass, not framework logic), so exposing operations/woo/sync/collections to an MCP/tool layer needs a build-time shim or marker removal but no domain rewrite — still a strong argument for keep or extract over a fresh rewrite.
