- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The factual part holds: `grep 'from "next'` over src/server, core, src/lib hits only src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:3, src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3 (next/cache refresh, used at :113 / :48) and src/server/api/v1.ts:2 (NextRequest/NextResponse in `serve()` at :57). No cookies/headers/redirect/revalidate calls in those trees.

The implication ("operations/woo/sync/collections are already framework-agnostic at the import level; exposing them to an MCP/tool layer needs no domain changes") does not follow, because the grep pattern missed the dominant Next coupling:

1. `import "server-only"` is on line 1 of ~110 modules across src/server/** and src/lib/env.ts — including every domain module the claim names: src/server/woo/client.ts:1, woo/publish.ts:1, woo/apply.ts:1, sync/price-sync.ts:1, sync/ledger.ts:1, collections/service.ts:1, collections/runner.ts:1, vetrina/operations/service.ts:1, runner.ts:1, etc. `server-only` is React/Next's RSC marker package: node_modules/server-only/package.json exports `react-server -> empty.js`, `default -> index.js`, and index.js is literally `throw new Error("This module cannot be imported from a Client Component module...")`. Any plain Node process (an MCP stdio server, a CLI, a worker) that imports these modules crashes at import time unless it runs under Next's bundler, sets `--conditions react-server`, or aliases the package. The repo already proves this: vitest.config.ts:9-11 comments "Next's server-only guard throws outside a server bundle" and aliases it to src/test/server-only-stub.ts. So the modules are Next-marked by design, not framework-agnostic at the import level; the auditor missed this because `import "server-only"` does not match `from "next`.

2. A large share of the use-case orchestration lives in `"use server"` action modules rather than domain modules: src/server/actions/preview.ts (898 lines) contains planChunk (:152), fetchAndPreview (:342), previewStoreChunk (:502), previewFromStore (:661), startStoreSync (:818); src/server/actions/sync.ts holds startStorePull/getSyncState/applySyncPrices (:41, :118, :282). An LLM tool layer would need to call exactly these, and "use server" files carry Next constraints (only async exports, feeds.ts:22 comment). Importable from Node, but it is not true that the reusable surface is confined to clean domain modules.

3. Minor: `@/` and `@core` path aliases (tsconfig.json:27-28, vitest.config.ts:6-8) and `@/lib/env` (server-only, module-load env validation) must be resolved by whatever runtime hosts the MCP layer; cron entry points are HTTP-only (scripts/trigger-cron.sh -> /api/cron/*).

Mitigation/severity: core/ really is clean (core/core-spine.ts header; no server-only or @/ imports) and the server-only coupling is mechanically removable (alias or react-server condition, as vitest already does), so the direction "no fresh rewrite needed" survives. But the severity "strength: already framework-agnostic at the import level" is overstated; a competent engineer would classify it as "cleanly layered but Next-marked throughout; needs a small, known workaround plus extraction of orchestration from actions/ before an MCP layer can import it".
  - Corrected claim: Only 3 files in src/server + core + src/lib import from a `next/*` package, and none is a domain module (two next/cache refresh() calls in server actions, NextRequest/NextResponse in the /api/v1 envelope). However, ~110 modules across src/server/** and src/lib/env.ts — including woo/, sync/, collections/ and vetrina/operations/ — begin with `import "server-only"`, Next's RSC guard that throws when imported outside a Next server bundle; the repo already has to alias it away in vitest.config.ts. In addition, substantial use-case orchestration (repricing preview/sync in src/server/actions/preview.ts and sync.ts) lives in "use server" action modules rather than domain modules. The code is well layered and core/ is genuinely framework-free, so a fresh rewrite is not warranted, but exposing it to an MCP/tool host requires (a) a server-only alias or react-server condition in the non-Next runtime and (b) lifting orchestration out of actions/ into plain services — a moderate, mechanical extraction, not zero work.

#### L2 [contested · minor] 87 src/server files (plus src/lib/env.ts, src/lib/chunk.ts, src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts) begin with import "server-only", which throws when loaded outside a React server bundle; the test suite already works around it with a module alias, proving the domain runs under plain Node.

- **Evidence**: grep -rln server-only src/server | wc -l = 87; src/lib/env.ts:1; vitest.config.ts resolve.alias maps "server-only" to src/test/server-only-stub.ts with comment 'Next's server-only guard throws outside a server bundle'; test.environment = "node"; 17 *.db.test.ts files exercise repos/operations under that setup.
- **Implication**: A plain-Node MCP script needs the same alias (tsx/loader or --conditions=react-server) or a 90-line mechanical removal. This is the single largest 'what would break' item and it is trivial; it is not a reason to start fresh. An MCP endpoint inside the Next process needs nothing.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Checked every cited site. Core mechanism is confirmed: vitest.config.ts:10-11 aliases "server-only" to src/test/server-only-stub.ts with the quoted comment, test.environment is "node" (line 15), 17 *.db.test.ts files exist, core/ has zero server-only references, and package.json:35 pins server-only 0.0.1. But the specific counts and file list are wrong in three places. (1) `grep -rln server-only src/server | wc -l` = 87 is inflated by two comment-only hits: src/server/feeds/ownership.ts:12 ("no server-only imports") and src/server/adapters/kicksdb/poison.ts:9 ("client.ts is server-only"); only 85 of 210 src/server .ts files actually begin with `import "server-only"`. (2) src/lib/chunk.ts does NOT import server-only — its line 1 is the comment "Client-safe (no server-only)" and the file has no import at all; the grep matched the comment. (3) The list of out-of-src/server importers omits src/i18n/server.ts:1 (`import "server-only"`, followed by `next/headers`, which is a harder coupling than the guard itself). src/lib/env.ts:1 and src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts:1 are confirmed. Implication and severity remain sound (the aliasing fix is mechanical), but the claim as stated is numerically overstated and names a file that is a counterexample, so it must be corrected.
  - Corrected claim: 85 src/server files (of 210), plus src/lib/env.ts, src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts and src/i18n/server.ts, begin with import "server-only" (src/lib/chunk.ts does not; it is explicitly client-safe, and two further src/server grep hits are comments only). The guard throws when loaded outside a React server bundle; core/ has no such imports, and the test suite already works around it with a vitest alias to src/test/server-only-stub.ts under environment "node", with 17 *.db.test.ts files exercising repos/operations that way — proving the domain runs under plain Node. A plain-Node MCP script needs the same alias or a mechanical removal across ~88 files (src/i18n/server.ts additionally pulls next/headers and needs real decoupling); an MCP endpoint inside the Next process needs nothing. Severity: minor.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Facts confirmed: `grep -rl server-only src/server | wc -l` = 87 (85 with the exact `^import "server-only"` form; the other two spell it differently); src/lib/env.ts:1, src/lib/chunk.ts, src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts carry it too (also src/proxy.ts and src/i18n/server.ts, which the auditor omitted but which are Next-edge files anyway). vitest.config.ts:11 aliases "server-only" to src/test/server-only-stub.ts (an `export {}`), test.environment is "node" (line 15), and `find src core -name '*.db.test.ts' | wc -l` = 17. The implication also holds up when attacked: I looked for a hidden heavier Next coupling that would make server-only NOT the largest break and found none. Across src/server only 3 files import a Next runtime module: src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:3 and src/server/actions/vetrina.ts:3 (`refresh` from next/cache) and src/server/api/v1.ts:2 (NextResponse/NextRequest), all in the UI/HTTP adapter layers, not in domain modules. Zero `react` imports in src/server; the 25 `headers()` hits are the repos' own private helper functions (src/server/woo/client.ts:203, src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:35), not next/headers; no auth()/session coupling in src/server; core/ has no Next or server-only references at all. Database access is plain drizzle-orm/node-postgres + pg. The proposed workarounds are valid: node_modules/server-only/package.json exports map the `react-server` condition to ./empty.js, so `node --conditions=react-server` or a tsx/vitest-style alias neutralises the guard; the only extra wrinkle is that a plain-Node entrypoint also needs tsconfig `paths` resolution for `@/*` and `@core/*` (tsconfig.json:27-31), which tsx handles. Severity "minor" and "not a reason to start fresh" are correct; an in-process MCP route under Next needs nothing. The claim and its implication stand; the only refinement is the 85-vs-87 exact-form count and the omitted path-alias requirement.
  - Corrected claim: 87 src/server files (plus src/lib/env.ts, src/lib/chunk.ts, src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts, src/proxy.ts and src/i18n/server.ts) import "server-only", which throws when loaded outside a React server bundle; the vitest suite already neutralises it with a module alias to src/test/server-only-stub.ts under environment "node", and 17 *.db.test.ts files exercise repos/operations that way, proving the domain runs under plain Node. Only 3 src/server files import Next runtime modules (two server actions using next/cache `refresh`, one API route using next/server), and none import react or next/headers, so server-only is indeed the largest "what would break" item for a plain-Node MCP entrypoint and it is trivial: an alias (or `--conditions=react-server`, which the package's exports map to an empty module) plus tsconfig path-alias resolution for `@/*`/`@core/*` via tsx. An MCP endpoint inside the Next process needs nothing. Severity: minor; not a reason to start fresh.

#### L3 [contested · minor] User-facing wording in the operations layer is resolved through one AsyncLocalStorage (words.ts) and pure TS dictionaries, so a headless caller gets English by wrapping the call in inLanguage("en", fn) exactly as /api/v1 already does; but page.ts reads UI-section dictionaries beyond the ops slice, and the default outside any request is Italian.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/operations/words.ts:22-37 (AsyncLocalStorage, defaultLocale fallback, words() = getDictionary(locale).vetrinaCatalog.ops); src/i18n/config.ts:7 defaultLocale="it"; src/i18n/dictionary.ts has no next/react imports; src/server/api/v1.ts:38-39,63 picks locale from Accept-Language and runs handler in inLanguage; proposals.ts:83,153,159 persist and replay row.locale; page.ts:90 dictionary() reads vetrina.blocks/editor/section and vetrinaCatalog.sections; 84 words() call sites in 17 domain files.
- **Implication**: Headless English output is available today with zero changes. For extraction into a package, the dictionaries (src/i18n, 29 files / 6,475 lines, of which ops.* is 663) must travel with the core or page.ts must be narrowed to a domain-owned slice; the coupling is to data files, not to Next.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Confirmed parts: src/server/vetrina/operations/words.ts:22-37 is the single AsyncLocalStorage (grep finds no other AsyncLocalStorage in src), inLanguage()/currentLanguage() fall back to defaultLocale, words() = getDictionary(locale).vetrinaCatalog.ops. src/i18n/config.ts:7 defaultLocale="it". src/i18n/dictionary.ts imports only ./config and ./dictionaries/{it,en} (pure data). src/server/api/v1.ts:38-40 picks "en" from Accept-Language and :63 runs the handler in inLanguage. proposals.ts:83 stores currentLanguage(), :153/:159 replay row.locale. page.ts:90 `dictionary = () => getDictionary(currentLanguage())` and :101,116,127,132,357 read vetrinaCatalog.sections, vetrina.blocks, vetrina.section.fields, vetrina.editor.fallbacks — UI-section slices beyond ops.

Refuting counterexamples: the wording a headless caller receives is NOT resolved only through words()/dictionaries. The operations layer imports and calls helpers that throw hardcoded Italian regardless of inLanguage: operations/page.ts:25 imports assertPins/assertSectionLimit/publishRail from src/server/vetrina/service.ts and calls them at page.ts:223,344,352,368,565; service.ts:165 "Al massimo ${max} prodotti in posizione fissa.", :171 "Da 1 a ... prodotti per sezione.", :194 "Valore non valido per ...", :210/:264/:267 "La homepage è stata modificata nel frattempo: ricarica...", :271 "Nessun campo da cambiare." operations/writes.ts:478,750 also call publishRail (stale 409 in Italian). operations/{context,margin,order,page,preview,service,standing,writes}.ts call readCatalogTrees/readTermProducts etc. from src/server/vetrina/catalog.ts, which throws :613-616 "Il collegamento al negozio non è configurato (...)" , :791-795 "Questa categoria non c'è più nel negozio.", :874-877 "Questo prodotto non è nel catalogo...". src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:61-64 hardcodes "Il sito non risponde alla Vetrina: aggiorna il plugin..." and :56/:109 passes the WordPress plugin's own message through untranslated. fixture-source.ts has ~10 more Italian literals (:319,:381,:388,:400,:535,:580,:634). Conversely src/server/api/v1.ts:46,52 and src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:51,96 hardcode English ("No such operation.", "No ${taxonomy} ${id} in the shop."), so an Italian caller gets mixed output too. So "headless English output is available today with zero changes" is false for error paths: an en caller hitting a stale section, a pin/limit limit, an unconfigured store or a missing plugin gets Italian.

Count errors in the evidence: words() appears 68 times (72 with `words(`) across 19-20 non-test files in src/server, not 84 in 17; src/i18n is 31 files / 6,579 lines (of which provider.tsx and server.ts import react/next — server.ts:2 imports next/headers; only dictionary.ts and the dictionaries are pure); ops.{it,en}.ts = 663 lines is correct. Also src/server/actions/vetrina*.ts:6-7 import getLocale from @/i18n/server (next/headers) — the Next coupling of locale resolution lives in the actions layer, which the claim omits though it is not core.

Directionally right about the mechanism (one ALS, pure dictionaries, /api/v1 wrapping, page.ts over-reach, Italian default), but "all user-facing wording ... through one AsyncLocalStorage and pure TS dictionaries" and "zero changes" are overstated by a non-trivial set of hardcoded Italian error messages on paths the operations layer actually exercises.
  - Corrected claim: Most user-facing wording in the operations layer (src/server/vetrina/operations/*, ~68 words() call sites in 19 files) is resolved through one AsyncLocalStorage (words.ts:22-37) over pure TS dictionaries (src/i18n/dictionary.ts imports only data), and /api/v1 already wraps handlers in inLanguage(en|it) from Accept-Language (v1.ts:38-40,63), so a headless caller gets English for the ops dictionary with no changes. However it is not the only channel: the operations layer calls into src/server/vetrina/service.ts (assertPins :165, assertSectionLimit :171, publishRail stale :210, :264-271), catalog.ts (readCatalogTrees :613-616, termDetail :791-795, readPlacement :874-877), wp-source.ts (:61-64 plugin_missing, plus WP's own messages passed through :56) and fixture-source.ts, all of which throw VetrinaError with hardcoded Italian text that ignores inLanguage; meanwhile src/server/api/v1.ts:46,52 and api/catalog-order.ts:51,96 hardcode English. So an English headless caller still receives Italian on stale/limit/not-configured/plugin-missing errors and an Italian caller receives English on id/SKU errors. page.ts:90,101,116,127,132,357 additionally reads UI-section dictionaries (vetrina.blocks/editor/section, vetrinaCatalog.sections) beyond the ops slice, and the default outside any request is Italian (config.ts:7). For extraction, the dictionaries (src/i18n: 31 files / 6,579 lines; ops.* = 663; provider.tsx and server.ts are the only react/next-coupled files) must travel with the core or page.ts be narrowed, and ~25 hardcoded Italian VetrinaError messages in service.ts/catalog.ts/wp-source.ts/fixture-source.ts must be moved behind words(). Severity: minor-to-moderate (localisation leak on error paths, not a framework coupling).
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Facts verified: src/server/vetrina/operations/words.ts:22-37 is a globalThis-shared AsyncLocalStorage<Locale>; currentLanguage() falls back to defaultLocale (src/i18n/config.ts:7 = "it"); words() returns getDictionary(locale).vetrinaCatalog.ops. src/i18n/dictionary.ts imports only ./config and the it/en dictionaries (no next/react). src/server/api/v1.ts:38-39 picks "en" only when Accept-Language starts with "en", and serve() at :63 runs the handler inside inLanguage(); 10 route files under src/app/api/v1 go through serve(). proposals.ts:83 persists locale: currentLanguage(), :85 stores an Italian label via words("it") alongside structured labelParams, and :153/:159 replay row.locale. page.ts:90 builds a full Dictionary and reads UI slices at :101 (vetrinaCatalog.sections.pick.lists), :127 (vetrina.blocks), :132 (vetrina.section.fields), :357 (vetrina.editor.fallbacks). Dictionary footprint: 31 files / 6,579 lines (auditor said 29/6,475 — close; difference is provider.tsx/server.ts), ops.{it,en}.ts = 663 lines. words() is used in 19 files / 68 call sites in src/server (auditor's 84/17 is in the same ballpark). The only other server-side getDictionary users are the Next server actions in src/server/actions/* (UI-facing, not core).

Implication check: (1) "Headless English available today with zero changes" — holds; /api/v1 already does it, and OperationPreview (src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:214-240) is prose strings (untouched.reason, sample.before/after, warnings, page.lines) rendered in the request's language, which an LLM consumes directly. (2) "For extraction, dictionaries must travel or page.ts must be narrowed" — holds; page.ts genuinely reaches outside the ops slice at five sites. (3) Severity minor — correct: no behavior is blocked, the fix is a data-file move or a ~5-line narrowing in page.ts.

One overstatement the auditor missed: "the coupling is to data files, not to Next" is not fully true for the very module cited. words.ts:1 is `import "server-only"`; node_modules/server-only/index.js throws unconditionally unless resolved under the `react-server` export condition, so words.ts (and 85 other files in src/server) cannot be imported from a plain Node/MCP process without aliasing — vitest.config.ts:9-11 already stubs it for exactly this reason. That is a real Next-runtime marker in the ops layer, albeit trivially removable, and it does not change the severity. Also, en-only resolution in v1.ts:39 means any non-"en" Accept-Language (e.g. "en-GB" matches, but "fr" or absent) yields Italian — a caller must send the header, so "zero changes" assumes the caller knows to do that.

Net: implication and severity stand; the "not to Next" sentence should be qualified.
  - Corrected claim: User-facing wording in the operations layer is resolved through one AsyncLocalStorage (src/server/vetrina/operations/words.ts) over pure TS dictionaries, so a headless caller gets English by wrapping the call in inLanguage("en", fn) exactly as /api/v1's serve() already does when Accept-Language starts with "en" (src/server/api/v1.ts:38-39,63); the default outside any request, or for any other Accept-Language, is Italian (src/i18n/config.ts:7). page.ts:90,101,127,132,357 reads UI-section dictionaries (vetrina.blocks/section.fields/editor.fallbacks, vetrinaCatalog.sections) beyond the ops slice. For extraction into a package the dictionaries (src/i18n, ~31 files / ~6.6k lines, of which ops.* is 663) must travel with the core or page.ts must be narrowed to a domain-owned slice. The i18n coupling itself is to data files, but words.ts:1 (and 85 other src/server files) also carries `import "server-only"`, a Next/React marker that throws when imported from a plain Node process unless aliased (as vitest.config.ts:9-11 does), so a headless host must stub or strip it. Severity: minor.

#### L4 [refuted · strength] Environment access is centralized in src/lib/env.ts (Zod-validated at module load, 15 server importers); the only stray process.env reads in production code are NODE_ENV checks and the debug heap watcher, but env.ts hard-requires DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL.

- **Evidence**: src/lib/env.ts:6-8 ('The ONLY place process.env is read'), :154-155 DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL z.url() required, :161-170 loadEnv throws on invalid; non-test process.env hits: src/server/debug/heap-watch.ts:42,44,47, src/server/scheduler.ts:843, src/server/db/client.ts:14 (all NODE_ENV/HEAP_WATCH). Redis degrades to MemoryCache on error at src/server/cache/redis.ts:27-36 but the URL must still parse.
- **Implication**: A separate service can boot the same way as the app with the same .env; configuration is not scattered. For a package, REDIS_URL should become optional or injected, a one-file change.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Confirmed parts: src/lib/env.ts:6-8 carries the "ONLY place process.env is read" comment; :154-155 `DATABASE_URL: z.url()` and `REDIS_URL: z.url()` are required; :163-170 loadEnv() throws on invalid; `env` is evaluated at module load (:173). 15 importers under src/server (plus 6 more under src/app, 21 total non-test). RedisCache degrades to MemoryCache per-operation via try/catch (src/server/cache/redis.ts:27-36 and following), while the URL still has to parse at boot. The cited NODE_ENV/HEAP_WATCH reads at src/server/debug/heap-watch.ts:42,44,47, src/server/scheduler.ts:843 and src/server/db/client.ts:14 exist.

Refuted parts: (1) "the only stray process.env reads in production code are NODE_ENV checks and the debug heap watcher" is false. src/proxy.ts:95 reads `process.env.AUTH_PROXY_SECRET` and :103 reads `process.env.VETRINA_HOST` — these are functional auth/routing config, not NODE_ENV or debug; proxy.ts:33-35 itself calls this "the one sanctioned exception" because the zod env module cannot be bundled into the Next.js proxy. env.ts:143 also acknowledges AUTH_PROXY_SECRET "is read there from process.env". Additional strays: src/instrumentation.ts:8 (`process.env.NEXT_RUNTIME`, Next-specific) and src/app/api/debug/heap/route.ts:39-40,45 (NODE_ENV/HEAP_WATCH, debug). (2) The implication "a separate service can boot the same way as the app with the same .env" is wrong as stated: src/lib/env.ts:1 is `import "server-only"`, and node_modules/server-only/index.js unconditionally throws outside a Next.js server bundle. The repo already has to alias it for vitest (vitest.config.ts:9-11, stub at src/test/server-only-stub.ts). So a non-Next service importing env.ts crashes at import unless it adds the same alias — env centralization is real, but the env module itself is framework-coupled, which matters for the layering dimension being audited. (3) Minor: importer count is 21 non-test files, 15 of them under src/server.
  - Corrected claim: Environment access is largely centralized in src/lib/env.ts (Zod-validated at module load; 21 non-test importers, 15 under src/server), which hard-requires DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL (:154-155). However, env.ts is Next-coupled: it starts with `import "server-only"` (:1), a package that throws outside a Next server bundle, so a separate service cannot import it without aliasing it (as vitest.config.ts:9-11 already must). And it is not the only production reader of process.env: src/proxy.ts:95,103 reads AUTH_PROXY_SECRET and VETRINA_HOST directly (a documented exception because env.ts cannot be bundled into the proxy), src/instrumentation.ts:8 reads NEXT_RUNTIME, and NODE_ENV/HEAP_WATCH are read in src/server/debug/heap-watch.ts:42-47, src/server/scheduler.ts:843, src/server/db/client.ts:14 and src/app/api/debug/heap/route.ts:39-45. Redis degrades to MemoryCache per-operation (src/server/cache/redis.ts:27+) but REDIS_URL must still parse at boot. For a package: make REDIS_URL optional/injected and drop or stub the server-only import.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Facts confirmed: src/lib/env.ts:6-8 comment, :154-155 DATABASE_URL/REDIS_URL z.url() required, :163-170 loadEnv throws; stray process.env hits at src/server/debug/heap-watch.ts:42,44,47, src/server/scheduler.ts:843, src/server/db/client.ts:14 (plus src/proxy.ts:95,103 and src/app/api/debug/heap/route.ts:39-45, src/instrumentation.ts:8, which the auditor omitted). Redis fallback at src/server/cache/redis.ts:27-46 confirmed.

Implication attacked: "A separate service can boot the same way as the app with the same .env" is false as stated. src/lib/env.ts:1 is `import "server-only"`; node_modules/server-only/index.js unconditionally throws "This module cannot be imported from a Client Component module" unless resolved under the `react-server` export condition (package.json exports), i.e. inside Next's server bundle. The repo itself acknowledges this: vitest.config.ts:9-11 aliases "server-only" to src/test/server-only-stub.ts "because Next's server-only guard throws outside a server bundle", and the only standalone Node entrypoint, scripts/migrate.mjs:24-28, bypasses env.ts and reads process.env.DATABASE_URL directly, duplicating the config contract rather than reusing it. Additionally env.ts:3 imports "@/lib/schedule" via the tsconfig path alias (tsconfig.json:28), so even without server-only it does not load under plain node/tsx without alias setup. `grep -rl '^import "server-only"' src/server | wc -l` = 85 of 88 such files, so the Next coupling is pervasive in exactly the layer the auditor says is portable.

So: config is not scattered (true), but the "boots the same way" consequence does not follow, and "REDIS_URL optional is a one-file change" is a correct but beside-the-point fix — the actual blocker for extracting a package/MCP host is the server-only guard and alias wiring across env.ts and the server layer. A competent engineer would grade this as a mixed finding (strength in centralization, weakness in framework coupling), not an unqualified strength for the "framework coupling" dimension.
  - Corrected claim: Environment access is centralized in src/lib/env.ts (Zod-validated at module load; the stray process.env reads are NODE_ENV/HEAP_WATCH checks plus the sanctioned AUTH_PROXY_SECRET/VETRINA_HOST reads in src/proxy.ts), and env.ts hard-requires DATABASE_URL and REDIS_URL. However, env.ts (like 85 of the src/server modules) begins with `import "server-only"`, which throws outside a Next.js server bundle, and it imports `@/lib/schedule` through a tsconfig path alias. A separate service therefore cannot boot "the same way with the same .env" by importing env.ts; the repo already works around this twice (vitest.config.ts aliases server-only to a stub; scripts/migrate.mjs re-reads process.env.DATABASE_URL by hand). Making the config layer reusable from a non-Next host is not a one-file REDIS_URL tweak: it requires removing/relocating the server-only guards and the alias, or stubbing them in every consumer. Severity: mixed (centralization is a strength; portability is a weakness).

#### L5 [contested · major] core/core-spine.ts is live (its domain types and pricing/plan functions are imported by ~20 server modules and the UI) but its port abstraction is half-realized: WooStoreAdapter/StorePort are dead, and the real WooClient does not implement the port.

- **Evidence**: Importers of @core/core-spine include catalog/repo.ts:4, vetrina/operations/margin-plan.ts:12, woo/publish.ts:3, woo/live-check.ts:1 (samePrice), sync/ledger.ts:3, actions/preview.ts:5 (buildPlan), feeds/ownership.ts:1; KicksDbSource implements SourcePort at src/server/adapters/kicksdb/client.ts:81; grep WooStoreAdapter|StorePort outside core -> none; real client is class WooClient src/server/woo/client.ts:182, getWooClient() :683 reading env directly; core/*.ts import only './' siblings; tsconfig.json paths @core/* -> ./core/*.
- **Implication**: The 'framework-agnostic core' exists for pricing/plan/collections but not for the store boundary; the Woo/WordPress capability surface (products, taxonomies, menus-via-plugin) is in src/server/woo and src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts, not behind a port. Extraction should treat src/server as the core and core/ as a library inside it, and may delete core-spine.ts:792-930.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Verified every cited location. tsconfig.json:31 maps "@core/*" -> ./core/*. core/core-spine.ts:13-14 imports only "./config"; the other core/*.ts (non-test) import only siblings. All seven cited importers are literally correct: src/server/catalog/repo.ts:4 (SourceProduct), vetrina/operations/margin-plan.ts:12, woo/publish.ts:3, woo/live-check.ts:1 (samePrice), sync/ledger.ts:3 (samePrice), actions/preview.ts:5 (buildPlan), feeds/ownership.ts:1. Importer count is actually larger than stated: 27 non-test modules under src/server plus 3 UI components (src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts, preview/PreviewWorkspace.tsx, preview/ProductGroup.tsx) = 32 non-test importers, so "~20" undercounts but the direction (live, widely used) is right. Port abstraction: core/core-spine.ts:710 SourcePort, :728 StorePort, :792 `class WooStoreAdapter implements StorePort` ending at :930 (file is 930 lines). grep for WooStoreAdapter|StorePort outside core/ across the whole repo hits only core/__tests__/woo-adapter.test.ts, core/__tests__/woo-matching.test.ts, README.md:427 and docs/catalog-centric-redesign.md (which itself says "revive WooStoreAdapter.applyPrices") -- zero references in src/, so "dead" is literally true. The private `interface WooClient` at core-spine.ts:740 is a different type from the real `export class WooClient` at src/server/woo/client.ts:182, which has no `implements` clause and none of StorePort's methods (resolveMappings/applyPrices/upsertProduct); its surface is getProductsPage, batchVariations, listCategories, createBrand, etc. The only `implements` of a core port in src is src/server/adapters/kicksdb/client.ts:81 `KicksDbSource implements SourcePort` (plus RedisCache/MemoryCache implements Cache, unrelated). getWooClient() at client.ts:683-689 builds from `env` -- a nuance: it reads the typed env object from @/lib/env (src/lib/env.ts:6 "The ONLY place process.env is read"), not process.env directly, but the point that construction is env-bound rather than injected via a port stands. One counterexample to the implication (not the claim): a store-side seam does exist in src/server -- `interface VetrinaSource` at src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24 with two implementations, wordpressSource() (wp-source.ts:131) and fixtureSource() (fixture-source.ts:448), selected by getVetrinaSource() (source.ts:58). It covers the WordPress/vetrina read-and-layout side, not the Woo product/taxonomy write boundary, so the claim that the Woo capability surface in src/server/woo is not behind a port remains accurate. Also note that deleting only :792-930 would leave the now-dead private helpers at :740-790 (WooClient/WooProduct/WooVariation interfaces, parsePrice, uniq, DEFAULT_MATCHING) and renderSkuTemplate (:774, used only by core tests); the deletable range is roughly :734-930.
  - Corrected claim: core/core-spine.ts is live (its domain types and pricing/plan functions are imported by 27 non-test server modules and 3 UI components) but its port abstraction is half-realized: StorePort and WooStoreAdapter (core-spine.ts:728, :792-930) are referenced nowhere in src/ (only in core/__tests__ and docs), and the real `class WooClient` (src/server/woo/client.ts:182) neither implements StorePort nor exposes its methods; only SourcePort is realized (KicksDbSource, src/server/adapters/kicksdb/client.ts:81). The WordPress/vetrina side does have its own seam in src/server (VetrinaSource, src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24, implemented by wordpressSource and fixtureSource), but the Woo product/taxonomy surface in src/server/woo is not behind any port; getWooClient() (:683) builds from the typed env module rather than being injected.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The facts hold: `StorePort` (core/core-spine.ts:728) and `WooStoreAdapter` (:792) are referenced only by core/__tests__/woo-adapter.test.ts and woo-matching.test.ts; `WooClient` in core-spine.ts:744 is a private structural interface, and the real `class WooClient` (src/server/woo/client.ts:182) does not `implements StorePort`; `getWooClient()` (:683) reads env. But the implication and severity do not follow, for four reasons found in the code.

1. The dead port is already known and documented, not a hidden structural gap. README.md:427 calls `WooStoreAdapter` a "skeleton", and docs/catalog-centric-redesign.md:67 lists `StorePort`+`WooStoreAdapter` in a dormant/dead-code table. Deleting ~140 lines of unused-but-tested code is a cleanup item, not a "major" architectural finding.

2. For LLM/MCP exposure the relevant seam is not a store-vendor port but a described action surface with preview/confirm/undo, and that seam already exists and is framework-agnostic. `src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41` defines `ACTIONS`, `:248 describeActions()` emits each action with title, description, undo semantics, example and JSON Schema (`z.toJSONSchema`) and its own docstring says "what an API or MCP client lists". It is served at `src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12` alongside `/proposals`, `/proposals/{id}/confirm`, `/operations/{id}/undo` (docs/api.md:1-20; README.md:415 "the ground for an assistant"). A `StorePort` abstraction would add nothing to this: the user's target is WooCommerce, not a swappable store backend.

3. "Not behind a port" overstates the coupling. The Woo capability code in src/server/vetrina/operations takes the client by injection (`live.ts:27,58,92`, `writes.ts:79,163,218,290`, `order.ts:313`) and tests substitute it (`margin.db.test.ts:85 getWooClient: () => store.client`). `getWooClient()` is called in only 12 non-test sites, all at entry points (apply.ts:591, pull.ts:118, publish.ts:410, …), i.e. composition-root style. A concrete `WooClient` type as the dependency is a normal, testable boundary; an interface would be a trivial extraction, not a prerequisite for exposure.

4. The Next.js coupling the auditor's dimension is about is absent from the core-to-Woo path: grep for `from "next"`/`next/` in src/server/vetrina/operations, src/server/woo, src/server/catalog and core/ returns nothing; the only Next-adjacent thing is the `import "server-only"` marker (32 files), a one-line compile-time guard. Real Next imports are confined to src/server/api/v1.ts:2 and src/server/actions/*.ts (Server Actions), which is exactly where they belong.

The one sound part of the implication, "treat src/server as the core and core/ as a library inside it", describes the status quo (core/ is imported via `@core/*` by src/server and has no reverse dependency), so it is not a change the port gap forces. Corrected severity: minor (dead-code cleanup).
  - Corrected claim: core/core-spine.ts is live for domain types and pricing/plan functions, while its StorePort/WooStoreAdapter (core-spine.ts:728-930) is a documented, test-only skeleton (README.md:427; docs/catalog-centric-redesign.md:67) that the real WooClient never implemented. This is a minor dead-code cleanup, not a layering blocker for LLM/MCP exposure: the store-facing capability surface is already structured as a described, schema-typed action registry with proposals, confirm and undo (src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts describeActions, /api/v1/actions, /proposals, /operations/{id}/undo), its operations take WooClient by injection and have no Next.js imports, and getWooClient() is called only at entry points. Extraction can keep src/server as the core with core/ as its library (already the dependency direction) and may delete core-spine.ts:792-930 plus its two test files; no StorePort abstraction is needed to expose the middleware to an LLM, since the target store is fixed to WooCommerce.

#### L6 [refuted · major] There are four concrete layering inversions: two domain modules import the Next 'use server' actions layer, two actions import a server module misfiled under src/components, and the UI reaches directly into server internals from 96 files rather than only through actions.

- **Evidence**: src/server/scheduler.ts:14 imports runKicksdbRefresh from @/server/actions/feeds; src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 imports advanceStoreSync/startStoreSync from @/server/actions/preview (898 lines, exports fetchAndPreview :342, previewFromStore :661, startStoreSync :818, advanceStoreSync :841); src/server/actions/catalog.ts:8 and actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5 import @/components/catalog/drawer-data (which itself imports @/server/catalog/repo, sync/ledger, pricing/store-terms); grep -rl '@/server/' src/app src/components | wc -l = 96 (actions 70, vetrina 41, api 20, woo 13, db 11, ...).
- **Implication**: Neither inversion drags Next into the domain at runtime (preview.ts and feeds.ts import no next module), but the sync/preview and feeds-refresh capabilities an LLM would need live in the actions layer and must be relocated (~1,100 lines across 2 files + 1 file move) before the middleware surface is complete. The 96 direct UI imports mean the UI depends on module internals, which constrains refactoring under option (a) but is irrelevant to (b)/(c).
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The four inversions are literally true. src/server/scheduler.ts:14 `import { runKicksdbRefresh } from "@/server/actions/feeds"`; src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 `import { advanceStoreSync, startStoreSync, type PreviewResult } from "@/server/actions/preview"`; src/server/actions/catalog.ts:8 and src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5 both `import { loadDrawerData, type DrawerData } from "@/components/catalog/drawer-data"`, and drawer-data.ts is `import "server-only"` and imports @/server/catalog/repo (l.5), @/server/sync/ledger (l.25), @/server/pricing/store-terms (l.26) etc. preview.ts is 898 lines with exports at 342/661/818/841 as stated; feeds.ts is 222 lines (1,120 total). Neither file imports any `next` module (grep empty), and both start with "use server". No other non-test domain->actions imports exist (only *.db.test.ts files). The raw count also holds: `grep -rl '@/server/' src/app src/components | wc -l` = 96, with the per-module breakdown (actions 70, vetrina 41, api 20, woo 13, db 11) matching.

The third leg is overstated and mischaracterized, which is why the claim falls. "The UI reaches directly into server internals from 96 files rather than only through actions" is not what the code shows: (1) 27 of the 96 import only @/server/actions, i.e. exactly the sanctioned path; (2) 18 of the remainder are src/app/api route handlers, which are server code, not UI; (3) 18 are React Server Component pages/layouts (src/app/(admin)/*/page.tsx, src/app/(vetrina)/**/page.tsx) where calling server repos directly is the normal Next pattern; (4) of the 29 "use client" components that import non-action server modules, 28 use `import type` only (e.g. src/components/sync/SyncWorkspace.tsx:26-30, src/components/preview/PreviewWorkspace.tsx:15,26, src/components/vetrina/HomeScreen.tsx:11), which is erased at compile time and creates no runtime coupling; the single runtime import is src/components/orders/OrdersWorkspace.tsx:12-19 pulling constants/pure helpers (CARRIERS, NEXT_STATUS, ORDER_STATUSES, formatAddress, needsWooMirror) from @/server/orders/model. So real client-UI runtime dependence on server internals is 1 file, not 96, and the "constrains refactoring under option (a)" implication is correspondingly much weaker (type-only imports only need re-exports or path updates). The 1,100-line relocation estimate for the first two inversions is fair.
  - Corrected claim: There are four concrete layering inversions: two domain modules import the Next 'use server' actions layer (src/server/scheduler.ts:14 -> actions/feeds; src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 -> actions/preview), and two actions (actions/catalog.ts:8, actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5) import a server-only module misfiled under src/components (components/catalog/drawer-data.ts). Neither drags Next into the domain at runtime, but the store-sync/preview and KicksDB-refresh capabilities live in actions/preview.ts (898 lines) and actions/feeds.ts (222 lines) and must be relocated before the middleware surface is complete. The UI's direct coupling to server internals is, however, small: of 96 files under src/app and src/components importing @/server/*, 27 import only actions, 18 are API route handlers (server code), 18 are React Server Component pages, and of the 29 client components importing non-action server modules, 28 do so with type-only imports; only src/components/orders/OrdersWorkspace.tsx imports runtime values (constants/helpers from @/server/orders/model). This is a minor constraint on refactoring under option (a), not a major one.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Facts confirmed: src/server/scheduler.ts:14 imports runKicksdbRefresh from @/server/actions/feeds; src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 imports advanceStoreSync/startStoreSync from @/server/actions/preview; src/server/actions/catalog.ts:8 and src/server/actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5 import @/components/catalog/drawer-data (which is `import "server-only"` and imports @/server/catalog/repo, sync/ledger, pricing/store-terms); grep count is 96. The implication and severity do not follow. (1) Relocation is not a precondition for an LLM/MCP surface: `grep -rn 'from "next' src/server core` shows only actions/vetrina.ts:3, actions/vetrina-operations.ts:3 (next/cache refresh) and api/v1.ts:2 — preview.ts and feeds.ts import no Next module, and "use server" is an inert string outside Next's bundler. vitest.config.ts already aliases `server-only` to src/test/server-only-stub.ts, and src/server/scheduler.db.test.ts and src/server/sync/{sync,ledger,sizes}.db.test.ts import scheduler.ts and price-sync.ts — i.e. actions/feeds.ts and actions/preview.ts already execute in plain Node. The ~1,100 lines measure file size, not refactor effort; the fix is a path move and dropping a directive. (2) The repo already treats actions files as callable services from non-UI entry points: src/app/api/cron/refresh-catalog/route.ts:3 and src/app/api/publish/route.ts:2 import them, and a bearer-token programmatic API exists (12 routes under src/app/api/v1/*, docs/api.md: "for a script, another service or an assistant") wired straight to src/server/vetrina/operations/* and src/server/api/reads with preview/confirm/undo — so a middleware surface is already partly in place, bypassing actions entirely. (3) The "96 files reach into server internals" is a miscount: of the 96, 54 are "use client" files whose only non-@/server/actions imports are `import type` (e.g. components/sync/SyncWorkspace.tsx:26-30, components/vetrina/HomeScreen.tsx:11), erased at build; of the 42 server-side files, 20 are route.ts, 17 page.tsx, 1 layout.tsx, plus DbUnavailable.tsx and call.ts — Next server entry points that are the intended consumers of server modules; the remaining two are drawer-data.ts itself and drawer-data.test.ts. The only real misplacement is that one file. A competent engineer would rate this minor housekeeping, not a major blocker. The actual Next coupling the auditor should have flagged is smaller and different: `import "server-only"` in 85 src/server files (needs the same alias/condition vitest already uses) and next/cache refresh in two vetrina actions.
  - Corrected claim: There are two minor layering inversions (src/server/scheduler.ts:14 and src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:2 import from the "use server" actions files feeds.ts and preview.ts) and one misfiled server module (src/components/catalog/drawer-data.ts, imported by actions/catalog.ts:8 and actions/vetrina-prices.ts:5). None blocks exposing the core to an LLM: preview.ts and feeds.ts import no Next module, the "use server" directive is inert outside Next, and the project already runs them in plain Node under vitest via a server-only alias; cleaning them up is a path move, not a ~1,100-line relocation. The 96 UI files importing @/server/ are 54 client components that import only actions plus type-only imports (erased at build) and 40 Next route/page/layout entry points, which is the normal App Router boundary, not a dependency on module internals. A programmatic surface already exists (/api/v1, 12 routes on src/server/vetrina/operations and src/server/api/reads, with proposals/confirm/undo). Severity: minor. The remaining Next coupling in src/server is `server-only` in 85 files (aliasable) and next/cache refresh in actions/vetrina.ts and actions/vetrina-operations.ts.

#### L7 [contested · major] Operation execution and several caches are single-process state on globalThis, so a middleware that imports the domain from a separate Node process would become a second, uncoordinated writer against the same store.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/operations/runner.ts:11-25 ('one at a time, in the order asked ... State lives on globalThis ... A restart loses the queue'), :35 g.__vetrinaOperations; 23 globalThis uses in 15 files (vetrina/catalog.ts:542,756 presence cache; words.ts:22; db/client.ts:11; cache/redis.ts:7; collections/runner.ts; scheduler.ts); docs/operations.md:86-87 and :104-107 plan the MCP endpoint 'in the same process' so actions file as via: "assistant".
- **Implication**: Option (a)/(a+MCP-in-process) is free of this problem. Option (b) as a separate service requires either the UI to call the service over HTTP (making the service the only writer) or moving the queue/caches to Postgres/Redis coordination; that is the one genuinely architectural change extraction would force.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location. src/server/vetrina/operations/runner.ts:11-25 says literally "one at a time, in the order asked ... State lives on globalThis ... A restart loses the queue"; :39 `const g = globalThis as { __vetrinaOperations?: RunnerState }` (claim says :35, off by four lines, trivial); the queue (`queued: string[]`, `chain: Promise`) is in-memory only (:27-37, :72-90), with no advisory lock / FOR UPDATE / SKIP LOCKED anywhere in runner.ts, repo.ts or collections/runner.ts (grep). Worse than the claim states: recover() (runner.ts:58-70 -> repo.ts markInterrupted :425-441) marks EVERY row with status "running"/"undoing" in Postgres as "failed" once per process start, so a second process booting would actively mark the first process's in-flight actions as interrupted. `grep globalThis src` gives exactly 23 hits in 15 files, matching the claim; the cited sites exist: catalog.ts:542 (`__vetrinaCatalogPresence`), :756 (`__vetrinaCatalogFilling`), words.ts:22, db/client.ts:11, cache/redis.ts:7, collections/runner.ts:36,95 ("One run at a time ... State lives on globalThis"), scheduler.ts:170; plus woo/publishing.ts:14 (`__storeHubPublishing` in-flight SKU set, comment "One app container per shop ... this process is the whole picture") and sync/apply-job.ts:40. docs/operations.md:86-87 says the MCP server is "a thin layer over these pieces, in the same process (so its actions are filed with `via: "assistant"`)" and :104-107 places the endpoint "in the Next app". docs/deploy.md:309 confirms the single-process assumption: "Run a single app container per shop: the syncs run inside it, so a second ...". Two of the cited evidence items are weak but do not undermine the claim: db/client.ts:11-14 only stashes the pg Pool on globalThis outside production (a dev hot-reload guard, not shared state), and cache/redis.ts:7 is a Redis client singleton — Redis itself is cross-process, so that file is not an example of single-process state. The substantive claim (operation queue, collections runner, scheduler, publishing set, presence/filling caches are process-local; the docs plan MCP in-process; a separate process importing the domain would be a second uncoordinated writer and would also trip recover()) is literally true of the code.
  - Corrected claim: Operation execution (src/server/vetrina/operations/runner.ts:19-39, in-memory queue; recover() marks every DB row still "running" as interrupted at each process start, repo.ts:425-441), the automatic-categories runner (collections/runner.ts:36,95), the scheduler (scheduler.ts:170), the live-publish in-flight set (woo/publishing.ts:14) and several caches (catalog.ts:542,756, taxonomy-cache.ts:21, store-terms.ts:480) are single-process state on globalThis (23 uses in 15 files, two of which — the dev-only pg pool stash in db/client.ts:11 and the Redis client singleton in cache/redis.ts:7 — are not really process-local state). docs/operations.md:86-87 and docs/deploy.md:309 explicitly assume one process per shop and plan the MCP endpoint in that same process. A middleware that imports the domain from a separate Node process would therefore become a second, uncoordinated writer against the same store, and its startup would mark the UI process's in-flight actions as failed.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Facts confirmed: runner.ts:19-21,39 keeps the operation queue on globalThis; scheduler.ts:168-170, collections/runner.ts:34-38,95, publishing.ts:9-14, taxonomy-cache.ts:21, catalog.ts:542,756 likewise. docs/operations.md:86-87 plans MCP "in the same process".

The implication, however, misattributes the constraint and misses existing mitigations:

1. The single-process assumption is pre-existing and documented, not something extraction would introduce. docs/deploy.md:309-310: "Run a single app container per shop: the syncs run inside it, so a second copy would run every sync twice." publishing.ts:9-10: "One app container per shop (docs/deploy.md): this process is the whole picture." The constraint is 'one process per shop writes to Woo', whatever repo the code lives in. Extracting the core into a fresh repo/package does not force a second process: the extracted core can still be hosted by the one Next app (or the one middleware host) that also serves the UI. Option (b) only hits this if someone *chooses* to deploy UI and middleware as two processes; that is a deployment choice, not a consequence of extraction. So "the one genuinely architectural change extraction would force" is wrong on both counts (not forced; not the only one — e.g. the `server-only` imports and AsyncLocalStorage locale in words.ts:22 must also be handled).

2. Option (a) is not "free of this problem" either: any multi-instance deployment of the Next app (two replicas, blue/green overlap) triggers it, and the second process actively damages the first: recover() (runner.ts:56-70) calls markInterrupted (repo.ts:424-441), which flips every row with status 'running'/'undoing' to 'failed' on startup — i.e. a second process marks the first process's in-flight action as interrupted while it is still writing. This is the same hazard regardless of (a)/(b).

3. The auditor missed that part of the coordination already lives in Postgres and the store, so a second process is not fully "uncoordinated": proposals are claimed by a conditional UPDATE (proposals.ts:142-148, status open→confirming, "two at once cannot both run it"); confirm re-digests the preview and refuses with 409 stale if the shop changed (proposals.ts:155-161); status transitions use conditional moveStatus (repo.ts:210-224); rail writes carry expected_modified_gmt / expectedAttrsHash preconditions to the store (writes.ts:479-482, 751-753; wp-source.ts:159,181,200); undo refuses values changed since (writes.ts:720-749). What is purely in-process is the ordering of product-level writes (visibility/categories in writes.ts:117-135, 225-238, read-again-then-write with no precondition) plus the scheduler/collections runs.

Net: the factual observation stands and matters, but the correct framing is "the system is designed for exactly one writer process per shop; whichever option is chosen, the middleware must run inside that process (or that process must become the only writer), and multi-instance deployment is unsafe today because recover() kills peers' in-flight work." That applies equally to (a) and (b) and is not a differentiator for the keep-vs-extract decision. Severity 'major' is defensible only for the scenario of deliberately running two processes; as a point against extraction it is overstated.
  - Corrected claim: The operation queue, scheduler, collections runner and several caches are single-process state on globalThis, and the codebase already requires exactly one app container per shop (docs/deploy.md:309, publishing.ts:9-10). Any deployment that runs two Node processes against the same shop — two replicas of the Next app, or a separately deployed middleware beside the UI — would be a second uncoordinated writer for product-level writes, and worse, recover()/markInterrupted (runner.ts:56-70, repo.ts:424-441) in the newcomer marks the other process's in-flight actions as failed. Proposal claiming, status transitions and rail writes are already DB/store-coordinated (proposals.ts:142-161, repo.ts:210-224, writes.ts:479-482), so the gap is the in-process queue ordering, not all writes. This constraint is independent of whether the core stays in this repo or is extracted: extraction does not force a separate process, and an extracted core hosted in the single writer process (or an MCP endpoint in the same process, as docs/operations.md:86 plans) avoids it. It only becomes an architectural change if the team chooses a separate middleware service, in which case either the UI must call the service or the queue must move to Postgres/Redis coordination. Severity: moderate as a deployment constraint to document; not a reason to prefer (a) over (b).


<a id="wordpress"></a>
## WordPress (non-WooCommerce) integration surface

### Summary

The Hub talks to the WordPress host over exactly two outbound HTTP surfaces, both authenticated with the same WooCommerce consumer key/secret as HTTP Basic: the `wc/v3` WooCommerce REST client (src/server/woo/client.ts, 695 lines, ~22 public methods over 12 distinct resource paths) and the bespoke `wc-gh/v1` namespace of the golden-hive-blocks plugin (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts, 211 lines, 7 routes). There is zero use of the core WordPress REST API: no `wp/v2/*` call exists anywhere in src, docs or e2e (menus, pages, posts, media, settings, users, revisions all absent), no WP-CLI, no SSH, no Application Passwords. Media is never uploaded by the Hub: product photos are passed as `{src, name}` URLs in `PUT wc/v3/products/{id}` and WordPress sideloads them server-side (media-plan.ts:116, media.ts:360). Every WordPress-page capability (homepage blocks, sections, revision history) exists only through the custom plugin; the Hub reads page revisions via `wc-gh/v1/homepage/history`, not `wp/v2`. There is a real capabilities handshake (`GET wc-gh/v1/capabilities` -> features[], fields{}, version, api) consumed through `siteFeatures()`/`requirePower()` with a 5-minute cache, gating 5 section features and field writes; a missing plugin is detected via `rest_no_route` and surfaces as `plugin_missing`. The `api` integer is parsed but never compared, and version gating is by feature strings, not semver. The WP client is not generic: `WooClient.apiUrl` hard-codes `/wp-json/wc/v3` and `wp-source.ts` hard-codes `/wp-json/wc-gh/v1`; the shared piece is only the retry/timeout `requestJson` adapter (src/server/adapters/http.ts). Adding "edit the navigation menu" would require a third client module, a decision on auth (Woo keys authenticate only `wc-*` namespaces, so `wp/v2` would need an Application Password or a plugin proxy route), and the project's own decision record (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121,129,133) has explicitly placed navigation menus, media hygiene and WP-CLI cleanup in the plugin or in WP admin, not in the Hub. The VetrinaSource interface (source.ts:24-38) is a clean port with a fixture implementation, which is a sound pattern to replicate for further WP adapters, but today it models one page's blocks, not WordPress content generally. For a WordPress-wide middleware, the missing pieces are: a `wp/v2` client with a separate credential, menu/page/post/media/settings adapters, a generic capabilities/version negotiation beyond rail features, and a decision on whether writes go through core REST or through more plugin routes.

### Inventory

## Outbound HTTP integrations touching the WordPress host

| Module | Namespace | Auth | Transport | Lines |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `src/server/woo/client.ts` | `/wp-json/wc/v3` (hard-coded at `apiUrl`, line 192) | HTTP Basic `consumerKey:consumerSecret` (lines 204-206) | `requestJson`/`requestJsonWithHeaders` from `src/server/adapters/http.ts`; cache-buster `_hub` query param (line 199) | 695 |
| `src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts` | `/wp-json/wc-gh/v1` (hard-coded at `apiRoot`, lines 29-33) | same WOO_* keys, HTTP Basic (lines 35-38); relies on Woo authenticating `wc-*` namespaces (comment lines 19-22) | `requestJson`, GET retried x3 / writes once (lines 24-27) | 211 |

Other `requestJson` callers are not WordPress: `adapters/kicksdb/client.ts`, `feeds/goldensneakers.ts`, `feeds/ownership.ts`, `scheduler.ts` (heartbeats), `lib/sign-in-page.ts`.

Env (src/lib/env.ts:44-46,53): `WOO_BASE_URL`, `WOO_CONSUMER_KEY`, `WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET`, `HUB_API_TOKEN`. No WP-specific credential (no `WP_USER`, `WP_APP_PASSWORD`, no SSH key).

## wc/v3 endpoints used by WooClient (client.ts)

| Method(s) | Path | Lines | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `products` (type=variable, status=publish, paged) | 219-253 | `X-WP-Total` header for totals |
| GET | `products/{id}/variations` (paged, `_fields`) | 255-300 | `VARIATIONS_PER_PAGE` |
| GET | `orders` (paged) | 306-327 | read-only; no order write exists (grep `orders/` → only this) |
| GET | `products/{id}` | 329-341, 648-665 | full product / typed product |
| GET | `settings/products/woocommerce_default_catalog_orderby` | 343-356 | the only "settings" read; Woo-scoped |
| GET | `products/attributes` | 358-376 | |
| GET/POST | `products/brands` | 379-385 | 404 → `[]` when taxonomy absent (lines 447-449) |
| GET/POST | `products/categories` | 388-398 | |
| GET/POST | `products/tags` | 400-405 | |
| GET/POST | `products/attributes/{id}/terms` | 409-418 | |
| POST | `products/attributes` | 421-427 | creates `pa_*` taxonomy |
| POST | `products/{id}/variations/batch` | 485-517 | per-parent, never global |
| GET | `products` index page (`_fields`, sku, status=any) | 523-562, 616-627 | |
| POST | `products/batch` (≤100) | 569-599 | |
| POST / PUT / DELETE | `products`, `products/{id}` | 601-675 | `updateProductOnce` is the media attach path |

Pagination: manual page loop (`listTerms` caps at 20 pages x 100, lines 436-452); `X-WP-Total`/`X-WP-TotalPages` read for products/orders. Batching: `products/batch` and per-parent `variations/batch` only. Retry policies: `DEFAULT_RETRY` (4 tries), `WRITE_ONCE`, `IDEMPOTENT_WRITE` (line 180).

Taxonomy cache: `src/server/woo/taxonomy-cache.ts` (75 lines) is an in-process `Proxy` over `WooClient` memoizing `getAttributeTaxonomies/listBrands/listCategories/listTags/listAttributeTerms` for 5 min and invalidating on the matching `create*` (lines 45-73). Woo-only; no WP taxonomy (post categories, nav_menu) is cached or read.

Site guard: `src/server/woo/site-guard.ts` compares the snapshot's `site_url` against `WOO_BASE_URL` (`assertSnapshotIsThisStore`, lines 24-32); protects against writing one shop's DB into another, not a WP-level handshake.

`getWooClient()` callers (non-test): actions/duplicates.ts, actions/store-edit.ts, collections/store.ts, orders/pull.ts, woo/apply.ts, woo/media.ts, woo/publish.ts, woo/pull.ts (2), woo/rebuild.ts, woo/repair.ts — 11 call sites.

## wc-gh/v1 routes used (wp-source.ts) — the ONLY WordPress-page surface

| Method | Route | wp-source.ts lines | What it does (per docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:520-531 and fixtures) |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `capabilities` | 134-136 | plugin, version, `api`, `features[]`, per-block `fields{type,max,options}`, `fallbacks`, `max_ids`, `hide_out_of_stock`, `front_page_id`, `site_url` (fixture `__fixtures__/wp-capabilities.json`, 12 features listed) |
| GET | `homepage?page_id` | 137-139 | parses the front page's Gutenberg blocks: rails (`golden-hive/shortcode-wrapper` with parsed `[gh_product_rail]` shortcode, attrs_hash, terms, pin/exclude/fallback) and other blocks as summaries (`core/html`, `golden-hive/hero-carousel`, `core/shortcode`, …) with `modified_gmt` (fixture `wp-homepage.json`) |
| GET | `rail?key&offset&count&fallback` | 140-146 | members of one rail in effective order + cards (fixture `wp-rail.json`) |
| GET | `products?ids=` (≤100) | 147-150 | product cards by id |
| POST | `homepage/block` | 151-187 | optimistic-concurrency write of one block's allow-listed attrs (`expected_modified_gmt`, `expected_attrs_hash`, `dry_run`) |
| POST | `homepage/layout` | 188-206 | insert / remove / arrange sections (plugin ≥ 5.15.0) |
| GET | `homepage/history?key` | 207-209 | the block's attributes across WordPress revisions (the Hub never calls `wp/v2/.../revisions`) |

Page selection: `hubConfig.vetrina.page` is `"front"` or `{id}` (src/config/schema.ts:106; hub.config.ts:20) — one page, not arbitrary pages.

Error mapping (wp-source.ts:46-72): `rest_no_route`/bare 404 → `plugin_missing` (message demands plugin ≥ 5.10.0); 401/403 → `unauthorized`; 409 → `stale`; 400/422 → `invalid`.

## Capabilities handshake

- Parser: `src/server/vetrina/schemas.ts:311-345` (`version: string`, `api: int`, `features[] .catch([])`, `fields .catch({})`).
- Consumption: `src/server/vetrina/powers.ts:59-78` (`siteFeatures()`, 5-min globalThis cache; `rememberSiteFeatures` fed by the homepage read) and `requirePower()` (lines 46-57) → `plugin_outdated` (needs 5.15.0).
- Feature strings gated: `src/lib/vetrina/sections.ts:12-18` — `rail-source`, `rail-hidden`, `layout-insert`, `layout-remove`, `layout-arrange`.
- Gating sites: operations/page.ts:146,172,217,251,289,348; layout.ts:199,254,303,352,379; service.ts:128,215.
- Field writes gated by presence of `fields` in capabilities (service.ts:223-226: "A size is only written by a plugin that reports fields (≥ 5.10.0)").
- NOT done: `api` integer is parsed (schemas.ts:339) but never compared anywhere (grep `api ===|api <|api >` → no hits); no semver comparison of `version`; no startup probe — capabilities are fetched lazily on first use.

## Searches that returned nothing (src, docs, e2e)

- `wp/v2`, `wp-json/wp` — 0 hits.
- `nav_menu`, `wp/v2/menus` — 0 hits. All `menu` hits are UI (`SectionMenu.tsx`, dock menu) or `menu_order` (Woo product sort).
- `wp/v2/pages|posts|media|settings` — 0 hits.
- `wp-cli`/`wp cli` — only docs/hub-and-plugin.md:133 ("Nuclear cleanup | Plugin (WP-CLI) | Never from the Hub").
- `ssh` — 0 hits.
- `sideload` — publish.ts:119,127 (option names; the sideload is done by WordPress, see media below).
- `webhook` — docs only (hub-and-plugin.md, smart-collections.md); no inbound webhook route under src/app/api.
- `*.php` in repo — none; the golden-hive-blocks plugin lives outside this repo (hub-and-plugin.md:4 points at `hive-commerce`).
- docker-compose.yml: postgres + redis only (lines 8, 24); e2e runs with `VETRINA_SOURCE=fixture` (playwright.config.ts:53) — no WordPress in the test stack.

## Media

- `src/server/woo/media.ts` (601 lines): a Postgres-backed photo queue; the write is `client.updateProductOnce(productId, plan.body)` (line 360) with body `images: [{src, name}]` built in `media-plan.ts:67,116`. WordPress downloads and thumbnails the URL inside the request (media.ts:16-18). `MediaClient` type (line 51) needs only `findProductsBySku|getProduct|updateProductOnce|getAllVariations`.
- No `wp/v2/media` upload, no multipart, no attachment metadata reads. Refusals detected by Woo error codes `woocommerce_product_image_upload_error|woocommerce_product_invalid_image_id` (media.ts:276-283).

## Where the repo has decided WP features live (docs/hub-and-plugin.md)

| Feature | Decision | Line |
|---|---|---|
| Navigation menus | Plugin / WP admin ("WordPress's menu editor already does it") | 121 |
| Media usage index, cleanup | Plugin (needs library internals) | 129 |
| Email, transactional, order metabox | Plugin (`wp_mail`, hooks) | 130-131 |
| Nuclear cleanup | Plugin (WP-CLI), never from Hub | 133 |
| Homepage sections/blocks | Plugin renders, Hub edits via plugin REST | 32, 156 |
| Products/prices/stock/categories | Hub via `wc/v3` | 155 |
| Full product form, descriptions, SEO | WordPress editor | 35, 117 |
| Store→Hub events | Woo webhooks (planned, not built) | 33, 158, 175 |

## Programmatic exposure of the WP surface today

- `GET /api/v1/homepage` (src/app/api/v1/homepage/route.ts) → `readHome()` → `getVetrinaSource()`; the 9 homepage operations in the registry (`sectionSwitch/Move/sectionsArrange/sectionAdd/Remove/Source/Edit/blockEdit/pin`, registry.ts:112-218) are the only WP-page writes reachable via `/api/v1/proposals`.
- `VetrinaSource` port (source.ts:24-38): 8 methods, two implementations (`wp-source.ts`, `fixture-source.ts`), selected by `VETRINA_SOURCE`/`hubConfig.vetrina.source`.

### Claims and verdicts

#### WP-1 [contested · blocker] The Hub makes no call to the core WordPress REST API (wp/v2) and has no WordPress-level credential; its only WordPress-page surface is the bespoke wc-gh/v1 plugin namespace.

- **Evidence**: grep -rniE 'wp/v2|wp-json/wp|nav_menu|wp-cli|ssh|application password' over src, docs, e2e returns 0 code hits; src/lib/env.ts:44-46 defines only WOO_BASE_URL/WOO_CONSUMER_KEY/WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET; src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-33 hard-codes /wp-json/wc-gh/v1 and lines 35-38 reuse the Woo keys; src/server/woo/client.ts:192 hard-codes /wp-json/wc/v3.
- **Implication**: Any customer need that mixes WP features (navigation menus, arbitrary pages/posts, media library, site settings) with Woo has no adapter to call today; exposing the current core as middleware exposes a Woo-plus-one-homepage surface, not a WordPress surface. Neutral for keep/extract/fresh: the gap is identical in all three options and is additive work, but it means the 'spec' this codebase provides covers the Woo half only.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location and searched for counterexamples. src/lib/env.ts:43-45 declares only WOO_BASE_URL, WOO_CONSUMER_KEY, WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET (plus CRON_SECRET at :48, unrelated to WP); a grep for env.(WOO|WP|WORDPRESS)* across src finds only the three WOO_* names, so there is no WordPress-level credential (no application password, no WP user). src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-33 builds `${origin}/wp-json/wc-gh/v1` and :35-38 base64-encodes the Woo consumer key/secret as Basic auth; all its endpoints are homepage, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history (:137-208), parameterised by hubConfig.vetrina.page (:40-43), i.e. one configured page. src/server/woo/client.ts:190-192 hard-codes `${base}/wp-json/wc/v3`. A repo-wide grep (excluding node_modules/.next) for `wp/v2`, `wp-json/wp`, `nav_menu`, `wp-cli`, `application password` returns zero code hits; the only hit is docs/hub-and-plugin.md:133 ("Nuclear cleanup | Plugin (WP-CLI) | Never from the Hub"), a doc row that confirms rather than refutes. Enumerating every `wp-json/<ns>/v<N>` string in the repo yields only wc/v3 (client.ts, tests, .env.example) and wc-gh/v1 (wp-source.ts). docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 explicitly assigns "Navigation menus" to the plugin/WordPress menu editor and :129-131 assigns media library internals and wp_mail to the plugin, so the absence of a WP-core adapter is by design, not oversight. No .php lives in the repo, so the wc-gh plugin itself is external. Minor precision only: the wc-gh/v1 surface targets one configured page (front or page_id) with block/layout/history (revision) operations, so "one-homepage surface" is slightly narrow but not wrong.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub makes no call to the core WordPress REST API (wp/v2) and has no WordPress-level credential (only WOO_BASE_URL/WOO_CONSUMER_KEY/WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET, src/lib/env.ts:43-45); its only WordPress-page surface is the bespoke wc-gh/v1 plugin namespace (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32), authenticated with the Woo consumer keys and scoped to a single configured page (homepage/block/layout/history). docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121-133 deliberately assigns navigation menus, media-library internals, email and WP-CLI cleanup to the plugin side, never to the Hub.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The facts hold (src/lib/env.ts:44-46 only WOO_* creds; src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-38 hard-codes /wp-json/wc-gh/v1 with the Woo keys; src/server/woo/client.ts:192 hard-codes /wp-json/wc/v3; no wp/v2 call anywhere). The implication and the "blocker" severity do not follow.

1. The absence is a recorded design decision, not an unnoticed gap. docs/hub-and-plugin.md is a decision record whose rule (line 21: "One writer of product data: the Hub ... WordPress stays the store") and placement table explicitly put WP-level features outside the Hub: line 121 "Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it"; line 129 media usage index/cleanup -> Plugin ("Needs the library's internals"); lines 130-131 email -> Plugin; line 31 anything that "must run inside a WordPress request or hook ... the media library's internals, the theme" -> Plugin; lines 35-36 "People keep editing in WordPress whatever the Hub does not own: descriptions, SEO, extra photos, sale prices ..., menus." Section 5 (lines 152-160) then specifies how the two halves meet. So the codebase-as-spec does cover the WordPress half: it states where it lives and the contract between them. A competent engineer reading this would not call the missing wp/v2 adapter a blocker for exposing the Hub's core; they would call it out-of-scope by design, re-openable if the middleware's scope is widened.

2. The auditor's own text undermines the severity: "Neutral for keep/extract/fresh: the gap is identical in all three options and is additive work." A gap that is identical in every option and purely additive cannot be a blocker for the keep/extract/fresh decision, which is the question being audited.

3. Mitigation missed: the code already has everything a wp/v2 adapter needs except two env vars. src/server/adapters/http.ts provides a generic requestJson with RetryPolicy; wp-source.ts:29-33 already derives the site origin by stripping /wp-json (reusable for `${origin}/wp-json/wp/v2`), lines 35-38 build Basic auth headers, and wpError (lines 46-70) parses WordPress's generic error envelope ({code,message,data.status}, "rest_no_route"), which is WP-core behaviour, not plugin-specific. Adding WP_APP_USER/WP_APP_PASSWORD (Application Passwords) to env.ts and a sibling source file is a small, well-patterned change; the comment at wp-source.ts:19-22 even documents why the Woo keys cannot cover non-wc namespaces, showing the authors understood the boundary.

4. "Woo-plus-one-homepage surface" understates the plugin surface: wp-source.ts:42,156,178,199 pass page_id, so the wc-gh/v1 routes edit any page carrying the plugin's blocks, not only the front page (src/config/schema.ts:107 "the site's static front page, or a page id"); writes produce WordPress revisions (docs/operations.md:17); a capabilities endpoint (wp-source.ts:134-135) negotiates the surface. And the user's own example ("change the navigation menu with these product categories") is partly covered today: product categories are Hub-owned (automatic categories, taxonomy rules, menu_order via /api/v1/catalog/order, docs/api.md:54-60; docs/smart-collections.md:43 "category page, menus, homepage rail" follow the taxonomy). Only the menu structure itself (nav_menu items) is missing.

Correct reading: a real but scoped, additive, low-to-medium gap, not a blocker; severity should be "minor / scope note", and it says nothing about keep vs extract vs fresh.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub makes no call to the core WordPress REST API (wp/v2) and holds no WordPress-level credential; its only WordPress-page surface is the wc-gh/v1 plugin namespace (page-generic via page_id, not just the homepage). This is a documented scoping decision (docs/hub-and-plugin.md: menus, media library and email are assigned to WordPress/the plugin; the Hub is the single writer of product data), not an architectural defect. Exposing the Hub as LLM middleware therefore exposes a product/catalog/storefront-blocks surface; mixed needs such as editing nav_menu items or arbitrary posts/media would require an additive wp/v2 adapter (Application Password env vars plus a sibling of wp-source.ts reusing adapters/http.ts and the existing WP error mapping). Severity: minor/scope note; neutral for keep/extract/fresh.

#### WP-2 [contested · major] Authentication is a single WooCommerce consumer key/secret used for both wc/v3 and wc-gh/v1; a wp/v2 adapter would need a second credential type or a plugin proxy, because Woo REST keys authenticate only wc-* namespaces.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:19-22 comment: 'WooCommerce authenticates "wc-*" namespaces with its REST API keys, so this uses the WOO_* credentials'; headers() at lines 35-38 and client.ts:204-206 both build Basic from the same keys; docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:520-522 'All endpoints use Woo-key auth and require manage_woocommerce'.
- **Implication**: A WordPress-wide middleware must add an Application Password (or JWT) credential path and the config/secret handling around it, or route every WP write through new plugin endpoints. This is an architectural fork to decide before adding menu/page capabilities, whichever repo strategy is chosen.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): All cited evidence is literally true. src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:19-21 carries the comment 'WooCommerce authenticates "wc-*" namespaces with its REST API keys, so this uses the WOO_* credentials'; wp-source.ts:35-38 builds `Basic base64(WOO_CONSUMER_KEY:WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET)` for the wc-gh/v1 root (apiRoot at :30-33); src/server/woo/client.ts:191-192 roots at /wp-json/wc/v3 and :204-212 builds the same Basic header from consumerKey/consumerSecret; docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:522 says 'All endpoints use Woo-key auth and require manage_woocommerce'. Counterexample search: grep across src/docs/.env.example for wp/v2, application password, WP_APP, JWT, WP_USER/WP_PASS returns nothing (only a package-lock hash). src/lib/env.ts:41-43 declares only WOO_BASE_URL/WOO_CONSUMER_KEY/WOO_CONSUMER_SECRET as WordPress-side credentials; .env.example repeats that the Vetrina is 'authenticated with the WOO_* keys above'. Every Authorization header in src (wp-source.ts:37, client.ts:206) that targets WordPress uses the Woo key pair; the other Authorization sites (goldensneakers.ts:39, kicksdb client.ts:94, api/v1.ts, cron routes) are for non-WordPress services or the Hub's own inbound API. The only WordPress REST namespaces touched anywhere in src are wc/v3 and wc-gh/v1. The claim's premise about WooCommerce REST-key auth covering only wc-* namespaces is also what the repo itself asserts (wp-source.ts:19-21) and matches WooCommerce's WC_REST_Authentication behaviour (it only hooks requests whose route is under a wc/ or wc-* namespace). Nothing in the repo contradicts the claim; no wp/v2 adapter, Application Password or JWT path exists.
  - Corrected claim: Authentication is a single WooCommerce consumer key/secret used for both wc/v3 and wc-gh/v1; a wp/v2 adapter would need a second credential type or a plugin proxy, because Woo REST keys authenticate only wc-* namespaces.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Facts confirmed: src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:18-22 (comment) and :35-38 (headers) plus src/server/woo/client.ts:204-206 both build Basic auth from WOO_CONSUMER_KEY/SECRET; docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:522 says wc-gh/v1 uses Woo-key auth + manage_woocommerce. Default WooCommerce does authenticate API keys only for wc/ and wc- namespaces.

The implication ("an architectural fork to decide before adding menu/page capabilities", severity major) is wrong, for three reasons visible in the repo:

1. The fork is already decided and documented. docs/hub-and-plugin.md is a decision record: §1 (lines 19-32) says anything that "must run inside a WordPress request or hook ... the theme" belongs in the plugin, and storefront presentation is "rendered by the plugin, edited by the Hub through the plugin's REST endpoints — exactly how the Vetrina and golden-hive-blocks work today"; §5 line 156 fixes "Hub → storefront: the plugin's REST endpoints (wc-gh/v1, as today)"; line 121 explicitly places "Navigation menus → Plugin". So the "plugin proxy" branch of the auditor's dichotomy is not an open decision but the established, production pattern (wc-gh/v1 homepage/layout/history endpoints already do page writes with 409 stale checks, revisions, allowlists — docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:520-540). Adding menu endpoints to golden-hive-blocks is incremental, not a fork.

2. The "second credential type" branch is not forced even if wp/v2 were wanted: WooCommerce exposes the `woocommerce_rest_is_request_to_rest_api` filter, so the plugin the Hub already requires (version-gated via /capabilities, wp-source.ts:60-66) can extend Woo-key Basic auth to wp/v2 in one line. The auditor missed this mitigation.

3. Even the Application Password path would be trivial, not architectural: WP app passwords use the same `Authorization: Basic` header over the same generic `requestJson` adapter (src/server/adapters/http.ts:50-60), and src/lib/env.ts already has the optional-secret pattern (lines 15-18, 42-46) for one more env pair. That is configuration plumbing, comparable to adding HUB_API_TOKEN or CRON_SECRET.

A competent engineer reading docs/hub-and-plugin.md would not call this a blocking architectural fork. Severity should be minor/informational: a note that WP-wide operations route through plugin endpoints (or a one-line auth filter), consistent with the existing decision record.
  - Corrected claim: Authentication is a single WooCommerce consumer key/secret used for both wc/v3 and wc-gh/v1 (wp-source.ts:35-38, client.ts:204-206), and by default Woo keys authenticate only wc-* namespaces. This is not an open architectural fork: docs/hub-and-plugin.md already decides that WordPress-side features (navigation menus, media, email, theme) live in the golden-hive-blocks plugin and are reached by the Hub through wc-gh/v1 endpoints with the existing Woo-key auth, exactly as the Vetrina page writes work today. Menu/page capabilities for an LLM-driven middleware therefore follow the existing pattern (new plugin endpoints under wc-gh/v1), or, if raw wp/v2 access is ever wanted, the plugin can extend Woo-key auth via the woocommerce_rest_is_request_to_rest_api filter or the Hub can add an optional Application Password env pair over the same Basic-auth adapter. Severity: minor (configuration/plumbing), not major.

#### WP-3 [refuted · strength] There is a working capabilities handshake with the plugin (features[], fields{}, version, api) that gates 5 section features and field writes, but the api integer is never compared and version is never semver-checked; a missing plugin is detected only by rest_no_route at call time.
