- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/schemas.ts:311-345 parses version/api/features/fields; src/server/vetrina/powers.ts:31-57 (sectionPowers, requirePower) and :59-78 (siteFeatures with 5-min cache); src/lib/vetrina/sections.ts:12-18 lists the 5 feature strings; gating call sites at operations/page.ts:146,172,217,251,289 and layout.ts:199,303,352,379; grep 'api ===|api <|api >' in src/server/vetrina and src/lib/vetrina returns nothing; wp-source.ts:60-66 maps rest_no_route to plugin_missing.
- **Implication**: Strength for middleware: the feature-flag pattern already lets tools advertise what the connected site can do and refuse cleanly, and is extensible to new feature strings. Weakness: no contract-version negotiation, so a plugin API bump is not detected. The pattern is worth preserving (keep or extract); a fresh repo would need to rebuild it.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): Confirmed parts: src/server/vetrina/schemas.ts:311-347 parses version (string), api (int), features[], fields{} (fields/features default via .catch). src/server/vetrina/powers.ts:31-57 sectionPowers/requirePower gate on siteCan(); src/lib/vetrina/sections.ts:12-18 lists exactly 5 feature strings (rail-source, rail-hidden, layout-insert, layout-remove, layout-arrange); powers.ts:59-78 caches features for 5 min. Call sites exist at operations/page.ts:146,172,217,251,289 and layout.ts:199,303,352,379 (plus layout.ts:254-255,269, page.ts:348-349, service.ts:215-221 which the evidence omitted). `grep -rnE "\.api\b|api ===|api <|api >|semver|\.version\b"` over src/server/vetrina, src/lib/vetrina, src/app finds only schemas.ts:338-339 (assignment) and vetrina.test.ts:69 — api and version are never compared anywhere; the version strings "5.10.0"/"5.15.0" appear only in user-facing messages (powers.ts:53, wp-source.ts:63). wp-source.ts:60-66 maps rest_no_route (and a bare 404 with empty code) to plugin_missing; no other plugin-presence probe exists.

Refuting part: the claim says the handshake "gates ... field writes" via fields{}. It does not. Capabilities.fields is parsed (schemas.ts:346, types.ts:264) but its only consumer is a test assertion (src/server/vetrina/vetrina.test.ts:71); `grep -rn "\.fields" src | grep -i capabilit` returns nothing else. Field writes are gated by a hard-coded mirror FIELD_SPECS in src/lib/vetrina/fields.ts:9-24 ("mirrored here ... keep the two lists in step"), the hub config (blockConfig(...).edit.fields, page.ts:342-349, service.ts:181-220, layout.ts:211-212), and whether the field is present in the block the site returned (fields.ts:36-47 editableFields/switchable). So the field contract is a duplicated static table, not negotiated — which weakens the "tools can advertise what the connected site can do" implication for fields. Minor overstatement too: features are primarily taken from the /homepage read (schemas.ts:150,180; service.ts:106-109 rememberSiteFeatures(home.features)), with GET /capabilities only the fallback refresh (powers.ts:75). The REST namespace wc-gh/v1 (wp-source.ts:32) is the only version-ish token and it is a fixed URL, not negotiated.
  - Corrected claim: There is a working capabilities handshake with the plugin (version, api, features[], fields{} parsed in schemas.ts:311-347, read from the /homepage response and refreshed via GET /capabilities with a 5-min cache) whose features[] gates the 5 section features (source/add/move/remove/switchOff) via sectionPowers/requirePower. However the handshake's fields{} is parsed but never used: field writes are gated by a hard-coded FIELD_SPECS mirror of the plugin's rules in src/lib/vetrina/fields.ts plus hub config and the fields present in the returned block, so the field contract is duplicated rather than negotiated. The api integer and version string are never compared or semver-checked (only quoted in error messages); the REST path is fixed at wc-gh/v1; and a missing plugin is detected only when a call returns rest_no_route (or a bare 404), mapped to plugin_missing in wp-source.ts:60-66.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Factual core mostly confirmed: src/server/vetrina/schemas.ts:311-345 parses version/api/features/fields; powers.ts:31-57 sectionPowers/requirePower gate on siteCan() over the 5 strings in src/lib/vetrina/sections.ts:12-18; powers.ts:59-78 caches features 5 min; wp-source.ts:60-66 maps rest_no_route/404 to plugin_missing; grep for any comparison of `.api` or semver on `version` finds only the parse (schemas.ts:315,339), the type (lib/vetrina/types.ts:254-255) and a test (vetrina.test.ts:69). One factual error: `fields{}` from capabilities does NOT gate field writes. The only consumer of Capabilities is powers.ts:75 (`.features`); field allow-listing comes from hub config `blockConfig(RAIL_BLOCK).edit.fields` (service.ts:212-218, service.ts:269, layout.ts:212,266, operations/page.ts:349). capabilities.fields is parsed and dropped. The implication is overstated on both sides. (a) The "no contract-version negotiation" weakness ignores mitigations: features[] is itself the negotiation (per-capability strings are finer than an api integer, and docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:572 defines them as the 5.15.0 contract); every plugin response goes through zod `parsed(...)` (wp-source.ts:135-146) so a shape change fails loudly; writes carry expected_modified/expected_attrs_hash and dry_run (wp-source.ts:118,160-203) so a drifted plugin yields 409/400 rather than silent corruption; the docs mandate a post-deploy capabilities + dry-run contract check (docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:785). An unused api integer between two co-developed components is a minor gap, not a middleware-readiness weakness. (b) "Worth preserving; a fresh repo would need to rebuild it" is weak: the whole pattern is ~80 lines (powers.ts + siteCan), and it is single-site by construction, a process-global cache keyed on nothing (powers.ts:60-61 `globalThis.__vetrinaSiteFeatures`) and a source bound to one WOO_BASE_URL (wp-source.ts:30-32). An LLM middleware serving more than one store would have to rewrite it anyway. (c) For the stated dimension (WordPress non-Woo surface) the handshake covers only the custom `wc-gh/v1` plugin namespace and 5 homepage-section features; it says nothing about WP core REST readiness (menus, pages). Correct framing: a sound but tiny, single-site, plugin-only feature-flag; neutral for keep-vs-extract.
  - Corrected claim: There is a working capabilities handshake with the custom wc-gh/v1 plugin: features[] (5 strings) gates the 5 homepage-section operations via sectionPowers/requirePower with a 5-minute process-global cache; version and api are parsed but never checked, and capabilities.fields is parsed but never used (field writes are gated by hub config blockConfig().edit.fields, not by the plugin). A missing plugin surfaces as plugin_missing on rest_no_route/404 at call time. Severity: neutral/minor strength. The unused api integer is a small gap, largely mitigated by zod-validated responses, expected_modified/attrs_hash optimistic concurrency, dry_run writes and the documented post-deploy capabilities+dry-run contract check. The pattern is ~80 lines, single-site (unkeyed global cache, one WOO_BASE_URL) and covers only the custom plugin, not WordPress core; it is trivial to rebuild and would need re-keying for a multi-store middleware, so it does not weigh on the keep-vs-extract decision.

#### WP-4 [confirmed · major] The WordPress-page capability is a clean port (VetrinaSource, 8 methods) with two implementations, but it models exactly one configured page's blocks and rails, not WordPress pages, posts, menus or media in general.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24-38 (interface), :58-66 (wordpress|fixture selection); src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:131-211 implements 7 routes; page selection is hubConfig.vetrina.page = 'front' | {id} (src/config/schema.ts:106, hub.config.ts:20) passed as page_id (wp-source.ts:40-43); the block allow-list is per block name in hub.config.ts:21-31.
- **Implication**: The port/fixture pattern is the right shape to add further WP adapters (e.g. a MenuSource with a fixture), so it argues for extracting/keeping rather than discarding; but the current abstraction cannot be generalised by configuration alone, it needs new ports and new plugin or wp/v2 routes.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location. src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24-38 defines `VetrinaSource` with `kind` plus 8 methods (capabilities, homepage, rail, products, writeRail, writeBlock, writeLayout, history); :58-66 `getVetrinaSource()` selects "fixture" vs "wordpress" from env/hubConfig. src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:131-211 (`wordpressSource`) hits exactly 7 distinct wc-gh/v1 routes (capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block x2, homepage/layout, homepage/history); fixture-source.ts:450-686 implements the same contract. Page selection: src/config/schema.ts:106 `page: z.union([z.literal("front"), z.object({id})])`, src/config/hub.config.ts:20 `page: "front"`, wp-source.ts:40-43 `pageParam()` maps it to `page_id`; writes carry `pageId` taken from the homepage read (service.ts:232, layout.ts:214-384), so still the one configured page. Block allow-list is keyed per block name at hub.config.ts:21-46. Counterexample search: no `wp/v2`, `nav_menu`, `/menus`, posts or pages routes anywhere in src/ or core/ (only wc/v3 in src/server/woo/client.ts:192 and the wc-gh/v1 base in wp-source.ts:30-32). Media (src/server/woo/media.ts) attaches photos through wc/v3 product `images` URLs, not wp/v2/media. docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 and :129 explicitly assign navigation menus and media-library work to the plugin, not the Hub. Minor nuance only: `products(ids)` returns Woo product cards rather than page content, so "models exactly one page's blocks and rails" is a hair narrow, but the overall claim is accurate.
  - Corrected claim: The WordPress-page capability is a clean port (VetrinaSource: `kind` plus 8 methods) with two implementations (wc-gh/v1 plugin client and in-memory fixture), but it models only one configured page's blocks and rails (plus product-card lookups for those rails), not WordPress pages, posts, menus or media in general; the repo has no wp/v2 client at all, and docs/hub-and-plugin.md deliberately leaves menus and media-library work to the plugin.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Facts confirmed: src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24-38 defines VetrinaSource with 8 members (kind + 7 methods: capabilities, homepage, rail, products, writeRail, writeBlock, writeLayout, history); :58-66 picks wordpress|fixture. src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-33 hardcodes the root to `/wp-json/wc-gh/v1` (a custom plugin namespace), and :40-43 derives page_id from hubConfig.vetrina.page (src/config/schema.ts:106; src/config/hub.config.ts:20 = "front"). Every read (homepage, rail, history) is scoped to that one page; writes take input.pageId but service.ts:232,274 only ever feeds back the pageId read from that same homepage. The block allow-list is per block name (hub.config.ts:21-47, with "*": hidden).

Attacking the implication: I looked for a mitigation the auditor missed and found none. (1) There is no generic WordPress REST client anywhere: `grep wp/v2|wp-json` across src/ and core/ hits only wp-source.ts:32 (wc-gh/v1), src/server/woo/client.ts:192 (wc/v3) and src/lib/site.ts:24 (URL normalisation). No code touches posts, menus (nav_menu), media library endpoints or wp/v2 pages; src/server/woo/media.ts is the Hub's own photo-attach job queue through wc/v3, not WP media. (2) The gap is deliberate: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:28-33 and :41 record that storefront presentation is "the plugin renders it; the Hub edits it through the plugin's REST endpoints" and that "menus" stay edited in WordPress. So the auditor's extension path (new ports + new plugin or wp/v2 routes) is exactly the repo's documented model, which confirms rather than weakens the implication. (3) An obstacle the auditor under-stated: wp-source.ts:19-22 notes the WOO_* consumer key/secret works because WooCommerce authenticates only "wc-*" namespaces. Reaching wp/v2 (menus, posts, media) would therefore need a different credential (Application Password / cookie auth) and a different auth adapter, not just new routes. WooClient.apiUrl (client.ts:190-193) technically accepts a base already containing /wp-json, but auth would still fail for wp/v2, so it is not a mitigation.

Severity: "major" is right for the stated question. The user's own motivating example (change the nav menu alongside Woo categories) has zero code support today, and the port/fixture shape is reusable but the concrete abstraction is single-page/homepage-blocks only. Not catastrophic (the architecture is extensible by design), so not blocker; more than minor since it needs new ports, new server-side routes and a new credential type. A competent engineer would reach the same conclusion from these files.
  - Corrected claim: The WordPress-page capability is a clean port (VetrinaSource: kind + 7 methods, src/server/vetrina/source.ts:24-38) with wordpress and fixture implementations, but it models exactly one configured page's blocks and rails via the plugin's custom wc-gh/v1 namespace (wp-source.ts:32, page from hubConfig.vetrina.page at :40-43), not WordPress pages, posts, menus or media in general; the repo has no wp/v2 client at all, menus are documented as staying in WordPress (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:41), and the WOO_* credentials only authenticate wc-* namespaces (wp-source.ts:19-22). The port/fixture pattern is the right shape for further WP adapters and argues for extracting/keeping, but generalising requires new ports, new plugin or wp/v2 routes, and a separate WordPress credential, not configuration changes. Severity: major.

#### WP-5 [contested · major] The Hub never uploads media; photos are URL-sideloaded by WordPress through PUT wc/v3/products/{id} with images:[{src,name}], and no media-library read or write exists.

- **Evidence**: src/server/woo/media-plan.ts:67 and :116 build {src, name}; src/server/woo/media.ts:360 calls client.updateProductOnce(productId, plan.body); media.ts:14-18 documents that WordPress downloads and thumbnails inside the request; media.ts:276-283 detects refusal via woocommerce_product_image_upload_error; grep 'wp/v2/media|multipart|FormData' → 0 hits in src/server.
- **Implication**: A middleware asked to 'upload this image to the media library' or 'replace the hero image' has no path; media needs are met only indirectly (product gallery via Woo). The project's decision record (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:129) explicitly assigns media hygiene to the plugin, so adding it to the Hub reverses a documented decision.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Every cited line checks out. src/server/woo/media-plan.ts:67 builds `{ src, name }` per photo and :116 builds the `image` body with `src`/`name` (plus optional `alt`); :121-125 restates existing gallery items by `{ id }` so Woo keeps them. src/server/woo/media.ts:14-18 documents that WordPress downloads and thumbnails inside the request; :360-362 the only write is `client.updateProductOnce(productId, plan.body)` (PUT on the product, client.ts:667); :276-283 `photoRefused` matches `woocommerce_product_image_upload_error|woocommerce_product_invalid_image_id`. The WooClient base is `${base}/wp-json/wc/v3` (client.ts:192) with no wp/v2 path anywhere. A repo-wide case-insensitive grep for `wp/v2/media|multipart|FormData|attachment|uploadMedia|/media` (excluding node_modules) finds only the Hub's own `/api/cron/media` route, docs, and the goldensneakers feed hostname — no media-library endpoint, no file upload. The second sideload path, repair-plan.ts:104 (`body.images = source.images.map((src) => ({ src }))`), is also URL-based via the product PUT. Reads: the Hub only reads image `src`/`id` as fields of the Woo product object (client.ts:90,164; store-product.ts:19), never the media library itself. docs/hub-and-plugin.md:129 assigns "Media usage index, safe cleanup, whitelist" to the Plugin, as stated. Minor precision: the Hub sends `id` references to existing attachments alongside the new `{src,name}` (media-plan.ts:125), but that is still within the product PUT and does not constitute media-library access.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub never uploads media; photos are URL-sideloaded by WordPress through PUT wc/v3/products/{id} with images:[{src,name}] (and {src} alone in repair-plan.ts:104), existing gallery items being restated by attachment id, and no media-library (wp/v2/media) read or write exists.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The factual part holds (media-plan.ts:67 and :116 build {src,name}; media.ts:360 updateProductOnce; client.ts:664-672 WRITE_ONCE because WP sideloads into a new media item per send; no wp/v2/media, multipart or FormData anywhere in src/server). The implication and severity do not hold.

1. "Adding media to the Hub reverses a documented decision" is a misreading of docs/hub-and-plugin.md. Line 128 assigns "Product photos at creation" to the Hub (the photo queue); line 129 assigns only "Media usage index, safe cleanup, whitelist" to the plugin, with the stated reason "Needs the library's internals: post content, attachment metadata". That is media *hygiene* (orphan detection and deletion, which needs post_content scanning and attachment meta), not media *upload* or *attach*. A URL-driven "attach/replace product image" tool is squarely on the Hub side of that record; a wp/v2/media POST would be additive, not a reversal. The decision record also lists Navigation menus as "Plugin" (line 123), so the auditor's framing of the media boundary as unusual is not supported either.

2. The "replace the hero image" example is not a media-library gap. The hero is a Vetrina block (golden-hive/hero-carousel, fixture-data.ts:237) edited through golden-hive-blocks' wc-gh/v1 routes (wp-source.ts:19-32), and the Hub currently exposes no editable fields for it (vetrina.test.ts:52-53: hero.fields is undefined). Uploading to the media library would not unblock that; the missing piece is a plugin route/block field, which is the plugin's documented territory.

3. The Hub's indirect path is the right shape for an LLM-driven middleware and already handles what a raw wp/v2/media upload would not. An LLM has URLs, not bytes; the queue takes URLs (media-plan.ts:67), handles WordPress's slow in-request thumbnailing asynchronously (media.ts:14-22), dedups by filed name after lost answers (media-plan.ts:62-69, 72-74), restates the gallery by id so Woo does not drop existing photos (media-plan.ts:118-122), supports replace vs. fill (job.replace, media-plan.ts:107), and distinguishes a refused download from a store outage (media.ts:270-283). Note that wp/v2/media itself has no URL-sideload; the Woo images:[{src}] path is the only bytes-free way in. A media *read* exists via product images (client.ts:41-43 parses id/src/name, used by liveOf in media.ts).

4. Severity. Nothing in the Hub's scope (catalog, taxonomy, publishing, Vetrina) needs a general media-library CRUD, and the one real constraint — the Hub authenticates only wc-* namespaces with Woo consumer keys (wp-source.ts:19-21), so any wp/v2/* call would need a separate Application Password credential — is a cross-cutting WP-auth finding, not a media finding. For the middleware/LLM-exposure question this is at most minor/informational: "no arbitrary-media-library tool, by scope; product images are attach-by-URL through the existing queue." A competent engineer would not rate the absence of a feature the decision record never assigned to the Hub as major.
  - Corrected claim: The Hub attaches product photos only by URL-sideload through PUT wc/v3/products/{id} with images:[{src,name}] (media-plan.ts:67,116; media.ts:360), and reads media only as product image id/src/name (client.ts:41-43); it has no wp/v2/media client. This is consistent with docs/hub-and-plugin.md, which keeps product photos in the Hub (line 128) and assigns only media hygiene (usage index, cleanup, whitelist) to the plugin (line 129), so a URL-based attach/replace-product-image tool is already the Hub's documented path and well suited to LLM use. A general "upload bytes to the media library" or "replace the Vetrina hero image" tool is out of the Hub's current scope (the hero block has no editable fields, vetrina.test.ts:53, and lives behind the plugin's wc-gh/v1 routes); adding one would be additive, not a reversal, and would mainly require a non-Woo credential for the wp/v2 namespace. Severity: minor / scope note, not major.

#### WP-6 [contested · major] The repository's own decision record places navigation menus, media cleanup, email and WP-CLI operations in the WordPress plugin or WP admin, explicitly out of the Hub's scope.

- **Evidence**: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 'Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it'; :129 media usage index/cleanup → Plugin; :130-131 email/metabox → Plugin; :133 'Nuclear cleanup | Plugin (WP-CLI) | Never from the Hub'; :35-36 'People keep editing in WordPress whatever the Hub does not own: descriptions, SEO, extra photos, sale prices, menus.'
- **Implication**: Turning this codebase into a WordPress-wide middleware contradicts its current decision record and 'one writer of product data' rule (hub-and-plugin.md:21); the owner must either amend that record (keep/extract) or treat it as a conscious scope change in a fresh repo. The code itself contains no partial menu/page work to build on.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Every cited line is literally present. docs/hub-and-plugin.md:21 "One writer of product data: the Hub."; :35-36 "People keep editing in WordPress whatever the Hub does not own: descriptions, SEO, extra photos, sale prices ..., menus."; :121 "Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it"; :129 "Media usage index, safe cleanup, whitelist | Plugin"; :130-131 "Email: brand, templates, campaigns | Plugin" and "Transactional emails, order metabox | Plugin"; :133 "Nuclear cleanup | Plugin (WP-CLI) | Never from the Hub". Code check for counterexamples: grep over all .ts/.tsx/.js/.php for nav_menu, wp/v2 (menus, pages, media, posts), wp-cli, wp_mail returned zero hits; the only non-wc REST namespace the Hub calls is the plugin's own wc-gh/v1 (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32), and the only WP REST roots in code are wp-json/wc/v3 (src/server/woo/client.ts:192) and wp-json/wc-gh/v1. No menu or WP-core page code exists. Two nuances that keep the claim from being fully literal but are minor: (1) the record is not "WordPress is out of scope" wholesale — hub-and-plugin.md:32 defines a sanctioned pattern for storefront presentation ("the plugin renders it; the Hub edits it through the plugin's REST endpoints"), and the Vetrina homepage editor (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts, targeting a WP page via page_id at :40-42) is working Hub→plugin→WP-page code that an LLM-facing middleware could build on; (2) the Hub does do media work within scope (product photo upload, src/server/woo/media.ts, record :127 "Product photos at creation | Hub ✓"), just not the library-wide usage index/cleanup. So "no partial menu/page work" should read "no menu or WP-core page/post/media-library work; the one WP-side editing pattern that exists is the Vetrina homepage via the plugin's REST routes".
  - Corrected claim: The repository's decision record (docs/hub-and-plugin.md) places navigation menus (:121), media-library usage index/cleanup (:129), email and order metabox (:130-131) and WP-CLI nuclear cleanup (:133) in the WordPress plugin or WP admin, explicitly outside the Hub, and states people keep editing menus, SEO, descriptions and extra photos in WordPress (:35-36). The code matches: there are no calls to wp/v2 menus, pages, posts or media, no WP-CLI or wp_mail usage, and the only WordPress REST namespaces used are wc/v3 and the plugin's wc-gh/v1. Exposing the Hub as a WordPress-wide middleware therefore contradicts the current record and its "one writer of product data" rule (:21), unless the record is amended or the change is made consciously in a fresh repo. The one WP-side pattern to build on is the record's own rule for storefront presentation (:32) as implemented by the Vetrina homepage editor (src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts: Hub -> plugin REST -> WP page); there is no partial menu or WP-core page work.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The facts hold (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 menus → Plugin; :129-131 media/email → Plugin; :133 nuclear cleanup → Plugin (WP-CLI); :35-36 people edit menus in WordPress). The implication and severity do not.

1. The record's rule is narrower than the auditor reads it. hub-and-plugin.md:7-10 and :21-24 state the purpose: avoid two sync engines on one store, so "one writer of **product data**" (sizes, identity, price, stock, rule-made category). Menus, media, email are not product data; the rule says nothing about them, so exposing them from a middleware cannot "contradict" it. The placement table at :27-33 assigns to the Plugin only what "must run inside a WordPress request or hook" (wp_mail, media library internals, order metaboxes, the theme). That constraint is physical, not a scope policy, and it binds equally whether the owner keeps this repo or starts a fresh one — so it cannot be a reason to prefer extraction.

2. The menus row is the one exception, and its stated reason undermines the auditor's point: :121 justifies "Plugin" only with "WordPress's menu editor already does it" — a UI-redundancy argument against building a second human UI. For an LLM-driven middleware that argument evaporates (the whole premise is no human UI). Nothing in the record says the Hub must not write menus through WordPress REST.

3. The record already blesses the exact pattern the auditor says is a contradiction: :31 "the plugin renders it; the Hub edits it through the plugin's REST endpoints", and section 5 (:152-158) lists Hub → non-Woo WordPress writes (wc-gh/v1 storefront sections; order meta `_rp_em_tracking_*` that drives the plugin's emails). In code, src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-37 talks to `/wp-json/wc-gh/v1` (a WordPress plugin namespace, not WooCommerce) with the same WOO_* Basic auth; src/server/vetrina/source.ts:19-37 defines a VetrinaSource contract with live/fixture implementations — a ready seam for a `wp/v2` menus source. So "the Hub only speaks WooCommerce" is false; reaching WordPress-wide REST is the established architecture.

4. The codebase already anticipates non-UI consumers: docs/api.md:1-7 describes `/api/v1` as "the Vetrina's actions for a script, another service or an assistant", with described actions, proposals/confirm and undo. The record is a living placement table (section 6 lists future Hub work); adding a row for menus is a one-line amendment, not a structural obstacle.

5. Severity: a decision-record row with a UI-redundancy rationale that is trivially amendable, in a repo whose architecture and API doc already model Hub → WordPress-plugin REST writes for programmatic consumers, is informational/minor at most. It is not a factor in keep-vs-extract: WP-CLI/wp_mail/media internals remain plugin-side in either repo, and menus can be added via core `wp/v2/menus` either way. The auditor's "no partial menu/page work" is true but irrelevant to that choice.
  - Corrected claim: The decision record (docs/hub-and-plugin.md) places navigation menus, media cleanup, email and WP-CLI operations in the WordPress plugin, but its binding rule ("one writer of product data", :21) concerns only product data and its Plugin criterion (:30) is "must run inside a WordPress request or hook" — which applies to email/media/WP-CLI in any repo and does not apply to menus, whose placement rests solely on "WordPress's menu editor already does it" (:121), a UI-redundancy argument that lapses for an LLM-driven middleware. The record and code already model Hub → non-WooCommerce WordPress REST writes (wc-gh/v1 in src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:29-37; order meta in :152-158) and a programmatic API for "a script, another service or an assistant" (docs/api.md:1-7). Exposing menus through wp/v2 is a one-row amendment consistent with the record, not a contradiction, and has no bearing on keep-vs-extract. Severity: informational.

#### WP-7 [contested · minor] The WooCommerce client is purpose-built for the sync path and covers 12 wc/v3 resource paths with hand-written methods, not a generic REST wrapper; the only reusable transport is the retry/backoff requestJson adapter.

- **Evidence**: src/server/woo/client.ts:12-17 header comment 'Thin WooCommerce REST v3 client for the live sync path'; public methods at lines 219,255,306,329,343,358,379-427,485,523,569,601,616,629,639,648,667 (products, variations, orders read, one settings key, attributes, brands, categories, tags, attribute terms, batches); apiUrl at :190-201 forces /wp-json/wc/v3; src/server/adapters/http.ts:53-70 is the shared fetch with retry; taxonomy-cache.ts:45-73 is a Proxy over named methods.
- **Implication**: Exposing the core as middleware means each new Woo or WP capability is a new typed method plus Zod schema, which is safe but not self-describing; there is no generic 'call any wc/v3 or wp/v2 resource' layer an LLM tool could lean on. Extractable as-is into a package (it depends only on env and the http adapter), so it does not force a rewrite.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location. src/server/woo/client.ts:12-17 header reads exactly "Thin WooCommerce REST v3 client for the live sync path". Public methods (grep of `^  async`) are at 219 getProductsPage, 255 getAllVariations, 306 getOrdersPage, 329 getFullProduct, 343 getDefaultCatalogOrderby, 358 getAttributeTaxonomies, 379/383 brands, 388/392 categories, 400/404 tags, 409/413 attribute terms, 421 createAttribute, 485 batchVariations, 523 getProductIndexPage, 569 batchUpdateProducts, 601 deleteProduct, 616 findProductsBySku, 629 createProduct, 639 updateProduct, 648 getProduct, 667 updateProductOnce — all hand-written. Distinct resource paths via apiUrl(): products, products/{id}, products/{id}/variations, products/{id}/variations/batch, products/batch, products/attributes, products/attributes/{id}/terms, products/brands, products/categories, products/tags, orders, settings/products/woocommerce_default_catalog_orderby = 12. apiUrl at :190-201 appends /wp-json/wc/v3 unless WOO_BASE_URL already contains /wp-json (line 192) — so "forces" is slightly overstated (an operator could point the base at another namespace), but the method is private and there is no public generic request method; the only generic path helpers are private listTerms(path) (:430-452) and createTerm(path, body) (:455-472). http.ts:53-70 is requestJson/requestJsonWithHeaders with retry/timeout as described. taxonomy-cache.ts:45-73 is a Proxy switching on named methods. Counterexample search: grep for wp/v2 in src finds nothing; the only other REST surface is src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts, which has a module-private generic call(method, route, query, body) (:74-98) but pinned to the custom plugin namespace /wp-json/wc-gh/v1 (:29-33) — it is not a wp/v2 or wc/v3 generic layer, so it does not refute the claim, though the claim omits that a second hand-rolled client to a WordPress plugin namespace exists. Dependency note: client.ts also imports ./paginate and zod besides env and the http adapter (lines 2-10) — trivial. Claim stands with minor wording corrections.
  - Corrected claim: The WooCommerce client (src/server/woo/client.ts) is purpose-built for the sync path and covers 12 wc/v3 resource paths with hand-written public methods, not a generic REST wrapper; its only generic path helpers (listTerms/createTerm) are private. apiUrl defaults to /wp-json/wc/v3 unless WOO_BASE_URL already contains /wp-json, and there is no public method to call an arbitrary wc/v3 or wp/v2 route. The only reusable transport is the retry/backoff requestJson adapter (src/server/adapters/http.ts); a second hand-rolled client exists in src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts with a module-private generic call() but pinned to the custom plugin namespace wc-gh/v1. Nothing in src calls wp/v2. The client depends on env, the http adapter, ./paginate and zod, so it is extractable into a package without a rewrite.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): The factual inventory holds (src/server/woo/client.ts:12-17 comment, hand-written methods at :219-:667, apiUrl at :190-201, shared requestJson in src/server/adapters/http.ts:53-70, Proxy in taxonomy-cache.ts:45-73), but the stated implication does not follow from it, for three reasons. (1) The "no generic call-any-resource layer" is framed as a gap for LLM exposure, when a typed, narrow method-per-capability surface with Zod validation is exactly the shape an MCP/tool layer wants (one tool per method, schema derivable from the Zod types); a raw passthrough is the thing a competent engineer would deliberately NOT hand an LLM. The auditor's own wording ("safe", severity minor) concedes this, so the consequence is at most neutral, not a liability. (2) The client is less rigid than claimed: apiUrl at client.ts:192 keeps any baseUrl that already contains /wp-json as the root (`base.includes("/wp-json") ? base : ...wc/v3`), so the namespace is not hard-forced; and the product write path is already a schema-free passthrough — createProduct(body: Record<string, unknown>) :629, updateProduct(id, body: Record<string, unknown>) :639, updateProductOnce :667, batchUpdateProducts(({id} & Record<string, unknown>)[]) :569, getFullProduct returning Record<string, unknown> :329. So "each new capability needs a new typed method plus Zod schema" is false for the product resource, the one that matters most. (3) The real obstacle for the user's scenario (WP core: menus, wp/v2) is not client shape but authentication, which the auditor missed: client.ts:204-212 and src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:18-21,35-38 authenticate with WooCommerce consumer key/secret via Basic auth, which WordPress honours only for wc-* namespaces (wp-source.ts comment: 'WooCommerce authenticates "wc-*" namespaces with its REST API keys'). No Application Password / wp/v2 credential exists anywhere in src (grep for wp/v2 and application password hits only docs/auth.md's unrelated APP_PASSWORD). A generic 'call any wc/v3 or wp/v2 resource' layer would therefore not unlock WP-core capabilities at all; what is needed is a second credential/auth path (WP Application Passwords) or a custom wc-* plugin namespace, the latter already demonstrated by vetrina/wp-source.ts (wc-gh/v1). Minor nit on extractability: the client also imports "server-only" (:1) and ./paginate (:3), not just env and the http adapter, so it is portable but not literally two-dependency. Severity "minor" is acceptable, but the implication points at the wrong remedy.
  - Corrected claim: The WooCommerce client (src/server/woo/client.ts) is a purpose-built, typed wc/v3 client with hand-written methods over a shared retry/backoff requestJson adapter (src/server/adapters/http.ts), and that shape is suited to LLM/MCP exposure rather than an obstacle: each method is a natural typed tool, and product writes already accept arbitrary bodies (createProduct/updateProduct/updateProductOnce/batchUpdateProducts take Record<string, unknown>), so a generic passthrough is not needed for Woo. The actual gap for WordPress-core capabilities (menus, wp/v2) is authentication, not client shape: the Hub only holds WooCommerce consumer key/secret, which WordPress accepts solely for wc-* namespaces (see vetrina/wp-source.ts using wc-gh/v1). Exposing WP-core features requires a separate WP credential (Application Passwords) or a custom wc-* plugin namespace, regardless of whether a generic REST layer exists. The client is extractable into a package with small dependencies (env, http adapter, paginate, server-only), so it does not force a rewrite. Severity: minor.


<a id="safety"></a>
## Safety model for an autonomous (LLM) caller

### Summary

The Vetrina action layer (21 action kinds in src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:41-220) is the part of this codebase that was visibly designed with an outside/LLM caller in mind, and its core safety primitives are real and tested: every action input is Zod-validated (parseAction, registry.ts:269-273; caps such as MAX_OPERATION_PRODUCTS=2000 and percent 1-90 in input.ts:68-90), the proposal/confirm flow previews on the live shop, fingerprints the preview (sha256 of stable JSON, proposal-plan.ts:30-32), expires in 15 minutes, claims the row atomically with a conditional UPDATE so two concurrent confirms cannot both run it (proposals.ts:144-149), re-previews at confirm and replaces the proposal on any drift (proposals.ts:157-162), and is idempotent on a second confirm (proposals.ts:126-128). Catalog writes run one at a time through a single in-process promise chain (runner.ts:269-286), re-read each product chunk from Woo right before writing (writes.ts:116,168,224,319,590,633,676; remove.ts:70), file before/after items for a 7-day undo (plan.ts:406-411), and homepage writes carry expected_modified_gmt / expected_attrs_hash compare-and-swap to the WP plugin (page.ts:93-96, wp-source.ts:160-205). Site-guard (assertSnapshotIsThisStore) protects the Hub-side apply/publish/rebuild/repair/duplicates/store-edit paths (8 call sites) against a database pointed at another shop. The gaps are in identity and authorization, not in write mechanics: authentication is one shared bearer token (HUB_API_TOKEN, v1.ts:57-61) with no per-caller identity, no scopes and no read-only mode; the "owner's yes" on confirm is any holder of that same token (confirm route calls confirmProposal(id) with no actor, proposals/[id]/confirm/route.ts:16); the log records only via (app|api|assistant|scheduler) and no caller/actor column (schema.ts:681, 755), and "assistant" and "scheduler" are never written by production code (only tests). Two API write paths bypass proposals entirely and start operations on one call (POST /api/v1/catalog/order and /order-sources/{id}/apply, catalog-order.ts:113, order-sources.ts:66), and DELETE /api/v1/order-sources/{id} deletes without preview. There is no rate limiting, no cap on open proposals or queue depth, and each proposal triggers live Woo reads. The heavy Hub-side destructive paths (sync apply, publish, rebuild, repair, pull, trash-duplicate, resetPricing, resetTaxonomy) are server actions behind the Authelia session only, outside the operations log/undo model, with dry-run but no proposal and no via. Multi-tenancy is one-Hub-per-shop with no shop column in any table (site-guard.ts:289-296 states this), so a multi-shop middleware would need a tenant dimension added to proposals, operations, token and every store table. The pieces an LLM caller needs are present and well-factored for the 21 Vetrina actions; what is missing is an identity/authorization layer, consistent proposal gating across every write, and backpressure.

### Inventory

## Authentication surfaces

| Surface | Check | Where | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| /api/v1/* | `Authorization: Bearer HUB_API_TOKEN`, constant-time compare | src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61, src/lib/auth.ts:101-108 | Single shared token, `z.string().min(24).optional()` (src/lib/env.ts:53); unset = 503. No per-caller id, no scopes, no read-only variant. |
| /api/cron/* (4 routes: media, pull-store, refresh-catalog, sync-goldensneakers) | `=== \`Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}\`` | e.g. src/app/api/cron/pull-store/route.ts:16-21 | Plain string `!==`, not `secretMatches`. |
| /api/publish | Origin==Host check only; relies on Authelia session via proxy | src/app/api/publish/route.ts:18-33 | Creates up to 25 products per call; not reachable with HUB_API_TOKEN (not in proxy's anonymous list). |
| /api/dock, /api/health | none of their own | src/app/api/dock/route.ts, health/route.ts | dock is behind the proxy gate only; health open. |
| UI (pages + server actions) | Authelia forward_auth via Caddy; `X-Auth-Proxy-Secret` + `Remote-User` required | src/proxy.ts:234-267 | `/api/v1/` and `/api/cron/` are "anonymous" at the proxy (proxy.ts:254-260) but still need the Caddy secret when set (proxy.ts:240). With AUTH_PROXY_SECRET unset the whole app is open (proxy.ts:260, documented as local dev). |

- `OperationVia = "app" | "api" | "assistant" | "scheduler"` (src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247-248). Stored on `vetrina_operations.via` (schema.ts:681) and `vetrina_proposals.via` (schema.ts:755). No `actor`, `created_by`, `token_id` or `shop_id` column on either table (schema.ts:667-706, 748-772).
- Production writers of via: `"app"` (default, service.ts:79,114), `"api"` (proposals/route.ts:22, catalog-order.ts:113, order-sources.ts:66). `"assistant"`: only in src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.db.test.ts:69-147. `"scheduler"`: zero occurrences in src/server outside i18n. The scheduler's sweep (scheduler.ts:756-757 -> runner.ts:310-328 -> writes.ts:163-180) files its hides as items on the ORIGINAL hide operation (writes.ts:178-180), so scheduler-driven writes inherit that row's via.

## Write paths and their gating

| Path | Entry | Validation | Preview/dry-run | Proposal required? | Logged in vetrina_operations (undo) | Site guard | Live re-read |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13 catalog actions + 8 homepage actions via proposals | POST /api/v1/proposals -> /confirm | `parseAction` Zod (registry.ts:269-273) | live preview at propose and again at confirm (proposals.ts:38-47,153) | yes | yes, via = proposal's via (proposals.ts:166-168) | no (not needed: writes target ids read live) | yes per chunk (writes.ts, remove.ts:70, order.ts:316,335); homepage: CAS on modified_gmt/attrs_hash (page.ts:93-96) |
| `order` action | POST /api/v1/catalog/order | `OrderBody` Zod (catalog-order.ts:77-87) | `dryRun: true` -> preview (live) | **no** — `startOperation(..., "api")` on one call, 202 (catalog-order.ts:113) | yes | no | yes (order.ts:316,335) |
| `sourceOrder` action | POST /api/v1/order-sources/{id}/apply | `ApplyBody` Zod (order-sources.ts:51-54) | `dryRun: true` | **no** (order-sources.ts:66) | yes | no | yes |
| Undo any action | POST /api/v1/operations/{id}/undo | UUID check only (v1.ts:45-48) | none | **no** | status transitions (service.ts:142-175) | no | undo writes only where store still shows `after` (writes.ts header, remove.ts:141-186) |
| Upload / delete order source | POST, DELETE /api/v1/order-sources | upload parser | none | no | no (Hub-only data) | n/a | n/a |
| Sync apply (prices/stock to Woo) | server action `startSyncApply`/`applySyncPrices` (actions/sync.ts:282-328) | Zod | `dryRun` option (apply.ts:61,553) | no | **no** — separate `apply_audit` table, no via/actor (schema.ts:97-110), no undo | yes (apply.ts:430) | `decideLiveWrites` per product (apply.ts:648, live-check.ts:345-381) |
| Publish products | server action `runPublish` + POST /api/publish | Zod | `dryRun` (publish.ts:386,404) | no | apply_audit only | yes (publish.ts:405) | reads on-store product before create/reconcile (publish.ts:574-585) |
| Rebuild variation sets ("obliterate + re-create", actions/sync.ts:207-212) | server action `rebuildStoreProducts` | Zod | `dryRun` (rebuild.ts:102,212) | no | apply_audit only | yes (rebuild.ts:105) | n/a |
| Repair | server action `runRepair` | Zod | `dryRun` (repair.ts:121,208) | no | no | yes (repair.ts:123) | reads on-store product (repair.ts:208) |
| Trash duplicate product | server action `trashDuplicateStoreProduct` (actions/duplicates.ts:22-57) | Zod | none; recomputes "safe duplicate" from snapshot | no | no; trash not force-delete | yes (duplicates.ts:31) | snapshot only (no live read) |
| Full store pull (replaces Hub snapshot) | POST /api/cron/pull-store, server action `startStorePull` | — | none | no | store_pull_runs | **exempt by design** (site-guard.ts:293-295) | n/a |
| Reset pricing / taxonomy config | server actions (config.ts:26, taxonomy.ts:97) | — | none | no | no | n/a | n/a |
| Store edits (manual price locks etc.) | server action (actions/store-edit.ts:38) | Zod | — | no | price_ledger | yes | — |

- `src/server/actions/debug.ts` (311 lines): **read-only** diagnostics (`debugMatch`, `debugBulkPrices`, `auditPrices`); no writes to Woo or DB. No "nuclear" op there.
- Most destructive thing an API token holder can do: `delete` action (trash, undoable 7 days while in WP trash; feed-sourced products refused, remove.ts:64-74; query targets frozen to ids at filing, service.ts:87-93), `sale` on up to 2000 products, `order`/`sourceOrder` without proposal.
- Most destructive thing a signed-in UI user can do without any proposal: `rebuildStoreProducts`, `applySync` whole-store, `runPublish`, full pull.

## Proposal / confirm model (src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts)

| Property | Implementation | Line |
|---|---|---|
| Preview at propose | `assess(input, { live: true })` / `assessPage` | 38-47 |
| Fingerprint | sha256 of stable JSON of preview | proposal-plan.ts:30-32; stored `digest` |
| Expiry | `PROPOSAL_MINUTES = 15` (src/lib/vetrina/proposals.ts:12); status "expired" derived from `expiresAt` | 63, 89 |
| Race on confirm | conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status='open' AND expires_at > now()` returning; loser recurses and gets confirmed/confirming answer | 144-149 |
| Idempotent re-confirm | status "confirmed" -> returns the operation started | 126-128 |
| Drift | re-preview in proposal's locale; digest mismatch -> status "changed", new proposal inserted, 409 with replacement | 153-162 |
| Refusal at confirm | VetrinaError invalid/stale/not_found -> "refused" and closed; other errors -> back to "open" | 181-188 |
| Retention | 30 days (`trimProposals`) | 35, 191-197 |
| Homepage `expected` | stripped from stored input; proposal preview is the check | 50-54 |
| Who confirms | anyone with HUB_API_TOKEN; no actor recorded, no binding of proposer to confirmer | confirm/route.ts:16 |
| Tests | drift and expiry covered (proposals.db.test.ts:97-136); no concurrent-confirm test (`Promise.all` absent in operations/*.test.ts) | — |

## Runner / backpressure (runner.ts)
- Single global promise chain, FIFO, `globalThis` state (runner.ts:236-240, 269-286); restart marks in-flight ops "interrupted" (254-267).
- No queue length cap, no per-caller limit, no dedupe of identical queued operations. `propose` has no cap on open proposals; each propose does live Woo reads.
- No rate limiting anywhere in src (grep for rate-limit/429 finds only outbound client retry handling in adapters/http.ts:32-49 and media.ts:262).
- Input caps that bound a single action: `MAX_OPERATION_PRODUCTS = 2000` (catalog-ops.ts:20), sale percent 1-90 (input.ts:90), margin 0-200 (catalog-ops.ts:144-145), `q` <= 100 chars, position <= 1,000,000, tag name <= 120.

## Undo window
- `OPERATION_UNDO_DAYS = 7` (catalog-ops.ts:31); `undoable()` plan.ts:406-411: done, or failed with changed>0; whole-term hide undoable indefinitely while standing.
- Limits: value written back only where store still shows the action's `after` (per-item); homepage undo refused if section fingerprint changed (page.ts:521,533,545,581); margin undo refused if rule changed since (service.ts:170); delete undo refused if trash emptied or SKU re-used (remove.ts:150-168). Log retention 30 days.

## Multi-tenancy
- Every table is one shop's state, no shop column (site-guard.ts:289-292 says so explicitly; grep for shop_id/tenant in schema.ts: 0 hits). Shop identity = env `WOO_BASE_URL/WOO_CONSUMER_KEY/SECRET` (env.ts:44-46), one `HUB_API_TOKEN`, one `DATABASE_URL`.
- Per-shop separation is done at the deployment layer: one Hub container per shop, Authelia groups per shop host (docs/auth.md:8-11).
- WP plugin (wc-gh/v1) calls authenticate with the same Woo consumer key/secret as Basic auth (wp-source.ts:35-38).

### Claims and verdicts

#### S1 [contested · strength] The proposal/confirm two-phase model is correctly implemented for atomicity, drift and idempotency: a conditional UPDATE claims the proposal so two concurrent confirms cannot both run it, the preview is recomputed and fingerprint-compared at confirm, and a second confirm returns the already-started operation.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:144-149 (UPDATE ... WHERE status='open' AND expires_at > now() ... returning; loser recurses), :153-162 (re-preview, digestOf mismatch -> 'changed' + replacement proposal, 409), :126-128 (confirmed -> loadOperation). Fingerprint: proposal-plan.ts:30-32 (sha256 of stable JSON). Expiry 15 min: src/lib/vetrina/proposals.ts:12. Tests for drift/expiry: proposals.db.test.ts:97-136; no concurrent-confirm test exists.
- **Implication**: This is the right primitive for an LLM caller (propose -> show summary -> confirm) and should be kept as-is in any of keep/extract/fresh; it is framework-light (Drizzle + Zod) and extractable with its two tables. The only addition needed is an actor/caller identity on the row.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Every cited line checks out literally. src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:144-149: `db.update(vetrinaProposals).set({status:"confirming"}).where(and(eq(id), eq(status,"open"), gt(expiresAt, now))).returning(...)`; `if (claimed.length === 0) return confirmProposal(id)` — the loser re-reads the row and hits the `confirming` (409, :129-130) or `confirmed` (:126-128 -> loadOperation) branch, so two concurrent confirms cannot both start the action. :153 re-previews in the proposal's locale via previewOf (assess live:true, :38-47); :157-161 compares `digestOf(made.preview) !== row.digest`, inserts a replacement proposal, marks the old one `changed`/`replacedBy`, throws VetrinaError("stale", 409, {proposal}). digestOf is sha256 over stableJson (proposal-plan.ts:15-32, key-sorted, undefined dropped). PROPOSAL_MINUTES = 15 (src/lib/vetrina/proposals.ts:12); the row stores `digest`, `status`, `operationId`, `replacedBy`, `expiresAt` (schema.ts:748-771) and has no actor/caller column — only `via`. Tests: proposals.db.test.ts:97-136 cover drift ("changed" + replacement, re-confirm of old points to new), refused, and expiry; grep for Promise.all/concurren/confirming in *.test.ts finds nothing, so "no concurrent-confirm test" is accurate. Minor nuances not in the claim but worth noting for the implication: (a) idempotent return of the operation applies only once status is `confirmed`; during the `confirming` window the second caller gets 409, not the operation; (b) a crash between the claim (:144) and the final update (:172) leaves the row `confirming` forever — proposalView (:63) only maps `open` past expiry to `expired`, and nothing (no recover()) resets a stuck `confirming`, so that proposal becomes permanently 409; (c) refuse() (:181-188) resets to `open` on non-Vetrina errors, which if startOperation failed after repo.insertOperation (service.ts:94-104) could let a re-confirm file the operation twice — a narrow edge. None of these contradict the stated claim, so it stands.
  - Corrected claim: The proposal/confirm two-phase model is correctly implemented for atomicity, drift and idempotency: a conditional UPDATE (status='open' AND expires_at>now, RETURNING) claims the proposal so two concurrent confirms cannot both run it, the preview is recomputed in the proposal's locale and sha256-fingerprint-compared at confirm (mismatch -> 'changed' + replacement proposal, 409), and a confirm after completion returns the already-started operation (a confirm during the brief 'confirming' window gets 409 instead). Gaps worth noting but not asserted: a crash mid-confirm leaves the row 'confirming' with no recovery path (permanent 409), there is no actor/caller identity on the row (only `via`), and no concurrent-confirm test exists.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The factual part holds: the conditional claim (src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.ts:144-149), re-preview + digest compare with replacement proposal (:153-162), confirmed -> loadOperation (:126-128), sha256 of stable JSON (proposal-plan.ts:30-32), 15 min expiry (src/lib/vetrina/proposals.ts:12) are all as described. But the implication "keep as-is; the only addition needed is an actor/caller identity" does not follow for the LLM-middleware question:

1. The confirm step is not an owner-consent gate at all on the server. Both POST /api/v1/proposals (src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22) and POST /api/v1/proposals/{id}/confirm (src/app/api/v1/proposals/[id]/confirm/route.ts:16) go through the same serve() with one shared HUB_API_TOKEN bearer (src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61). Whoever can propose can confirm. In the proposed flow the LLM holds that token, so the "owner's yes" is a prompt-level convention, not enforced. The fix is not a column on the row but a separate confirm credential/channel (per-proposal confirm secret handed only to the human, or a human-only confirm route). That is a structural change to the primitive's authorization, so "keep as-is" is wrong for exactly the dimension being audited.

2. The "confirming" state has no expiry and no recovery. proposalView maps only open -> expired (:64); grep shows no other code touching "confirming" besides the 409 (:129-130) and the claim (:146). If the process dies between the claim (:144-149) and the final update (:174) (deploy restart, OOM, WP call hanging past the request lifetime), the proposal returns 409 "It is being done already" forever. For a page action, startPageAction writes to WP first (page.ts:470-474) and files the log after, so the write may have landed with no operationId recorded, and the promised "second confirm returns the already-started operation" never happens. No test covers this (proposals.db.test.ts only tests the happy double-confirm at :79). An autonomous caller with no UI cannot diagnose or repair that; a human can look at the shop.

3. Idempotency is at-least-once, not exactly-once: refuse() resets status to "open" on any non-VetrinaError (:184-190), so a timeout after the Woo/WP write has actually landed lets the next confirm run the action again. The code comments admit this ("leaves it open, to be confirmed again"), but it contradicts "only once" for the transient-error case an LLM retry loop will hit.

4. Drift detection is coarse: the digest covers counts, warnings and at most SAMPLE=20 sampled before/after names (preview.ts:51, :98, :148), and startOperation at confirm re-assesses with live:false (service.ts:83). Changes outside the sample that do not alter the counts pass the fingerprint. Acceptable, but not "every change" and worth stating when selling the primitive as the safety model.

5. propose has no idempotency key (route.ts:22; insert at :73-92): an LLM retrying propose creates several open, independently confirmable proposals; re-preview mitigates for toggles but not for additive actions.

A competent engineer would still rate the design a strength conceptually, but would not conclude it is ready as-is: the shared-token confirm (1) and the unrecoverable confirming state (2) must be addressed before exposure to an autonomous caller, and neither is "add an actor column". Severity should be "strength with required changes", not an unqualified strength.
  - Corrected claim: The proposal/confirm model is well designed and extractable (conditional UPDATE claim, re-preview + sha256 digest compare with replacement proposal, confirmed -> same operation), and should be kept in any keep/extract/fresh option. It is not ready as-is for an autonomous caller: (a) propose and confirm are authorized by the same single HUB_API_TOKEN (src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61), so the server does not enforce that a human, rather than the proposing LLM, confirms; confirm needs a separate credential/channel, not just an actor column; (b) the "confirming" status never expires and has no recovery path (proposals.ts:64, :129-130, :144-149), so a crash mid-confirm leaves the proposal stuck at 409, possibly after the write landed; (c) retries after transient errors re-open the proposal (:184-190), giving at-least-once rather than exactly-once; (d) the digest covers only counts, warnings and up to 20 sampled items (preview.ts:51), so drift outside the sample is not detected; (e) propose has no idempotency key. None of these is covered by tests (no concurrent-confirm, crash, or stuck-confirming test).

#### S2 [contested · blocker] Authentication for the programmatic API is a single shared bearer token with no per-caller identity, no scopes, and no read-only mode; the audit trail can distinguish only `via` (app|api|assistant|scheduler), never which caller or which token, and 'assistant' and 'scheduler' are never written by production code.

- **Evidence**: src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61 (one env.HUB_API_TOKEN, secretMatches); src/lib/env.ts:53 (single string). schema.ts:681 and :755: `via` column only, no actor/created_by. OPERATION_VIAS at src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247. grep '"assistant"' in src: only proposals.db.test.ts:69-147; grep '"scheduler"' in src/server (non-i18n): 0 hits. Confirm route passes no identity: src/app/api/v1/proposals/[id]/confirm/route.ts:16.
- **Implication**: Blocker for 'customer -> LLM -> middleware': the 'owner's yes' is currently 'whoever holds the token', so an LLM agent holding the token can propose AND confirm its own proposals with nothing in the log telling it apart from a human script. Any direction (keep/extract/fresh) must add a caller principal (per-token or OAuth identity), a scope/read-only flag, and store the principal on proposals and operations. The schema change is small (two columns) and the gating point is one function (`serve`), so this is additive, not a rewrite.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited location and searched for counterexamples.

Single shared token, no identity/scopes/read-only: src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61 — `serve()` reads `Authorization: Bearer`, compares with `secretMatches(token, env.HUB_API_TOKEN)`, and on success calls `handler(req)` with nothing derived from the token; the only other header read in the API layer is `accept-language` (v1.ts:39). src/lib/env.ts:53 — `HUB_API_TOKEN: z.string().min(24).optional()`, one string. All 14 /api/v1 routes (src/app/api/v1/**/route.ts) go through `serve`. grep for scope/readOnly/read_only in src/server and src/app/api finds nothing token-related; the only "read-only" affordance is the per-request `dryRun` body flag (src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:87,112; order-sources.ts:66), which any token holder can simply omit. No apiKeys/tokens/actor table exists in src/server/db/schema.ts (grep for pgTable names with key/token/actor/user: none).

Audit trail: src/server/db/schema.ts:681 (`vetrina_operations.via`, default "app") and :755 (`vetrina_proposals.via`) are the only provenance columns; grep for actor/created_by/createdBy/principal/caller in schema.ts: none. Authelia's `Remote-User` is consumed only by src/lib/auth.ts:51 `signedInUser` for the top bar (src/app/(admin)/layout.tsx:28, TopBar.tsx) and is never passed to startOperation/propose/insert*; src/proxy.ts comments confirm the app has no login of its own.

OPERATION_VIAS at src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247 = ["app","api","assistant","scheduler"]. Production writers of `via`: "api" at src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66, src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113, src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22; "app" by default at service.ts:79,114 and page.ts:468; confirmProposal re-uses row.via (proposals.ts:167-168). grep '"assistant"' in non-test, non-i18n src: only the OPERATION_VIAS literal itself; the only user is src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.db.test.ts:69 (`propose(..., "assistant")`). grep '"scheduler"' in src/server: 0 hits; the scheduler's only live write, `repair()` at src/server/scheduler.ts:418-430, calls repairProducts directly and files no operation at all.

Confirm passes no identity: src/app/api/v1/proposals/[id]/confirm/route.ts:16 calls `confirmProposal(proposalId(id))`; its signature (proposals.ts:122) takes only the id. So the same token that POSTs /api/v1/proposals can POST .../confirm, and both rows carry via="api" with nothing else distinguishing callers. The implication (additive fix: principal column(s) + gate in `serve`) is consistent with the code structure. Claim stands as written.
  - Corrected claim: Authentication for the programmatic API (/api/v1/*) is a single shared bearer token (env.HUB_API_TOKEN, checked in `serve` at src/server/api/v1.ts:57-61) with no per-caller identity, no scopes, and no read-only mode (the only read-only affordance is the optional per-request `dryRun` body flag, which any token holder may omit); Authelia's Remote-User is used only for the UI top bar and never stored. The audit trail (vetrina_operations.via, vetrina_proposals.via) records only `via` (app|api|assistant|scheduler), never which caller or token; production code writes only "app" and "api" — "assistant" appears only in a test and "scheduler" is never written (the scheduler's self-repair files no operation). The confirm route passes no identity to confirmProposal, so a token holder can propose and confirm its own proposals indistinguishably from any other API caller.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Facts confirmed: src/server/api/v1.ts:56-61 checks one env.HUB_API_TOKEN via secretMatches and passes nothing about the caller to the handler; src/lib/env.ts:53 is a single string; src/server/db/schema.ts:681 and :755 store only `via` (no actor column); src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22 calls propose(..., "api") and the confirm route (confirm/route.ts:16) calls confirmProposal(id) with no identity; proposals.ts:105-175 never inspects who confirms. 'assistant'/'scheduler' are not written by production code (grep: only catalog-ops.ts:247 and tests).

The implication and severity, however, do not follow. (1) The gap is already the documented design, not an oversight the keep/extract/fresh decision turns on: docs/operations.md:84-106 states the MCP layer is "the next step", runs in-process, files actions with via:"assistant" (so the 'assistant' value exists precisely for that unbuilt layer), and adds "the sign-in a remote client needs (OAuth for a connector in the Claude app)". The bearer token is a scripts-only transport (docs/auth.md:217-224; docs/api.md:3). None of this discriminates between the three directions, so it cannot be a blocker for choosing one; the auditor itself concedes it is "additive, not a rewrite" with "two columns" and one gating function. (2) The proposed remedy (per-token principal + scope/read-only) does not deliver the separation the auditor wants: in the documented flow (operations.md:97-99) the LLM is the component that calls confirmProposal after the owner's yes in chat, so an LLM with a write-scoped principal would still propose and confirm its own proposals; a principal only labels the row. The real fix for "owner's yes" is a human-only confirm channel (confirm from the signed-in Vetrina/Hub session, where Authelia already supplies Remote-User/Remote-Groups through src/proxy.ts and the proxy strips forged Remote-* on bypassed routes, src/proxy.test.ts:140-150; the vetrina_proposals table already works as a pending queue with 15-minute expiry, src/lib/vetrina/proposals.ts:12). That is also small and the repository already has the parts. (3) Mitigations the auditor missed that lower severity: the API is off unless HUB_API_TOKEN is set (503, v1.ts:57); it is unreachable from outside until an explicit Authelia bypass is added (auth.md:220-222); every action is logged and undoable for 7 days (catalog-ops.ts:31, /operations/{id}/undo); confirm re-previews and refuses on a changed shop (proposals.ts:158-162); deployments are single-operator plus one shop account (auth.md:1-12), so "which token" has little to distinguish today. A competent engineer would file this as a required pre-exposure work item (high), not a blocker for the architecture decision, and would aim the fix at a human-confirm channel plus storing the principal, rather than scopes on the shared token.
  - Corrected claim: The programmatic API authenticates with one shared HUB_API_TOKEN and no caller identity (src/server/api/v1.ts:56-61); proposals and operations record only `via` (schema.ts:681, :755), and 'assistant'/'scheduler' are not yet written by production code because the in-process MCP layer that would file via:"assistant" is documented but unbuilt (docs/operations.md:84-106). Consequence: today the server does not enforce that the "owner's yes" on /proposals/{id}/confirm comes from a human rather than the same automated caller that proposed. This is a required pre-exposure work item, not a blocker for the keep/extract/fresh decision (it is additive under every direction, and the API is disabled by default, behind Authelia, logged and undoable for 7 days). The fix is to store a principal on proposals/operations and, more importantly, to gate confirm on a human channel (the Authelia-signed-in Hub/Vetrina session, whose Remote-User src/proxy.ts already forwards) or a separate human-only credential; per-token scopes alone would not stop an LLM from confirming its own proposals.

#### S3 [contested · major] Two API write paths bypass the proposal model and start a background operation on a single authenticated call, and undo is likewise single-call: POST /api/v1/catalog/order, POST /api/v1/order-sources/{id}/apply, and POST /api/v1/operations/{id}/undo.

- **Evidence**: src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 (`operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api")` when !dryRun, 202), src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 (same), src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14 (`undoOperation(id)` directly). Compare proposals route which only previews: proposals/route.ts:22. docs/operations.md:331 states 'Anything asked from outside the screens should not write on its own word' — these three paths contradict that.
- **Implication**: For an LLM caller these are the paths where a hallucinated id or a misread instruction writes to Woo with no human checkpoint (undo in particular reverses an arbitrary logged action). Fixing it is cheap in this codebase: route order/sourceOrder through `propose` (the kinds already exist in the registry: registry.ts:123-139) and gate undo behind a proposal or a confirm flag. This favours 'keep/extract' over 'fresh': the correct gate already exists and only three call sites need redirecting.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited site. src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:112-113: `if (body.dryRun) return preview; return { dryRun:false, operation: await operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api") }` — a single POST without dryRun starts the write. src/server/api/order-sources.ts:65-66: identical pattern for kind "sourceOrder". The routes (src/app/api/v1/catalog/order/route.ts:26-33, src/app/api/v1/order-sources/[id]/apply/route.ts:18-30) answer 202 when not dryRun; no proposal/confirm step. src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14 calls `undoOperation(operationId(id))` from service.ts directly; service.ts:142-167 only checks status, the 7-day window and staleness of a section — no human confirmation. By contrast proposals/route.ts:22 calls `propose(...)` and only previews; confirm is a separate POST (docs/api.md:223). Registry: `order` at registry.ts:123 and `sourceOrder` at :132, so the kinds exist for `propose`. Grepping `startOperation(` across src/app/api and src/server/api finds exactly these two API call sites (plus proposals.ts:168 behind confirm), so "two write paths + undo" is complete; order-sources POST/DELETE only touch Hub-stored rankings, not Woo. Only inaccuracies: docs/operations.md is 114 lines — the quoted sentence is at line 59, not 331; and docs/api.md:17 already labels these as "Shortcuts that act in one call (they predate proposals)", i.e. the bypass is known/documented, not accidental. Both are trivial corrections that do not change the claim.
  - Corrected claim: Two API write paths bypass the proposal model and start a background operation on a single authenticated call, and undo is likewise single-call: POST /api/v1/catalog/order (src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113), POST /api/v1/order-sources/{id}/apply (src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66), and POST /api/v1/operations/{id}/undo (src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14, calling service.ts undoOperation directly, which only enforces status/7-day/staleness checks). The proposals route (proposals/route.ts:22) only previews. docs/operations.md:59 (not :331) states "Anything asked from outside the screens should not write on its own word", and docs/api.md:17 already acknowledges the two order endpoints as "Shortcuts that act in one call (they predate proposals)" — so the gap is known but unclosed. The `order` and `sourceOrder` kinds exist in the registry (registry.ts:123, :132), so routing them through `propose` and gating undo is a small change.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Facts check out: src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 and src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 call `operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api")` when !dryRun, and src/app/api/v1/operations/[id]/undo/route.ts:14 calls `undoOperation(operationId(id))` directly; docs/operations.md:59 says outside callers "should not write on its own word". But the implication and severity do not follow from them:

1. The shortcuts are not an unguarded gap the LLM must use; they are a documented legacy surface. docs/api.md:17-19 explicitly labels `/catalog/order` and `/order-sources/{id}/apply` as "Shortcuts that act in one call (they predate proposals)". The proposal path already accepts `order` and `sourceOrder` today: `propose()` (proposals.ts:105) goes through `parseAction`, whose registry includes both kinds (registry.ts:123-139), so an LLM tool manifest can expose only `POST /proposals` + `/proposals/{id}/confirm` and omit the shortcuts with zero code change. For an LLM-exposure question that is a tool-surface decision, not a codebase defect; a competent engineer would not call it "major".

2. Blast radius of the two shortcuts is small and reversible: they write only WooCommerce `menu_order` (order.ts:293-295), are logged, and are undoable for 7 days (registry.ts:129,138). A hallucinated term id or SKU is refused before any write (catalog-order.ts:97-98 unknown SKU -> 400; readOrder/parseOperationInput -> 404/400).

3. Undo is not "reverses an arbitrary logged action with no checkpoint". Mitigations the auditor missed: the id must be a UUID (v1.ts:43-47, otherwise 404, nothing written — a hallucinated id does not write); undoOperation (service.ts:142-175) refuses running/undoing/already-undone/too-old (>7 days) rows, refuses when a homepage section changed since (166) or a margin rule was re-set (170), and for product actions restores only where the store still shows the value the action wrote, counting others as `kept` (docs/api.md:249-254). It is the same one-click "Annulla" the UI's toasts and Attività offer (docs/operations.md:54), so API undo is consistent with the product's own safety model, not a bypass of it. The auditor is right that undo cannot be routed through `propose` (there is no `undo` action kind in the registry), so the "correct gate already exists" claim is only true for order/sourceOrder, not for undo.

Net: the doc inconsistency (operations.md:59 vs api.md:17) is real but acknowledged in the API doc; the practical risk is low (reversible menu_order writes, heavily guarded undo), and the remediation for LLM exposure is to publish only the proposal endpoints. Severity should be minor/doc-hygiene, not major. The "favours keep/extract over fresh" conclusion stands, but for a weaker reason than stated.
  - Corrected claim: Two legacy API shortcuts (POST /api/v1/catalog/order, POST /api/v1/order-sources/{id}/apply) and POST /api/v1/operations/{id}/undo write on a single authenticated call rather than through the proposal/confirm model. The shortcuts are documented as predating proposals (docs/api.md:17) and their kinds (`order`, `sourceOrder`) are already accepted by POST /api/v1/proposals, so an LLM tool surface can simply omit them with no code change; they only write `menu_order` and are undoable for 7 days. Undo has no proposal gate and none can be added via the existing registry (no `undo` kind), but it is bounded: UUID-validated ids (404 otherwise), 7-day window, refuses running/undone/stale rows, and restores only values the store still shows as the action wrote them — the same one-click "Annulla" the UI offers. Severity: minor (documentation inconsistency in docs/operations.md:59 and an optional confirm flag on undo), not major.

#### S4 [refuted · major] The high-blast-radius Hub-side writers (sync apply, publish, rebuild, repair, trash-duplicate, full pull, pricing/taxonomy reset) are server actions outside the operations log, with dry-run but no proposal, no via/actor, and no undo; they are reachable only through the Authelia session, not the API token.

- **Evidence**: src/server/actions/sync.ts:207-227 (rebuildStoreProducts, 'Obliterate + re-create ... Destructive'), :282-328 (applySyncPrices/startSyncApply); actions/publish.ts:131 (runPublish) and src/app/api/publish/route.ts:18-33 (origin check only, no token); actions/repair.ts:62; actions/duplicates.ts:22-57; actions/config.ts:26; actions/taxonomy.ts:97. They log to `apply_audit` (schema.ts:97-110: status, dryRun, counts — no via/actor). dryRun exists in apply.ts:553, publish.ts:386/404, rebuild.ts:102/212, repair.ts:121/208. Site guard on all: apply.ts:430, publish.ts:405, rebuild.ts:105, repair.ts:123, duplicates.ts:31, store-edit.ts:38, preview.ts:349/671/826. proxy.ts:254-260 keeps them behind the session.
- **Implication**: Today an LLM with the API token cannot reach these, which is safe by accident, not by design. If the middleware is to cover 'sync apply', 'publish', 'rebuild' as LLM tools (the owner's flow implies it), these need to be lifted into the same propose/confirm + operations-log shape as the Vetrina actions — a design the codebase already has but applied to only one of its two write families. This is the main structural debt for exposure, and it exists equally whether you keep, extract or restart; a fresh repo would have to rebuild these guards (site-guard, live-check, ledger) from scratch.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Core structure confirmed: rebuildStoreProducts (src/server/actions/sync.ts:207-227, "Destructive — dry-run first"), applySyncPrices/startSyncApply (sync.ts:282-318), runPublish (actions/publish.ts:131), runRepair (actions/repair.ts:62), trashDuplicateStoreProduct (actions/duplicates.ts:22-50), resetPricingToDefaults (actions/config.ts:26), resetTaxonomy (actions/taxonomy.ts:97) are plain server actions; none goes through vetrinaProposals/vetrinaOperations (schema.ts:667-695 has via, undo, undoneAt; schema.ts:748-755 proposals have via), and apply_audit (schema.ts:97-110) has only status/dryRun/counts, no via/actor. /api/v1 serves only the Vetrina registry (src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts; registry.ts hide/show/sale/tag/categorize/pin/delete/section*), so HUB_API_TOKEN cannot reach these writers; /api/publish/route.ts:18-33 is origin-check only behind the Authelia session.

Three parts are literally false, and one changes the implication:
1. "reachable only through the Authelia session": full pull is exposed headlessly at src/app/api/cron/pull-store/route.ts:15-28 behind `Bearer CRON_SECRET`, and proxy.ts:19-20,117-118 bypasses the session for /api/cron/* and /api/v1/*. Additionally the in-process scheduler runs runFullPull (src/server/scheduler.ts:367-374), repairProducts(..., {dryRun:false}) (scheduler.ts:420-426) and applySync via price-sync (src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:113) unattended with no session at all. So a token-bearing, non-session path to some of this write family already exists (CRON_SECRET), just not the HUB_API_TOKEN — "safe by accident" is only true for publish/rebuild/trash/reset.
2. "They log to apply_audit": only apply.ts, publish.ts:726, rebuild.ts:345 insert into apply_audit. repair.ts has no audit write at all (grep applyAudit|audit in src/server/woo/repair.ts: none), duplicates/config/taxonomy write no log, and full pull logs to store_pull_runs (pull.ts:5,54-81), not apply_audit.
3. "with dry-run" across the set: trashDuplicateStoreProduct has no dryRun (relies on isSafeDuplicate recomputation, duplicates.ts:31-35); resetPricingToDefaults and resetTaxonomy have no dry-run either.
The ledger/undo/proposal gap is real, but the claim as stated ("only via session", "they log to apply_audit", "with dry-run") is overstated across the family, and the CRON_SECRET/scheduler paths matter for the exposure argument.
  - Corrected claim: The high-blast-radius Hub-side writers (sync apply, publish, rebuild, repair, trash-duplicate, full pull, pricing/taxonomy reset) are server actions outside the Vetrina operations log: no proposal, no via/actor, no undo. Only sync apply, publish and rebuild have dry-run AND log to apply_audit (status/dryRun/counts, no via/actor); repair has dry-run but logs nowhere; trash-duplicate and the pricing/taxonomy resets have neither dry-run nor a log; full pull logs to store_pull_runs. They are not reachable with HUB_API_TOKEN (/api/v1 exposes only the Vetrina registry), but they are not session-only either: full pull is callable headlessly with CRON_SECRET (/api/cron/pull-store), and the scheduler runs full pull, live repair and sync apply unattended. Exposing them as LLM tools means lifting them into the propose/confirm + operations-log shape the Vetrina actions already have.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Facts confirmed: the Hub-side writers are plain server actions (src/server/actions/sync.ts:207-227 rebuildStoreProducts, :282-328 applySyncPrices/startSyncApply; publish via src/app/api/publish/route.ts:16-37 with an origin check only), they audit to apply_audit which has status/dryRun/counts but no via/actor (src/server/db/schema.ts:97-110), and only /api/v1 and /api/cron bypass the Authelia session (src/proxy.ts:117-121). The API token path (src/server/api/v1.ts:56-61) serves only the Vetrina operation registry (src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts; proposals/operations/undo routes). vetrina_operations/vetrina_proposals carry `via` (schema.ts:681, 755); apply_audit does not. So "two write families, one with proposal/log/undo, one without" is accurate.

The implication fails on two points. (1) "Safe by accident, not by design" is contradicted by the code's own design documents: docs/operations.md:1-7 and :59 state the rule "anything asked from outside the screens goes through a proposal the owner says yes to", docs/api.md:1-20 defines /api/v1 as exactly "the Vetrina's actions ... with the same preview, the same log and the same Annulla", and src/proxy.ts:19-21 documents /api/v1 as the programmatic surface. The operator maintenance tools (rebuild 'Destructive', full pull, repair, trash-duplicate, pricing/taxonomy reset) being absent from the token path is a deliberate boundary: the API exposes a registry of described, undoable actions, and these were never registered. (2) The severity "major" for the LLM-exposure question presumes the owner's flow requires sync-apply/publish/rebuild as LLM tools. The owner's stated examples (navigation menu, product categories) fall in the Vetrina action family, which already has the propose/confirm + ledger shape; keeping bulk/destructive operator tooling out of autonomous reach is a legitimate design decision, not debt.
