## Contradictions between recorded intent and a mixed WP+Woo middleware

1. hub-and-plugin.md:121 assigns navigation menus to the Plugin/WP menu editor; smart-collections.md:15,43 and README.md:247 then assume "the menus follow" a category automatically (true only if a menu item already points at that category). The user's example need (rebuild a menu from product categories) has no port, no action kind, no plugin route.
2. hub-and-plugin.md:129 (media usage index/cleanup → Plugin) vs README.md:197-212: the Hub already owns a photo queue (`src/server/woo/media.ts`, `media_jobs`) that attaches images to products — the Hub does write media, through WooCommerce's product `images`, but has no media-library read/write of its own.
3. operations.md:59 "anything from outside the screens should not write on its own word" vs api.md:17-19 shortcuts; and vs order-sources.md:224-226 "or the pipeline applies it too".
4. operations.md:33-38 "every action is a row of `vetrina_operations`" vs automatic categories (scheduler) logging to `collection_changes` and the price sync logging to `apply_audit`: three logs, one `via` vocabulary.
5. vetrina-customer-hub.md:1-6 and catalog-centric-redesign.md:3 still carry pre-implementation status lines; a fresh repo "using this codebase as the spec" would be reading superseded decisions (e.g. "No new database tables", "Update-only at first: no product creation over REST" at catalog-centric-redesign.md:206, while Publish creates products, README.md:189-235).

### Claims and verdicts

#### C1 [confirmed · strength] The registry really generates draft-2020-12 JSON Schema from the same Zod discriminated unions the server validates, and GET /api/v1/actions serves it for all 21 action kinds; every function the docs' MCP table names already exists with the documented signature.

- **Evidence**: src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:225-232 builds a kind→schema map from OperationInputSchema.options and PageActionInputSchema.options; :262 `z.toJSONSchema(schema, { io: "input", unrepresentable: "any" })`; package.json:39 zod 4.4.3; src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12-14; registry.test.ts:26-48 checks examples parse and schemas carry `kind` const and descriptions. MCP table (docs/operations.md:89-97) → reads.ts:36 readHomepage, :98 readProducts, catalog-order.ts:24 listTerms, service.ts:132 listOperations, :142 undoOperation, proposals.ts:105 propose(input, via), :122 confirmProposal.
- **Implication**: The tool-list half of an LLM-facing middleware is already a pure function over existing code; an MCP layer is additive, not a refactor. This weighs toward keep or extract rather than fresh: a fresh repo would be re-deriving exactly this.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Every cited location is literally true. src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:225-232 `schemasByKind()` iterates `[...OperationInputSchema.options, ...PageActionInputSchema.options]` and keys by `shape.kind.value`; :262 calls `z.toJSONSchema(schema, { io: "input", unrepresentable: "any" })`. package.json:39 pins `"zod": "4.4.3"`, and node_modules/zod/v4/core/to-json-schema.js:12 defaults `target` to "draft-2020-12", so the no-target call does emit draft 2020-12. OperationInputSchema (input.ts:115, discriminatedUnion) has 15 `kind: z.literal` entries and PageActionInputSchema (page-input.ts:35, discriminatedUnion) has 6, matching the 21 entries in ACTIONS (registry.ts:42-211) and `ACTION_KINDS = Object.keys(ACTIONS)`; describeActions() throws if any kind lacks a schema. src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12-14 serves `{ actions: describeActions() }` via `serve()`. registry.test.ts:17-48 asserts ACTION_KINDS equals catalog kinds + PAGE_ACTION_KINDS, every example parses with its kind, every schema has `properties.kind.const === kind`, and field descriptions are present; I ran it and all 5 tests pass. MCP table at docs/operations.md:89-97 names readHomepage (src/server/api/reads.ts:36 `export async function readHomepage()`), readProducts (reads.ts:98 `readProducts(query)`), listTerms (src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:24 `listTerms(taxonomy)`), listOperations (service.ts:132 `listOperations(limit = 20)`), undoOperation (service.ts:142 `undoOperation(id)`), propose (proposals.ts:105 `propose(input: unknown, via: OperationVia)`, and OPERATION_VIAS in src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247 includes "assistant"), confirmProposal (proposals.ts:122 `confirmProposal(id)`). All signatures match the documented ones. Only caveat (not a refutation): Zod refinements (wholeTerm, date >= shopToday, etc. at input.ts:59-133) are not expressible in JSON Schema, so the emitted schema is the structural shape only — which the registry's own doc comment at :241 already states ("the shape the server checks; the shop's own checks come after").
  - Corrected claim: The registry really generates draft-2020-12 JSON Schema from the same Zod discriminated unions the server validates, and GET /api/v1/actions serves it for all 21 action kinds; every function the docs' MCP table names already exists with the documented signature. (Caveat: Zod refinements such as the whole-term and not-in-the-past date checks are not representable in JSON Schema, so the emitted schema is the structural shape only; those checks still run on parse.)
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Facts verified: src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:225-232 `schemasByKind()` iterates `OperationInputSchema.options` and `PageActionInputSchema.options` (the same unions `parseAction` at :268-272 dispatches to via parseOperationInput/parsePageActionInput), and :262 emits `z.toJSONSchema(schema, { io: "input", unrepresentable: "any" })` (zod 4.4.3, package.json:39, whose default target is draft-2020-12). ACTIONS has 21 `example: { kind` entries; registry.test.ts:20-22 asserts ACTION_KINDS equals catalogKinds + PAGE_ACTION_KINDS and :34-42 asserts each schema has `properties.kind.const`. src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12-14 serves `describeActions()` behind `serve()` (src/server/api/v1.ts:57-63: bearer HUB_API_TOKEN, Accept-Language). Every function in the docs table (docs/operations.md:89-97) exists with the stated signature: reads.ts:36 `readHomepage()`, :98 `readProducts(query)`, catalog-order.ts:24 `listTerms(taxonomy)`, service.ts:132 `listOperations(limit = 20)`, :142 `undoOperation(id)`, proposals.ts:105 `propose(input: unknown, via: OperationVia)`, :122 `confirmProposal(id)`. `"assistant"` is already an OperationVia (src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247), so filing MCP-originated actions needs no type change.

Implication attack: I looked for reasons an MCP layer would require a refactor rather than addition. (a) describeActions is memoized and depends only on the schemas and static ACTIONS text; the only locale-dependent call in registry.ts is `words()` inside parseAction (:271), not in schema generation, so tool listing is effectively pure. (b) The schemas carry `.refine/.superRefine/.transform` (input.ts:36,59,69,85-87,116,132-133); with `unrepresentable: "any"` these constraints (percent 1-90, date >= shopToday, wholeTerm) are dropped from the JSON Schema, so the model sees a looser shape than the server enforces. This is a quality caveat, not a refactor: the server still validates and parseAction returns plain-language errors (test :51-57) an agent can relay. (c) What is missing (Streamable HTTP endpoint, OAuth, per-kind tool wiring) is exactly what docs/operations.md:103-106 already names as additive. A competent engineer would reach the same conclusion: the tool-list and tool-call primitives exist as callable server functions; a fresh repo would re-derive them. Severity "strength" is appropriate; the implication is a genuine consequence.
  - Corrected claim: The registry generates draft-2020-12 JSON Schema (zod 4.4.3 `z.toJSONSchema`, `unrepresentable: "any"`) from the same two Zod discriminated unions the server validates with, GET /api/v1/actions serves it for all 21 action kinds behind the bearer-token wrapper, and every function in the docs' MCP table exists with the documented signature (including `via: "assistant"` already being a legal OperationVia). The tool-list half of an LLM-facing middleware is therefore an existing pure function; an MCP layer is additive (endpoint, OAuth, per-kind tool wiring). One caveat for the LLM surface: `.refine`-level constraints (discount 1-90%, date not in the past, whole-term checks) are not emitted into the JSON Schema, so the model relies on the server's plain-language validation errors rather than the schema for those limits.

#### C2 [contested · blocker] The recorded decision places navigation menus, media hygiene and email on the WordPress/plugin side, and the code has no WordPress-core REST (wp/v2) client at all: the Hub talks only to wc/v3 product endpoints and seven wc-gh/v1 homepage routes.

- **Evidence**: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 'Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it', :129 media → Plugin, :130-131 email → Plugin. grep for wp/v2, nav_menu, menus in src/server returns only the base-URL builders client.ts:192 (/wp-json/wc/v3) and wp-source.ts:32 (/wp-json/wc-gh/v1). wp-source.ts:135-208 calls capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history. smart-collections.md:15,43 and README.md:247 assume 'the menus follow' a category.
- **Implication**: The owner's own example ('change the navigation menu with these product categories') is outside the recorded intent and outside the code. Whichever path is chosen, the design record must add a WordPress-core port (menus, pages, media) either in the Hub (wp/v2 with an application password, since Woo keys do not authenticate wp/v2) or as new wc-gh/v1 routes in the plugin, and the 'one writer' table needs a row for navigation/pages/media. This is new scope, not extraction.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited line. docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 reads "| Navigation menus | Plugin | WordPress's menu editor already does it |"; :129 "Media usage index, safe cleanup, whitelist | Plugin"; :130 "Email: brand, templates, campaigns | Plugin"; :131 "Transactional emails, order metabox | Plugin". So the recorded decision does place menus, media hygiene and email on the plugin side. grep over src for wp/v2, nav_menu, /menus, application password, wp-json: the only base-URL builders are src/server/woo/client.ts:192 (`${base}/wp-json/wc/v3`) and src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32 (`${origin}/wp-json/wc-gh/v1`); src/lib/site.ts:24 only strips /wp-json from a URL. No file constructs a wp/v2 URL, no nav_menu or menus endpoint exists, no application-password auth exists. Confirmed: there is no WordPress-core REST client. The wc-gh/v1 routes (file is src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts, not src/server/wp-source.ts as cited) are exactly seven distinct: capabilities (:135), homepage (:138), rail (:145), products (:149), homepage/block (:155,:177), homepage/layout (:198), homepage/history (:208). smart-collections.md:15 and :43 and README.md:247 do say 'the menus' follow the category, as cited. The only inaccuracy is the phrase "only wc/v3 product endpoints": client.ts also calls wc/v3 `orders` (client.ts:312) and `settings/products/woocommerce_default_catalog_orderby` (client.ts:346). These are still WooCommerce (wc/v3) endpoints, not WordPress-core, so they do not touch the claim's substance (no wp/v2 port exists); the correction is trivial and placed in corrected_claim. The implication (menu/pages/media access would be new scope requiring either a wp/v2 client with application-password auth or new wc-gh/v1 plugin routes) follows from the code as read.
  - Corrected claim: The recorded decision (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121, :129-131) places navigation menus, media hygiene and email on the WordPress/plugin side, and the code has no WordPress-core REST (wp/v2) client at all: the Hub talks only to WooCommerce wc/v3 endpoints (products, products/categories|tags|brands|attributes, products/batch, plus orders and one settings key, src/server/woo/client.ts) and seven wc-gh/v1 routes in src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts (capabilities, homepage, rail, products, homepage/block, homepage/layout, homepage/history). No code path can read or write WordPress menus, pages or the media library.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The factual part holds: there is no wp/v2 client (only src/server/woo/client.ts:192 builds /wp-json/wc/v3 and src/server/vetrina/wp-source.ts:32 builds /wp-json/wc-gh/v1), and docs/hub-and-plugin.md:121 places navigation menus on the Plugin side. The implication and severity do not follow.

1. This is a recorded decision, not drift. docs/hub-and-plugin.md:35-36 states explicitly that people keep editing menus in WordPress, and :121 records the reason ("WordPress's menu editor already does it"). smart-collections.md:15 and :43 and README.md:247 are not an unexamined assumption that menus are editable from the Hub; they describe the deliberate mechanism by which the Hub influences menus without owning them: category membership is written to the store as real terms (smart-collections.md:43-47, client.ts:565-572 products/batch), so WooCommerce taxonomy menu items that point to a product category show the right products automatically. The Hub also lists and creates categories via wc/v3 (client.ts:387-396 products/categories, plus brands/tags/attribute terms at :378-425), so the auditor's "only product endpoints" summary undersells what exists: the Hub already decides which categories exist and who is in them, which is the substance of "the navigation reflects these product categories".

2. Even if "add/remove menu items" is wanted, the design record already contains the rule that places it: hub-and-plugin.md:25-33 says storefront presentation is rendered by the plugin and edited by the Hub through the plugin's wc-gh/v1 REST endpoints, "exactly how the Vetrina works today" (wp-source.ts:135-208 homepage/layout, homepage/block, rail). A menu endpoint would be one more wc-gh/v1 route under an existing rule, with the existing auth story (wp-source.ts:36-38 already authenticates wc-gh/v1 with the Woo consumer key, so the "Woo keys do not authenticate wp/v2" concern does not arise on the plugin path). The "one writer" table needs at most a one-line row, and the auditor's own text concedes two viable paths ("whichever path is chosen"). That is a small feature addition that fits the recorded architecture, not a redesign.

3. Severity is wrong for the question asked. Exposing the Hub as an LLM middleware (tool calls / MCP) is about whether the capabilities it has are safe and well-shaped to expose. A capability it does not have (menu-item CRUD) is a scope gap, not a readiness blocker; it does not impair exposing catalog, pricing, collections or Vetrina writes as tools. The "owner's example" the auditor cites does not appear anywhere in the repo docs (grep for navigation/menu across docs, README, CLAUDE.md returns only the lines above), so it cannot be a recorded design intent that has drifted. Correct severity: low/medium feature gap, or at most "document the menu-item path if the owner wants it as a tool".
  - Corrected claim: The design record deliberately places navigation menus, media hygiene and email on the WordPress/plugin side (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:35-36, :121, :129-131), and the Hub has no wp/v2 client: it talks to wc/v3 (products, products/batch, variations, and the term endpoints products/categories, products/brands, products/tags, products/attributes) and to seven wc-gh/v1 homepage routes. The Hub shapes navigation indirectly and by design: it creates categories and writes their membership as real terms, so WooCommerce category menu items follow (smart-collections.md:43-47). Direct menu-item editing is not implemented; if wanted as an LLM tool, the recorded placement rule (plugin renders, Hub edits via a wc-gh/v1 route, as the Vetrina does) already covers how to add it with the existing consumer-key auth. This is a feature gap of low/medium severity, not a blocker to exposing the current capabilities as middleware.

#### C3 [contested · major] `via: "assistant"` and `via: "scheduler"` are declared, labelled and defaulted in the schema but never passed by any code path; only "app" and "api" are ever written, and the scheduler's writes land in other tables.

- **Evidence**: src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247 OPERATION_VIAS = [app, api, assistant, scheduler]; schema.ts:681 text column default 'app'; labels act.it.ts:277, act.en.ts:252. Call sites: service.ts:79 default "app"; proposals/route.ts:22, catalog-order.ts:113, src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 pass "api". grep for "assistant") and "scheduler") as arguments: 0 hits. Automatic categories log to collection_changes (smart-collections.md:45,226), price syncs to apply_audit (README.md:148).
- **Implication**: Wiring an MCP caller to `propose(action, "assistant")` is a one-argument change, so the intent is cheap to honour. But the docs' claim that 'whatever it does is in Attività, undoable' (operations.md:102) is only true for the 21 Vetrina kinds; an LLM driving the Hub would not see or undo scheduler-made category moves or price syncs through the same log. Any extraction should decide whether one operations log is a core invariant or three logs are accepted and documented.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked every cited line and grepped for counterexamples.
- Declaration: src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247 `OPERATION_VIAS = ["app","api","assistant","scheduler"]`. Schema: src/server/db/schema.ts:681 `via: text("via").$type<OperationVia>().notNull().default("app")` (operations table) and :755 (proposals table, no default). Labels: src/i18n/dictionaries/catalog/act.it.ts:275-279 has api/assistant/scheduler. ActivitySheet.tsx:130 renders the label when via !== "app".
- Defaults: src/server/vetrina/operations/service.ts:79 `startOperation(input, via = "app")`, :114 `startPageOperation(..., via = "app")`, page.ts:468 `via = "app"`.
- Production call sites that pass a literal via: src/app/api/v1/proposals/route.ts:22 `propose(..., "api")`; src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 and src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 `startOperation(parsed.data, "api")`. Server actions (src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:78, vetrina.ts:97-258) pass no via, so they default to "app". proposals.ts:159/167-168 only propagate `row.via`. src/server/scheduler.ts has no call to startOperation/startPageOperation/propose.
- grep for "assistant"/"scheduler" as an argument across src (excluding dictionaries): the only hits are in src/server/vetrina/operations/proposals.db.test.ts:69,100,123,134,144,147 (`propose(..., "assistant")`). So the literal "never passed by any code path" is false only for a DB test; no production path passes "assistant" or "scheduler". That is a trivial qualifier, not a refutation.
- Scheduler writes land elsewhere: src/server/collections/repo.ts:337 inserts into `collectionChanges` (schema.ts:543, docs/smart-collections.md:45,226); src/server/woo/apply.ts:545/829 writes `applyAudit` (schema.ts:97, README.md:148 "Every run lands in apply_audit"). Neither touches the operations table.
- docs/operations.md:100-102 does say "whatever it does is in Attività, undoable from the phone" in the MCP section, and the MCP table there prescribes `propose(action, "assistant")`, confirming the intent is recorded but unwired.
The claim stands; the only correction is to say "no production code path (only a DB test passes 'assistant')". I did not independently recount the "21 Vetrina kinds" figure from the auditor's implication, as it is not part of the claim text.
  - Corrected claim: `via: "assistant"` and `via: "scheduler"` are declared (catalog-ops.ts:247), labelled (act.it.ts:277, act.en.ts:252) and defaulted in the schema (schema.ts:681, default 'app'), but no production code path ever passes them; only "app" (via the defaults in service.ts:79/114 and page.ts:468) and "api" (proposals/route.ts:22, catalog-order.ts:113, order-sources.ts:66) are ever written. The sole caller passing "assistant" is a DB test (proposals.db.test.ts:69 etc.). The scheduler never files an operation: automatic category moves are logged to collection_changes (collections/repo.ts:337) and price syncs to apply_audit (woo/apply.ts:545), outside the Attività log.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Factual part confirmed: OPERATION_VIAS at src/lib/vetrina/catalog-ops.ts:247 lists assistant/scheduler; no code passes either (grep over src: only "app" defaults at src/server/vetrina/operations/service.ts:79,114 and page.ts:468, and "api" at the API routes). Scheduler work (src/server/scheduler.ts:411 automatic categories, :339 store sync) lands in collection_changes, apply_audit and scheduler_runs, never in vetrina_operations.

The implication and severity do not follow:
1. Misread of the docs sentence. docs/operations.md:95-102 is the "For an assistant" section; "whatever it does is in Attività, undoable" has the MCP layer as its subject — the tools listed are propose/confirm/undo over the registry kinds and listOperations. The docs do not claim that all Hub writes are in Attività; they say exactly what the auditor concedes is true (assistant actions over the Vetrina kinds). The docs already treat the other histories as separate and deliberate: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:86 (apply_audit, ingestion_runs, scheduler_runs as run audit), docs/vetrina-customer-hub.md:738,764 ("Price runs stay in apply_audit"), docs/smart-collections.md:45 (collection_changes). "Three logs accepted and documented" is already the recorded state, not an open decision.
2. Scheduler writes have a different reversal model, so a per-row undo in the same log would be the wrong primitive. Automatic categories re-apply a standing rule every few minutes (src/server/collections/runner.ts:22,469; docs/smart-collections.md:35-52); "undoing" one move would be re-done at the next check, and the designed way back is editing the rule, with risky changes held for a person's "Conferma e applica" (docs/smart-collections.md:100-104). Price syncs are feed-driven re-applications (src/server/sync/price-sync.ts:10). An LLM would not undo these through Attività because nobody does; it would read live state (readProducts/readHomepage, docs/operations.md:88-91) and the collections/feeds state (src/server/collections/service.ts:116-135).
3. The interplay the auditor worries about is already handled: the Vetrina undo keeps values changed since, explicitly including an automatic category's writes (src/server/vetrina/operations/plan.ts:309 "a tag or category someone (or an automatic category) added since stays"; docs/operations.md:41-46 "a value changed since stays, and is counted as kept").
4. What remains is real but minor: docs/operations.md:37 promises `scheduler` "beside the time" in Attività, and schema.ts:681 plus the act.it/act.en labels carry a value nothing writes — a dead enum value / doc-schema drift, and `assistant` is simply reserved for the not-yet-built MCP step (operations.md:84-86), i.e. recorded intent not yet reached, not drift. None of this blocks or complicates exposing the Hub as an LLM middleware; `propose(action, "assistant")` honours the intent as the auditor notes. A competent engineer would file this as a doc fix (drop or qualify the `scheduler` via) rather than a major architectural decision.
  - Corrected claim: `via: "assistant"` and `via: "scheduler"` are declared in OPERATION_VIAS (catalog-ops.ts:247), the schema default (schema.ts:681) and the labels, but nothing writes them: only "app" and "api" are passed. "assistant" is reserved for the planned MCP layer (operations.md:84-102) and is honoured by a one-argument change. "scheduler" is a dead value: docs/operations.md:37 says the Attività sheet shows it, but scheduler-driven writes (automatic categories, price/store syncs) deliberately live in collection_changes, apply_audit and scheduler_runs, have their own reversal model (edit the rule, re-run the feed) and are already treated by the Vetrina undo as "changed since, kept" (plan.ts:309). Severity: minor — fix the doc line or remove the unused via; the operations log being scoped to owner-confirmed catalog/homepage actions is the documented design, not an undecided invariant.

#### C4 [refuted · major] 'Proposals as the only outside write path' is contradicted by two documented one-call shortcuts that start operations directly with via "api", and the API's read surface (5 reads) is far narrower than what the UI can see (107 exported server actions).

- **Evidence**: docs/operations.md:59-60 vs docs/api.md:17-19 ('Shortcuts that act in one call (they predate proposals)'); src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 and src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 `operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api")` with no proposal; order-sources.md:224-226 'or the pipeline applies it too'. Reads: api.md:8-10 lists homepage, catalog/products, catalog/terms, catalog/order, operations only; no tags, attributes, pricing rules, automatic-category rules, feeds, orders, media queue. src/server/actions: 26 files, 24 'use server', 107 exported functions; 14 route files under src/app/api/v1.
- **Implication**: For an LLM-driven flow the recorded invariant (nothing outside the screens writes on its own word) must either be enforced (remove or proposal-gate the shortcuts) or explicitly relaxed. More work is in reads: most of what the UI knows (pricing rules, collections rules, feeds, orders, publish delta) is reachable only through Next server actions, which an MCP in the same process can call but which are UI-shaped (VetrinaResult wrappers). Extracting a core package would mean lifting these 107 actions' bodies into a transport-neutral service layer first.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): What holds: docs/operations.md:59 records "Anything asked from outside the screens should not write on its own word"; docs/api.md:17-19 documents two one-call shortcuts; src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 and src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66 call `operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api")` with no proposal; docs/order-sources.md:224-226 says "or the pipeline applies it too"; src/server/actions has 26 entries (24 non-test files, all 'use server') with exactly 107 exported functions; 14 route.ts files under src/app/api/v1; no routes exist for tags, attributes, pricing rules, collection rules, feeds, orders or media.

What is overstated or wrong: (1) The non-proposal write surface is wider than "two shortcuts": `POST /order-sources` (create), `DELETE /order-sources/{id}` and `POST /operations/{id}/undo` (src/app/api/v1/order-sources/route.ts, order-sources/[id]/route.ts, operations/[id]/undo/route.ts; docs/api.md:20,176-177,249) also write in one call outside proposals, so the claim undercounts the very drift it is reporting. (2) "5 reads" is an undercount of the code: there are 10 GET handlers (actions, catalog/order, catalog/products, catalog/terms, homepage, operations, operations/[id], order-sources, order-sources/[id], proposals/[id]); the "5" is the api.md:8-10 summary bullet, not the API. (3) Comparing "5 reads" with "107 exported server actions" conflates reads with writes/mutations. (4) "UI-shaped (VetrinaResult wrappers)" applies to only 4 of 24 action files (grep VetrinaResult: src/server/actions/vetrina-catalog.ts, vetrina-order-sources.ts, vetrina-operations.ts, +1). (5) "Extracting a core package would mean lifting these 107 actions' bodies into a transport-neutral service layer first" is contradicted by the code: a service layer already exists and the actions are thin wrappers over it — src/server/actions/vetrina-operations.ts:25-31 says so explicitly ("thin, validated wrappers over server/vetrina/operations"); collections.ts imports `* as service from "@/server/collections/service"`; feeds/orders/overrides/publish import @/server/catalog, @/server/orders/pull, @/server/overrides/repo, @/server/sync/ledger. The remaining work is exposing those existing modules, not extracting bodies from 107 actions. The claim is directionally right (drift exists; read surface narrow) but the counts and the implication are materially off, so it is refuted as stated.
  - Corrected claim: The recorded invariant "anything asked from outside the screens should not write on its own word" (docs/operations.md:59) is contradicted by five documented one-call API writes that bypass proposals: POST /catalog/order and POST /order-sources/{id}/apply (both call `operations.startOperation(parsed.data, "api")` at src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 and src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66; docs/api.md:17-19 label them shortcuts that predate proposals), plus POST /order-sources, DELETE /order-sources/{id} and POST /operations/{id}/undo. The API's read surface (10 GET handlers across 14 route files under src/app/api/v1: homepage, products, terms, term order, actions, operations list/one, order sources list/one, proposal) is far narrower than the UI's: 107 exported server actions in 24 'use server' files under src/server/actions cover tags, attributes, pricing rules, collection rules, feeds, orders, publish and media, none of which has an API route. For an LLM-driven flow the invariant must be enforced (proposal-gate or remove the one-call writes) or explicitly relaxed; the larger gap is reads. However, the server actions are already thin wrappers over an existing transport-neutral service layer (src/server/{vetrina/operations,collections/service,catalog,orders,overrides,pricing,feeds}; only 4 of 24 action files use the VetrinaResult wrapper), so exposing that layer via MCP/API means adding routes or tool adapters over existing modules, not lifting 107 action bodies first.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, high): The facts hold (two one-call shortcuts at src/server/api/catalog-order.ts:113 and src/server/api/order-sources.ts:66; api.md:8-10 lists five reads; 107 exported server actions), but the implication and severity do not survive the code.

1. The invariant is already "explicitly relaxed", and the shortcuts are not ungoverned. docs/api.md:17-19 records the two shortcuts as a known exception ("they predate proposals"), and docs/operations.md:3-4 names "a script over the API" as a legitimate actor. Both shortcuts still pass through startOperation (src/server/vetrina/operations/service.ts:79-109), which runs the same assess()/refusal path as the screens, files the row with via: "api" (operations.md:36), and is undoable for 7 days (operations.md:41; test demo.db.test.ts:260-266 undoes a via "api" operation). Both offer dryRun previews (catalog-order.ts:112, order-sources.ts:65) and require HUB_API_TOKEN on the Hub address only (api.md:24-36). So the "enforce or relax" dichotomy the auditor demands is already satisfied on the "relax" side, in writing.

2. The shortcuts are irrelevant to the LLM/MCP path as the codebase designs it. docs/operations.md:85-105 ("For an assistant") specifies the MCP layer as in-process over propose(action, "assistant") / confirmProposal / undoOperation, not over the REST shortcuts. The kinds the shortcuts start (order, sourceOrder) are ordinary registry kinds (registry.ts:130-139) and propose() accepts any parsed action (proposals.ts:105-110), so a proposal-gated route for exactly these operations already exists (/api/v1/proposals, src/app/api/v1/proposals/). Nothing has to be removed or rewritten for an LLM flow to be proposal-gated; the LLM simply is not given the shortcut.

3. The read-surface comparison mixes scopes. api.md and operations.md describe the Vetrina (homepage/catalog/order/log). The "missing" reads (pricing rules, feeds, orders, publish delta, debug, config) belong to the operator Hub, and operations.md:89-96 deliberately scopes the assistant's reads to homepage/products/terms/log. Whether an LLM should see feeds or orders is a product-scoping decision, not evidence of unreadiness.

4. "Lifting 107 actions' bodies" is overstated. Many action files are already declared and implemented as thin wrappers over service modules: collections.ts:15-17 ("thin, validated wrappers over server/collections"), orders.ts over server/orders/repo, feeds.ts over server/feeds/repo, vetrina-* over server/vetrina. The VetrinaResult/CollectionsResult shape ({ok,data} | {ok:false,code,error}, service.ts:29-31) is transport-neutral and maps directly to a tool result; it is not "UI-shaped". A handful of fat actions remain (preview.ts 898 lines, config.ts 394, sync.ts 332, debug.ts 311) and would need extraction if ever exposed, but that is a bounded refactor of ~4 files, not 107.

Severity: the documented shortcut exception plus the recorded, proposal-based assistant design mean a competent engineer would not treat this as a major blocker; at most it is a minor note to state that the MCP tools exclude the REST shortcuts and to pick which Hub reads (if any) to expose.
  - Corrected claim: Two REST shortcuts (POST /catalog/order, POST /order-sources/{id}/apply) act in one call without a proposal; this is a recorded, documented exception (api.md:17-19), still assessed, logged with via "api", previewable with dryRun and undoable. The recorded MCP/assistant design (operations.md:85-105) routes LLM actions through propose()/confirm in-process, where the same order kinds are already proposal-gated, so the shortcuts do not undermine LLM exposure. The API's five reads cover the Vetrina scope the assistant plan targets; wider Hub data (pricing rules, feeds, orders, publish) is out of that recorded scope and would be a product decision to add. Most server actions are already thin wrappers over service modules with a transport-neutral {ok,data|code,error} result; only a few large action files (preview, config, sync, debug) would need extraction. Severity: minor.

#### C5 [contested · major] Of the six items in hub-and-plugin.md §6 'What comes next in the Hub', only bulk actions is done; generic feeds, oversell guard, inbound webhooks, rules-as-code and KicksDB discover/price webhooks have no code, and 'config as code per shop' remains one committed hub.config.ts per deployment.

- **Evidence**: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:161-179. Bulk actions: vetrina-catalog.md:465 phase 3 Done, registry.ts 13 catalog kinds. Feeds: src/server/feeds contains only goldensneakers-model.ts, goldensneakers.ts, owner.ts, ownership.ts, repo.ts. Webhooks: src/app/api has cron/, debug/, dock/, health/, publish/, v1/ only; smart-collections.md:214-216 'left for later'. grep oversell|orderedFromSupplier|exportConfig|importConfig|discover → 0 hits. hub.config.ts is 54 lines of UI/edit flags validated in src/config/index.ts:62; deploy.md:309 one container per shop; hub-and-plugin.md:90 project.json 'Hub →'.
- **Implication**: Development effort since the decision record went into the Vetrina/API layer, not the product-sync roadmap. For exposure as middleware this means the write-side vocabulary is rich for storefront/catalog curation but the ingestion/pricing side is still single-supplier and not scriptable (no rules import/export, no feed registry API). Inbound webhooks are the one roadmap item an event-driven middleware would need first.
- **Skeptic** (stands, high): Checked docs/hub-and-plugin.md:161-179 — the six §6 items are exactly as quoted (generic feeds, bulk actions, oversell guard, webhooks in, rules as code, KicksDB Discover/price webhooks). Bulk actions: docs/vetrina-catalog.md:465 phase "3 · Act — Selection and bulk actions … Done", and src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts exists. Generic feeds: src/server/feeds contains only goldensneakers-model.ts, goldensneakers.ts, owner.ts, ownership.ts, repo.ts (+ tests); src/server/feeds/owner.ts:11-14 still hardcodes ownership as "manual pin > goldensneakers > kicksdb" by name; src/server/db/schema.ts:297-300 feed_items has a generic `feed` text column but the only producer is "goldensneakers"; no StockFirmati or column-mapping code anywhere. Webhooks: src/app/api has only cron/, debug/, dock/, health/, publish/, v1/; `grep -rni webhook src` returns zero hits (only docs/hub-and-plugin.md and docs/smart-collections.md:214-216 "left for later" mention it). Oversell guard / rules as code / discover: grep for oversell|orderedFromSupplier|exportConfig|importConfig|discover over src yields only src/lib/catalog.ts:1 ("discoverable KicksDB catalog" doc comment — a false positive, not a Discover feature); src/server/orders has no supplier-ordered step; src/server/config/rule-list.ts has no export/import, no Content-Disposition/attachment download anywhere in src. Config as code: the file is src/config/hub.config.ts (54 lines, UI/edit flags only), imported at src/config/index.ts:1 — the evidence's path "hub.config.ts" at repo root and "validated in src/config/index.ts:62" are slightly imprecise but the substance (one committed per-deployment config, no per-shop export/import) is correct; docs/deploy.md:309 "single app container per shop"; docs/hub-and-plugin.md:90 project.json config as code marked "Hub →" (not done). Git log shows recent work (PRs #93-#95, Elimina, source colours) is Vetrina/catalog work, consistent with the implication. No counterexample found; the only correction is the config file path.
  - Corrected claim: Of the six items in hub-and-plugin.md §6 'What comes next in the Hub', only bulk actions is done; generic feeds, oversell guard, inbound webhooks, rules-as-code and KicksDB discover/price webhooks have no code (the feeds layer is still GoldenSneakers-only by name in src/server/feeds/owner.ts), and 'config as code per shop' remains one committed src/config/hub.config.ts (54 lines of UI/edit flags) per deployment, with no export/import of margins, taxonomy rules or automatic categories.
- **Skeptic** (refuted, medium): Facts checked and largely confirmed: docs/hub-and-plugin.md:161-179 lists the six items; src/server/feeds has only GoldenSneakers modules; src/app/api has no webhook route; grep for oversell/orderedFromSupplier/exportConfig/importConfig returns no code hits. One factual slip: "config as code per shop" is not one of the six §6 items, and the pricing/taxonomy rules do NOT live in hub.config.ts — they live in the DB `config` jsonb row (src/server/config/repo.ts:18-28 getActiveConfig, :118-132 updateActiveConfig under an advisory lock), so hub.config.ts being 54 lines of UI flags says nothing about pricing scriptability.

The implication falls on three points:
1. "Pricing side is not scriptable" is wrong. /api/v1/actions (src/app/api/v1/actions/route.ts:12 describeActions) exposes a `margin` action kind (src/server/vetrina/operations/registry.ts:114-122) that writes a scoped pricing rule into the Hub config and reprices, with 7-day undo, JSON Schema input, proposals/confirm and a log (docs/api.md:1-20). Pricing rules carry scopes source/brand/category (src/server/actions/config.ts:4-5, 29-33). What is missing is cross-shop export/import of rules, which is a multi-tenant portability concern, not a prerequisite for exposing one shop to an LLM via tool calls.
2. "Inbound webhooks are the one roadmap item an event-driven middleware would need first" conflates two exposure modes. The question is tool calls / MCP, which is request-response. Staleness on the write path is already handled without webhooks: margin reprices run through runPriceSync with liveCheck: true (src/server/vetrina/operations/margin.ts:59, :248), src/server/vetrina/operations/live.ts:12-34 re-reads the touched products from Woo and refreshes the index, and proposals confirm "only if the shop still gives that preview" with a 409 `stale` code (docs/api.md:14-16, 46-48). The decision record itself keeps polls as the safety net (docs/hub-and-plugin.md:158) and cron/pull-store, refresh-catalog, sync-goldensneakers are bearer-token HTTP routes (src/app/api/cron/pull-store/route.ts:9-20), i.e. scriptable by an orchestrator.
3. "Effort went to Vetrina/API, not the roadmap" is true from git (commits since ec20a82 2026-10-06 concentrate in src/server/vetrina/operations, src/server/api, components/vetrina), but that effort IS the LLM-exposure layer (describeActions with schemas, proposals, undo), and the decision record was kept current (5d8dbb2 updated the §4 table: Sorter marked Hub ✓ with api.md link). An unexecuted forward roadmap is not design drift; the only genuine drift is that §6 says "in order of value" with feeds first and the team did #2 before #1.

What survives: the supplier/ingestion side (GoldenSneakers sync, KicksDB import via src/server/actions/import.ts, feeds.ts runGsSyncFromApi) is server-action and cron-only, not under /api/v1, and is single-supplier. That is a scope gap for an LLM wanting to drive ingestion, not a readiness blocker for catalog/pricing/homepage exposure. Severity should be minor/informational, not major.
  - Corrected claim: Of the six items in hub-and-plugin.md §6, only bulk actions is done; generic feeds, oversell guard, inbound webhooks, rules export/import and KicksDB discover/price webhooks have no code (config-as-code per shop is not a §6 item; pricing/taxonomy rules live in the DB config row, not hub.config.ts). For LLM/MCP exposure this is a scope gap, not a readiness defect: the /api/v1 layer built since the record already covers catalog curation, homepage and per-term margins (the `margin` action writes the Hub's scoped pricing rule, with proposals, live re-read and undo), and write-path staleness is handled by live checks and 409 `stale` confirmations plus cron polls, so inbound webhooks are not a prerequisite for request-response tool calls. What remains unexposed under /api/v1 is the supplier/ingestion side (GoldenSneakers sync, KicksDB import), which stays single-supplier and reachable only via server actions and bearer-token cron routes. Severity: minor.

(no new tables)" contradict src/server/db/schema.ts, which has 24 pgTable definitions (grep count 24) including storeIndex (:503, which §8.3 itself cites as `store_index` while saying "no new tables"), vetrinaOperations (:667, `vetrina_operations`), vetrinaOperationItems (:715), vetrinaProposals (:748), vetrinaOrderSources (:785). docs/catalog-centric-redesign.md:3 "agreed direction, not yet implemented" and :206 "Update-only at first: no product/variation creation over REST (`upsertProduct` stays dormant)" vs README.md:189-235 documenting Publish (`/publish`) that "creates the selected products on WooCommerce: parent, canonical EU pa_taglia sizes..." implemented in src/server/woo/publish.ts; `upsertProduct` no longer appears in client.ts/publish.ts at all. apply_audit (schema.ts:97) and mediaJobs exist, so "not yet implemented" is false. The auditor concedes these are current (2026-10-30), which contradicts the conclusion "keeping the codebase makes the code the spec by default" — the current contract docs plus `/actions` are the spec, and stale design-history docs do not change that. (4) The only surviving consequence ("a fresh repo built from these docs would re-litigate") is a hypothetical rewrite path, not a readiness defect of exposing this codebase as middleware; for the stated question the finding is informational housekeeping (add a "superseded by implementation" banner), not a readiness blocker even at "minor".
